攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: the impact is not self-only, the preconditions are realistic, and the required teacher privilege is precisely the lower-privileged identity whose authorization boundary is bypassed. The protected write path does not mitigate the issue because default teachers possess exam:write and the defective object check admits every quiz exam. Network exposure is remote in the documented reverse-proxy deployment, supporting high likelihood. Impact is high because the reachable sinks alter or delete authoritative cross-boundary academic data, including exam-wide rescoring. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact plus high likelihood yields high rather than critical because the path requires an authenticated teacher, remains within one installation and quiz-exam scope, and does not establish administrator takeover, unauthenticated compromise, remote code execution, or complete installation compromise.

路径
Authenticated scoped teacher --uses normal authenticated web API--> GET /api/exams enumerates unrelated quiz exams --discloses attacker-selectable quiz exam IDs--> Global quiz IDs enter getVisibleExamIds --visibility membership is reused as write authorization--> requireExamAccess authorizes every HTTP method --passes destructive route middleware--> Score, answer-key, mode, or delete endpoint --persists unauthorized mutations or deletion--> Cross-class and cross-subject academic data corruption or deletion

Attacker story: a malicious or compromised Mathematics subject-teacher account logs in through the normal web API. GET /api/exams returns History quiz exams owned by another teacher because withQuizExamIds unconditionally adds every quiz ID. The teacher then supplies one of those IDs to PUT /api/exams/:examId/student/:studentId/scores, PUT /api/exams/:examId/answers, PATCH /api/exams/:examId, or DELETE /api/exams/:examId. The exam capability gate accepts the request because the shipped teacher role has exam:write, and requireExamAccess accepts it because it does not distinguish visibility from mutation authority. The supplied executable validation used the real Express application with authentication enabled and proved cross-subject score modification, mode modification, and deletion of an unrelated quiz exam and dependent rows; an equivalent formal exam returned 403. No application secret is exposed or required beyond the attacker's legitimate session, while sensitive student scores, rankings, answer keys, scan-batch associations, and exam records are affected. The strongest repository counterevidence is that comments, schema text, and the introducing change describe quiz exams as globally visible or having full permissions, suggesting some broad access was intentional. That is not dispositive under the supplied threat model because the same helper is reused for destructive authorization, formal teacher scoping still exists, and the mode-switch UI is administrator-only while the server lacks that check. Additional counterevidence is that Node binds only to loopback and deployments can customize teacher permissions; however, the repository explicitly documents public HTTPS reverse proxy deployment, and the shipped default teacher role grants the required write capability. The issue remains bounded to a single installation and primarily to quiz exams, but it crosses real teacher, class, subject, and grading boundaries.

发生可能性
High - The path uses normal authenticated HTTP requests, globally enumerable quiz IDs, and the shipped default teacher permissions. It requires only a plausible malicious or compromised teacher account and a quiz exam; no administrator role, race, local shell access, secret extraction, matching class, or matching subject is required. The repository documents remote HTTPS deployment through Nginx, and the executable validation confirmed the path through production Express routes. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - A teacher crosses exam, class, subject, ownership, and grading boundaries to alter authoritative student scores and rankings, replace an answer key and rescore every participant, or delete an unrelated exam and its score and scan-batch rows. These are major integrity and availability impacts on educational records, although they are bounded to one Project-X installation and the affected quiz exam set.
假设条件
The deployment uses the repository-supported web workflow and is reachable by teachers directly or through the documented HTTPS Nginx reverse proxy.
The attacker controls a valid active subject-teacher or head-teacher account but has no administrator role, victim-exam ownership, matching class, or matching subject.
At least one victim exam is marked exam_mode='quiz' and the teacher role retains the shipped default exam:write permission.
Authentication enforcement has not been explicitly disabled with PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE=0 or false.
A valid authenticated teacher account
A victim exam marked exam_mode='quiz'
The teacher role's default exam:write permission
Knowledge of an exam or student ID, with quiz exam IDs available from GET /api/exams
控制措施
Default-on authentication accepts an opaque bearer token or HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookie.
Login requests are limited to 10 attempts per IP per 15 minutes.
The /api/exams capability gate requires exam:write for PUT, PATCH, POST, and DELETE.
requireExamAccess blocks unrelated formal exams for scoped teachers, but incorrectly admits all quiz exams.
Score and answer request bodies receive structural validation.
Score and answer-key changes use transactions, record modified_by or answer_overrides, and recompute rankings.
The client hides exam-mode switching from non-administrators, but this is not a server-side authorization control.
Operators can customize role permissions to remove exam:write, but the shipped default teacher role grants it.
盲点
Actual installations may remain loopback-only or on a private school network rather than using the documented public HTTPS deployment.
Operators may customize teacher roles to remove exam:write, reducing exploitability for those deployments.
Repository comments explicitly call quiz mode globally visible or full-permission, leaving some policy-intent ambiguity, although the supplied threat model and administrator-only mode UI contradict treating all destructive operations as universally authorized.
The executable validation used SQLite; MariaDB was not dynamically exercised, although the authorization decision and affected route composition are database-independent.
The validation directly demonstrated score mutation, mode mutation, and deletion; answer-key-driven exam-wide rescoring is established from reachable source code rather than separately executed in the supplied PoC.

## 7. Unscoped claims let teachers monopolize and grade any review block

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/4e2b2b0bf3bc81919798177834a2ad92?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Unscoped claims let teachers monopolize and grade any review block
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
7d7ff51
10:41 PM Aug 4, 2026

操作者：Tim-Hanchuan-Yang

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduces persistent paper claims and makes claim ownership the authorization condition for score submission, but the newly introduced claim operations do not enforce existing block or student assignments. The result is a new durable lock-monopolization path and an authorization path to grade papers outside the teacher's assigned scope.

The new review-pool claim endpoints apply only `requireExamAccess` and the generic `GRADE_WRITE` permission. They do not verify that the caller has a `review_assignments` row for the requested block, that the selected student is among the teacher's `assigned_student_ids`, or that the number of outstanding claims is within the assigned workload. `claimNextPaper` consequently selects from every unclaimed crop in the caller-supplied exam and block and assigns ownership directly to the caller. A teacher who can access the exam can repeatedly call the endpoint until the pool is empty, including for a block assigned to other teachers. Claims have no lease expiration or stale-claim reclamation; normally only the claimant can release them and only an administrator can force release them. The changed submission logic then rejects the legitimate assigned reviewer because `claimed_by` belongs to the attacker, while accepting submissions from the attacker. The attacker can therefore leave all papers locked during a grading window or submit attacker-selected, range-valid scores; in single-review mode those can become final scores, while multi-review mode can be manipulated or forced into disputes. The atomic update prevents two teachers from claiming the same paper but does not provide object-level authorization. Enforce block and student assignment scope inside the claim transaction, limit outstanding claims to authorized workload, and add expiration/reclamation for abandoned claims.

验证
 Verify a non-admin teacher with exam access and GRADE_WRITE can reach claim endpoints without review-assignment, block, or student-scope enforcement.
 Verify the claim service persists claims on crops outside the teacher's review_assignments and assigned_student_ids, without an assigned-workload quota.
 Verify claims can be repeated to exhaust the pool, are not expired based on claimed_at, and cannot be released by the legitimate assigned teacher.
 Verify claim ownership rejects the legitimate reviewer while allowing the unassigned claimant to submit a range-valid score and become the recorded reviewer.
 Reproduce the chain through authenticated production HTTP routes and confirm the resulting database state.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The claim-next route checks only broad exam visibility and GRADE_WRITE; it passes the caller and arbitrary block/class parameters to the service without validating a review assignment or claim quota.
- The specific-paper claim route likewise lets any teacher with generic grade-write permission claim an arbitrary crop in an accessible exam and block.
- A claim held by another teacher can only be force-released through the administrator-only path, and the implementation provides no expiration mechanism for abandoned or malicious claims.
- The service selects from every unclaimed crop for the supplied exam/block and sets claimed_by to the caller. It does not constrain candidates by review_assignments, assigned_student_ids, or an outstanding-claim limit.
- Submission authority is now derived from claimed_by, so an unauthorized claimant may submit while the legitimately assigned teacher is rejected.

// POST /api/review-pool/exams/:examId/blocks/:blockId/crops/:cropId/release — 释放回池（本人/强制）
router.post(
  "/exams/:examId/blocks/:blockId/crops/:cropId/release",
  requireExamAccess,
  requirePermission(PERMISSIONS.GRADE_WRITE),
  async (req, res) => {
    try {
      const examId = Number(req.params.examId);
      const blockId = String(req.params.blockId ?? "");
      const cropId = String(req.params.cropId ?? "");
      const teacherId = (req as any).user?.id;
      if (!teacherId) return res.status(401).json({ ok: false, error: "未登录" });
      const force = req.body?.force === true;
      if (force && (req as any).user?.role_id !== 1) {
        return res.status(403).json({ ok: false, error: "仅管理员可强制释放他人领取的试卷" });
      }
      await releasePaper(examId, blockId, cropId, teacherId, undefined, { force });

### [src/server/services/ReviewPoolService.ts:152](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/7d7ff51d1951570ca37836dc0c80311f2039a88a/src/server/services/ReviewPoolService.ts#L152)

~~~~typescript
export async function claimNextPaper(
  examId: number,
  blockId: string,
  teacherId: number,
  db: DbAdapter = getMysqlDb(),
  options: { classId?: number } = {}
): Promise<ReviewPoolEntry> {
  const cropId = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
    const classFilter = options.classId
      ? `AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM class_students cs WHERE cs.student_id = answer_block_crops.student_id AND cs.class_id = ?)`
      : "";
    const classParams = options.classId ? [options.classId] : [];
    const candidates = await tx.all(
      `SELECT id, score_breakdown FROM answer_block_crops
       WHERE exam_id = ? AND block_id = ? AND ${statusClause()} AND claimed_by IS NULL ${classFilter}
       ORDER BY student_number, page_number, segment_index`,
      examId,
      blockId,
      ...classParams
    ) as Array<{ id: string; score_breakdown: string | null }>;
    const candidate = candidates.find((item) => {
      try {
        const history = item.score_breakdown ? JSON.parse(item.score_breakdown) as Array<{ reviewerId?: number }> : [];
        return !history.some((review) => review.reviewerId === teacherId);
      } catch { return true; }
    });
    if (!candidate) throw new ReviewPoolError("试卷池暂无可用试卷");

    const now = new Date().toISOString();
    const result = await tx.run(
      `UPDATE answer_block_crops
       SET claimed_by = ?, claimed_at = ?, claim_count = claim_count + 1
       WHERE id = ? AND claimed_by IS NULL AND ${statusClause()}`,
      teacherId,
      now,
      candidate.id
    );
    if (result.changes === 0) {
      throw new ReviewPoolError("试卷刚被其他教师领取，请重试");
    }
    return candidate.id;
  });
  return getPoolEntry(examId, blockId, cropId, db);
~~~~

攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: the impact is not self-only, the preconditions are achievable by a lower-privileged ordinary teacher, and the affected path is a normal production grading workflow rather than an administrator-, operator-, developer-, physical-, or protected-write-only path. The attack crosses an explicit teacher/block/student assignment boundary and was reproduced end to end. Calibrated impact is high due to block-wide durable availability loss and unauthorized grading records; calibrated likelihood is high due to default teacher permissions, low-complexity authenticated HTTP requests, and no victim interaction or race requirement. Applying the required matrix mechanically gives high impact × high likelihood = high because the critical threshold is not met: the attacker already needs a teacher account with exam visibility, no broad confidentiality compromise or installation takeover occurs, and final-score corruption in single-review mode was not demonstrated.

路径
Authenticated ordinary teacher with exam visibility and GRADE_WRITE, but no assignment --POST attacker-selected examId/blockId/cropId--> Express review-pool claim endpoint --Passes after exam-level access and generic permission checks--> ReviewPoolService claimNextPaper/claimSpecificPaper --Writes attacker ID without review-assignment or workload validation--> answer_block_crops.claimed_by and claimed_at --claimed_by is accepted as grading authority--> Review score submission endpoint --Attacker score is persisted; assigned reviewer is rejected--> Unauthorized score history, assigned-reviewer lockout, and block availability impact

Attacker story: a malicious or compromised ordinary teacher can access an exam but is not assigned to grade BLOCK-A or any of its students. The teacher repeatedly calls the normal authenticated review-pool claim endpoint. The Express route checks exam visibility and GRADE_WRITE, then ReviewPoolService selects any unclaimed crop in the supplied exam and block and writes the teacher's ID to claimed_by without consulting review_assignments. Because there is no claim quota or lease expiration, the teacher can drain the block and leave papers locked. Release by another ordinary teacher is rejected, while force release is administrator-only. ReviewService subsequently treats claimed_by as submission authority, so the legitimate assigned teacher is rejected and the attacker can submit a range-valid score. The supplied executable PoC exercised the real Express interface with authentication enabled: an attacker reported by the assignment API as isSelected=false and assignedToMe=0 claimed all three papers assigned exclusively to another teacher, caused the assigned teacher's claim/release/submit attempts to fail, and persisted a score as the reviewer in two-review mode. No credentials or application secrets are exposed or required beyond the attacker's legitimate session. Atomic claiming, score bounds, prior-review checks, and CAS updates limit races and malformed scores but do not establish object-level authorization. The strongest impact counterevidence is that single-review final-score production was not demonstrated because this commit's default review_round conflicts with one-review submission logic; that narrower claim is excluded. Block-wide durable denial of grading and unauthorized multi-review score persistence are independently demonstrated.

发生可能性
High - Likelihood is high for the defined insider threat. Teachers receive GRADE_WRITE by default, only exam-level visibility is required, exploitation uses ordinary HTTP endpoints with attacker-controlled identifiers, no race or victim interaction is needed, and repeated requests can exhaust the pool. The executable reproduction succeeded through the real authenticated Express interface. Direct binding is localhost-only and tunnel use varies by launcher or deployment, but this lowers exposure confidence rather than making the path unrealistic because the product is a web workflow and repository launchers support public tunneling. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - Impact is high because an ordinary teacher crosses an explicit reviewer-to-block/student authorization boundary and can affect every claimable crop in a targeted block. The demonstrated effects are durable block-wide grading unavailability until attacker or administrator action, rejection of the legitimate assigned reviewer, and persistence of an attacker-selected but range-valid score and reviewer identity in a two-review workflow. These threaten academic-result integrity and grading-window availability. Confidentiality impact and automatic single-review finalization are not relied upon.
假设条件
Production authentication remains enabled, as it is by default unless PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is explicitly set to 0 or false.
The attacker has an ordinary teacher account, GRADE_WRITE permission, and exam-level visibility, but no review_assignments row for the targeted block or students.
Claimable answer_block_crops exist for an exam and block that has explicit assignments to other teachers.
Explicit review assignments are intended to constrain grading when assignment records exist; the UI's open-pool fallback applies only when no assignment records exist.
The Express service is reachable through the normal web client, a reverse proxy, or the repository's Cloudflare tunnel launcher.
An authenticated ordinary teacher account
The default or explicitly granted grade:write permission
Exam-level visibility under requireExamAccess
Knowledge of a target examId and blockId
At least one claimable crop assigned to another reviewer
控制措施
Authentication is enabled by default unless PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is explicitly disabled.
The mounted analysis gate requires GRADE_READ, and claim/submit routes additionally require GRADE_WRITE.
requireExamAccess rejects students and teachers lacking visibility of the selected exam.
Claim updates require claimed_by IS NULL, preventing concurrent duplicate ownership.
Review history prevents the same teacher from normally reclaiming and reviewing the same crop again.
Submission validates question membership, numeric finiteness, score bounds, scoring mode, and authoritative maximum scores.
Transactional claim rechecks and compare-and-swap review updates reduce race-condition overwrite risks.
Only the claimant can ordinarily release a paper, and only an administrator can force release another claimant's paper.
No control validates review_assignments, assigned_student_ids, or student_count during claim or submission.
No outstanding-claim quota, claim lease expiration, stale-claim reclamation, or general request rate limit is present.
盲点
The checkout proves loopback binding and provides Cloudflare tunnel launchers, but it does not establish which ingress configuration every real deployment uses.
The supplied executable reproduction used SQLite; the SQL is straightforward and shared through DbAdapter, but the same end-to-end exploit was not separately rerun against MariaDB.
The reproduction proved unauthorized score persistence in two-review mode, not that a newly created single-review crop necessarily reaches a final score at this commit.
The repository does not fully specify whether review assignments are intended as mandatory authorization in every institutional policy; however, explicit assignments existed in the reproduced case and the threat model treats block/student reviewer boundaries as security boundaries.
No confidentiality impact from crop images is included because this finding's demonstrated path concerns claim ownership, grading integrity, and availability.

## 8. Unbounded annotations enable persistent layout and PDF DoS

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/ae21198237b08191be712769f9ad01be?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Unbounded annotations enable persistent layout and PDF DoS
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
81ad500
1:38 PM Aug 3, 2026

操作者：Tim-Hanchuan-Yang

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced. This commit adds the persisted `annotation` input and its per-line expansion/rendering but adds only a client-side 160-character restriction. It does not add equivalent validation in the server normalization path, even though the server already explicitly bounds other layout-amplifying data such as essay-grid dimensions and target character counts.

The client sets `maxLength={160}`, but `PUT /api/cards/:cardId` accepts a free-form card body and `normalizeCard()` spreads every subjective question without validating the type or length of `annotation`. The layout code normalizes the complete string, slices it into lines based on the cell width, and allocates a text-and-rectangle object for every line. Fill-blank pagination invokes this layout work repeatedly while estimating segment heights and then again while constructing the final layout. `saveCardWithLayout()` subsequently serializes the expanded structure to disk. With an approximately 8 MB ASCII annotation, an A4 cell produces on the order of 100,000 annotation-line objects, substantial transient memory allocation, a large layout file, and event-loop CPU consumption. On SQLite, where the TEXT column is not narrowly bounded, the malicious annotation remains stored, so subsequent layout, recognition-preparation, and PDF requests repeat the expensive processing; PDF generation additionally invokes PDFKit once per generated line. Concurrent requests can exhaust heap or monopolize the Node process. Authentication reduces reachability, but default teacher roles have the permission accepted by the `/api/cards` write gate, and there is no general API rate limit. The annotation should be validated server-side as a string with a small maximum length before `buildLayout()` runs; layout generation should also enforce aggregate line and output-size limits.

验证
 Attacker-controlled annotation can enter the production card-save API under a realistic authenticated teacher role, bypassing the browser-only 160-character limit.
 Server-side normalization and persistence impose no equivalent annotation type/length limit before layout generation; only the broader 8 MiB JSON-body limit applies.
 Layout and PDF processing expand annotation length into unbounded per-line objects/operations and serialize the expanded layout to disk.
 Focused production-path tests demonstrated material CPU, memory, and output amplification: 108,696 line objects, a 34.3 MB layout file, a 35-second PDF operation, and approximately 2.64 GiB peak RSS.
 Stored annotation was reloaded through the real API and triggered repeated layout work; ordinary teachers possess the required permission. A literal process crash was not required to establish the demonstrated availability risk.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The intended 160-character annotation limit exists only as a browser `maxLength` and is bypassable by direct API requests.
- Subjective questions are copied with `...q`; unlike essay-grid amplification parameters, the newly added annotation is not type-checked or length-limited.
- Card saves build the attacker-controlled layout before database persistence and serialize the expanded layout to disk.
- PDF generation performs a PDFKit text-rendering operation for every attacker-generated annotation line, creating a repeatable CPU and output-size sink.
- The complete annotation is copied and split into an array with no maximum text or line count.
- Every generated line becomes a separate object containing text and rectangle data, amplifying a compact input string into a large in-memory layout.
- Fill-blank pagination repeatedly computes segment heights before constructing the segment, causing annotation wrapping and allocation to be repeated.

~~~~typescript
async function saveCardWithLayout(cardRepo: CardRepository, card: AnswerCard, createdBy?: number): Promise<AnswerCard> {
  const normalized = normalizeCard(card, card.id);
  const layout = buildLayout(normalized);
  const exists = await cardRepo.findById(normalized.id);

  if (exists) {
    await cardRepo.updateCard(normalized);
  } else {
    await cardRepo.createCard(normalized, createdBy);
    await cardRepo.updateCard(normalized);
  }

  await writeLayoutDocument(normalized.id, layout);
~~~~

### [src/shared/layout.ts:712](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/81ad50044fd0a070c9570742a60cf270cb31f08b/src/shared/layout.ts#L712)

~~~~typescript
/** 按单元格宽度把注释文字按字符折行（中文按全角字符估算宽度）。 */
function wrapBlankAnnotation(text: string, maxWidthMm: number): string[] {
  const clean = text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  if (!clean) return [];
  const charsPerLine = Math.max(1, Math.floor(maxWidthMm / BLANK_ANNOTATION_CHAR_WIDTH));
  const lines: string[] = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < clean.length; i += charsPerLine) lines.push(clean.slice(i, i + charsPerLine));
  return lines;
~~~~

攻击路径分析

Policy adjustment was applied after establishing the facts. Hard suppression does not apply: the effect is not self-only; preconditions are plausible; and the attacker is an ordinary teacher using a routine content-authoring workflow, not an administrator, host operator, developer, or physical-access attacker. Although PUT /api/cards/:cardId is a protected write path, the security delta is that routinely authorized teacher content crosses into unbounded shared-server resource consumption; the authority to design a card does not authorize denial of service. Remote reachability is deployment-dependent but supported by the repository's tunnel path, and the authenticated real-API PoC proves reachability when the service is available. With high impact and high likelihood, the required matrix yields high unless critical criteria are satisfied. Critical is not warranted because the demonstrated reach is one shared service and there is no RCE, administrator takeover, complete data extraction, or silent systemic grade corruption.

路径
Malicious or compromised ordinary teacher account --Sends authenticated network request--> Project-X HTTP API on port 5174, optionally exposed through Cloudflare Tunnel or another proxy --Routes request to /api/cards--> Server-side /api/cards RBAC gate requiring grade:write for PUT --Default ordinary-teacher permission satisfies the write gate--> PUT /api/cards/:cardId with an oversized subjective-question annotation --Supplies free-form annotation below the global 8 MiB JSON limit--> normalizeCard() copies annotation without a server-side bound --Unbounded annotation reaches buildLayout()--> buildLayout() repeatedly wraps text and allocates one object per annotation line --Expanded layout is serialized and annotation is retained on SQLite--> SQLite TEXT and pretty-printed layout JSON persist attacker-amplified content --Stored card can be requested repeatedly--> GET /api/cards/:cardId/layout and GET /api/cards/:cardId/pdf reload and process the stored card --PDF endpoint rebuilds and renders every line--> PDFKit drawText operation for each annotation line --Amplifies one stored annotation into multi-gigabyte memory use and prolonged processing--> Shared Node process CPU, memory, disk, bandwidth, and event-loop exhaustion

Attacker story: a malicious ordinary teacher, or an attacker holding a teacher session, bypasses the browser-only maxLength=160 control and submits a roughly 7.5 MB annotation directly to PUT /api/cards/:cardId. This is a routine product API protected by server-side authentication and RBAC, but ordinary teachers receive the permission accepted by the write gate. The server copies the annotation without validation, performs layout construction before persistence, and creates one render object per wrapped line. On SQLite, it then persists the full annotation and a much larger pretty-printed layout, allowing later layout and PDF requests to repeat the work. The supplied executable PoC used the real authenticated Express API and an ordinary teacher: the PUT returned 200, retained all 7,500,000 characters, produced a 34,315,466-byte layout file, and a later layout request returned 14,618,017 bytes. A production createPdf benchmark took 35.07 seconds, emitted 57,801,015 bytes, and reached approximately 2.84 GB RSS. The strongest counterevidence is that the process binds only to loopback, public tunneling is opt-in, authentication is enabled by default, the endpoint is a protected teacher write operation, requests are capped at 8 MiB, MariaDB TEXT is more restrictive than SQLite, and bounded-heap layout-only tests did not crash. These facts reduce deployment-independent likelihood but are not dispositive. The repository provides a public-tunnel deployment path; ordinary teachers are intended remote product users rather than operators; the authenticated PoC passed the actual gate; the 8 MiB cap still admitted a payload with multi-gigabyte PDF amplification; SQLite is a supported backend and demonstrated persistence; and the PDF sink's dominant external/ArrayBuffer allocation is not controlled by the tested JavaScript heap caps. No literal crash or concurrent exhaustion was demonstrated, but one production-function PDF invocation already established severe CPU and memory consumption. No application secrets are read or exposed by this path; the relevant trust boundary is teacher-controlled persisted content entering a shared server-side resource-intensive renderer.

发生可能性
High - Likelihood is high for the in-scope malicious or compromised teacher actor: ordinary teachers receive the required permission by default, exploitation uses one normal authenticated API request with no race or complex chain, the client-side limit is trivially bypassed, and there is no general rate limit. The exact path was demonstrated through the real API. Loopback binding, opt-in public ingress, the 8 MiB request cap, and MariaDB's lower TEXT capacity reduce universality but do not make exploitation unlikely in the supported SQLite and tunnel/proxy deployment described by repository artifacts. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - Impact is high because a single authenticated card can expand into more than 100,000 retained layout objects, tens of megabytes of persistent and generated output, prolonged event-loop work, and multi-gigabyte process memory during PDF generation. The affected Node process hosts shared card, scanning, grading, and analysis workflows, so failure or monopolization affects users other than the attacker. The finding does not establish code execution, confidentiality loss, grade corruption, or fleet-wide compromise, so it does not meet the threat model's critical criteria.
假设条件
The attacker is a malicious ordinary teacher or has compromised an ordinary teacher session; no administrator, host, or developer access is required.
Production authentication remains enabled, which is the repository default unless PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is explicitly set to 0 or false.
Remote exposure is deployment-dependent: the Node process binds to 127.0.0.1:5174, while the repository's start script supports forwarding it through an optional Cloudflare Quick Tunnel.
Persistent repeat-trigger behavior is strongest on the supported SQLite backend. MariaDB TEXT size limits may reject the oversized value during persistence, although saveCardWithLayout still performs layout expansion before the database write.
The supplied executable validation artifacts and captured results were produced against this exact commit using isolated local storage and the real authenticated Express interface.
Network access to the Project-X web/API service, directly or through its configured tunnel or proxy
A valid ordinary teacher account or session carrying the default grade:write permission
A PUT /api/cards/:cardId request containing a fill-blank subjective question with an oversized annotation
For persistent retriggering through stored layout and PDF requests, a backend that retains the oversized annotation, as demonstrated with supported SQLite
控制措施
Authentication is enabled by default; valid bearer-token or cookie authentication is required unless the compatibility flag explicitly disables enforcement.
The /api/cards RBAC gate requires grade:write for PUT requests, but that permission is granted to ordinary teachers by default.
The global Express JSON parser limits individual requests to 8 MiB, bounding input size but not the demonstrated layout and PDF amplification.
The React editor applies maxLength=160, but this is not a server-side security control.
The Node process binds to 127.0.0.1:5174; remote access requires a proxy or tunnel, and the repository supplies an optional Cloudflare Quick Tunnel path.
CORS uses an origin allowlist, but non-browser clients can still send authenticated requests.
No general card-layout or PDF rate limit, concurrency cap, aggregate element limit, output-size limit, or annotation-specific server validation is present.
MariaDB TEXT storage limits may prevent long-term persistence of a multi-megabyte annotation, but layout construction occurs before the database update and SQLite demonstrably retains it.
There is no code-execution sink or secret-disclosure path in this finding; the demonstrated effect is shared-service availability exhaustion.
盲点
No literal process termination, host OOM, disk exhaustion, or concurrent-request test was performed; exact failure thresholds depend on host memory, Node version, PDFKit behavior, and process supervision.
The validation PDF benchmark invoked the production createPdf() function and drained its stream, but it did not send the PDF through the complete HTTP endpoint under concurrent load.
The repository does not establish that every deployment enables the optional Cloudflare tunnel or another public reverse proxy; some installations may remain reachable only from the local host or managed network.
Persistent replay was demonstrated with SQLite. MariaDB TEXT size limits may reject the full annotation at database update time, although pre-persistence layout work remains reachable.
No infrastructure manifest establishes container memory limits, autoscaling, worker isolation, reverse-proxy request limits, or external rate limiting that an operator might add outside the repository.
The finding does not quantify how many simultaneous requests are required to terminate each supported production environment.

## 9. Multi-page papers enable disk and heap exhaustion

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/a535a22db0008191abde49272868dd8a?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Multi-page papers enable disk and heap exhaustion
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
7d5086d
9:16 PM Jul 26, 2026

操作者：NaOH1g

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced. At the baseline revision, the endpoint accepted one file and stored it under a fixed `original.*` name, while AI analysis returned after loading one image or PDF. This commit changes the route to a 40-file array, appends uniquely numbered pages indefinitely, and changes AI loading to retain all pages without adding an aggregate resource limit.

The card route gate permits writes to users with `grade:write`, which teachers receive by default. The changed endpoint now accepts up to 40 files, while Multer's only size control is the per-file 50 MB limit. Consequently, one request can place approximately 2 GB in the temporary directory. The handler then assigns indexes starting after the current maximum and stores every page under a distinct filename. There is no total-byte quota, maximum page count across requests, or rate limit, so repeated requests permanently grow the paper directory instead of overwriting the one bounded original file used at the baseline revision. The new AI path compounds this problem: `getPaperFiles` reads every accumulated image or PDF and retains its base64 representation in an array, after which `JSON.stringify` constructs another large representation for the sidecar request. With a multimodal provider configured, a modest number of maximum-sized pages can exceed the Node heap and terminate the process; independently, uploads can fill the data volume and disrupt scanning, grading, and database writes. Enforce a small aggregate request limit and a per-card page/byte quota, reject uploads before persistence when that quota would be exceeded, clean temporary files on every failure path, and stream or strictly size-bound AI inputs rather than collecting every page as base64.

验证
 Confirm attacker reachability: a normal default-role teacher authenticated through the real login endpoint and possessed the grade:write permission required by the card route gate.
 Confirm insufficient request controls: the code permits 40 files at 50 MiB each and has no aggregate request-byte, route-rate, or per-card quota.
 Confirm cross-request persistence: two real HTTP requests appended pages 1-40 and 41-80, retaining all 80 files and database rows rather than overwriting the prior paper.
 Confirm AI amplification and availability sink: all 80 PDF pages were read as base64 and JSON serialization caused a fatal Node heap exhaustion under a bounded 128 MiB heap.
 Demonstrate realistic impact with bounded testing: authenticated HTTP reproduction retained exactly 83,886,080 bytes; source arithmetic establishes up to 2,000 MiB per request, while avoiding an unsafe host-disk-fill test.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Multer applies only a 50 MB per-file limit; there is no aggregate request-byte limit.
- The changed route accepts up to 40 maximum-sized files in one request.
- Every request starts after MAX(page_index) and persists distinct page files, with no total page or byte quota.
- Multimodal analysis collects the files and serializes all base64 data into one JSON request.
- All stored images or PDFs are read and retained as base64 strings, allowing aggregate page size to exhaust the Node heap.

async function getPaperFiles(cardId: string): Promise<Array<{ mimeType: string; base64: string }>> {
  const dir = paperDir(cardId);
  const files: Array<{ mimeType: string; base64: string }> = [];
  let entries: string[] = [];
  try { entries = await readdir(dir); } catch { return files; }

  const imgRe = /^original(-\d+)?\.(jpg|jpeg|png|bmp|tiff|webp)$/i;
  const pdfRe = /^original(-\d+)?\.pdf$/i;

  // 所有页的图片（按页码排序）
  const imgEntries = entries.filter((e) => imgRe.test(e)).sort();
  for (const e of imgEntries) {
    const fp = path.join(dir, e);
    const buf = await readFile(fp);
    files.push({ mimeType: getFileMime(e), base64: buf.toString("base64") });
  }
  if (files.length) return files;

  // 回退：所有页的 PDF
  const pdfEntries = entries.filter((e) => pdfRe.test(e)).sort();
  for (const e of pdfEntries) {
    const fp = path.join(dir, e);
    const buf = await readFile(fp);
    files.push({ mimeType: "application/pdf", base64: buf.toString("base64") });
  }
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The established facts produce high impact multiplied by high likelihood. The attacker is a realistic in-scope default-role teacher, and the privilege delta is service-wide resource exhaustion rather than merely using an authorized upload for self-only effects. The executable validation confirmed cross-request persistent growth and a bounded-heap process crash. Under the required matrix, high impact plus high likelihood remains high because the critical threshold is not met: authentication is required, the target is a single service, recovery and deletion are possible, and no confidentiality, identity takeover, remote code execution, or silent grade-integrity compromise is shown. The original high rating is therefore retained rather than suppressed or promoted.

路径
Authenticated default-role teacher with grade:write --uses a valid teacher session over the remotely exposed web service--> Documented Nginx HTTPS proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel --forwards authenticated multipart requests to loopback port 5174--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/paper --accepts up to 40 x 50 MiB and appends unique page files across requests--> Unbounded original-N.pdf files and original_paper_pages rows --accumulated pages become analysis input--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/knowledge-points/analyze --reads every page, base64-encodes it, and serializes the full array--> All pages retained as base64 and serialized as JSON --aggregate allocation can exceed the V8 heap limit--> Shared disk exhaustion or Node process termination

An ordinary teacher can use the normal authenticated paper-upload API to consume resources beyond the privilege intended by grade:write. Multer limits each file to 50 MiB but accepts 40 files, allowing approximately 2,000 MiB of content in one request. The handler starts allocation after the current maximum page index and stores each accepted page under a distinct name, so later requests accumulate rather than replace prior content. PDFs are copied without compression. The executable validation used the real login and HTTP routes: an unauthenticated upload returned 401, while a default-role teacher uploaded two sets of 40 one-MiB PDFs, producing 80 rows, 80 files, and 83,886,080 persistent bytes. The optional multimodal path reads all accumulated pages into base64 strings and then JSON-serializes them; validation against the production endpoint with an intentionally constrained 128 MiB heap terminated Node with a fatal out-of-memory error before any external LLM response was needed. The strongest counterevidence is that Node binds only to 127.0.0.1, authentication and grade:write are required, each file and request have finite limits, explicit deletion endpoints exist, and the demonstrated heap crash used a constrained heap. These facts reduce neither reachability nor the disk issue dispositively: repository deployment documentation and scripts expose loopback port 5174 through Nginx or Cloudflare Tunnel, grade:write belongs to the default teacher role, 40 times 50 MiB remains an exceptionally large per-request allowance, repeated requests have no cumulative quota, and deletion is a reactive authorized action rather than automatic prevention. AI configuration and the exact default-heap threshold remain deployment-dependent, but persistent disk growth is independent of AI and was directly reproduced.

发生可能性
High - The path uses documented production HTTP interfaces, requires only a default teacher account, involves no race or specialized parser exploit, and was reproduced through authentication and the production routes. Public deployment through Nginx or Cloudflare Tunnel is explicitly supported. Disk growth works without AI and can be repeated indefinitely. Actual upstream body limits, available disk, and heap size are deployment-dependent, but no effective limit is established in the repository. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The attack can consume the persistent volume used by the application and disrupt paper upload, scanning, grading, and database-dependent operation for all users. The multimodal branch can terminate the shared Node process through aggregate base64 and JSON allocations. No direct confidentiality or grade-integrity compromise is established, and the effect is limited to one deployed Project-X service, so the impact is high availability loss rather than critical system compromise.
假设条件
Production authentication remains enabled.
The attacker controls a normal teacher account or an equivalent account carrying the default grade:write permission.
The installation uses the repository-documented HTTPS reverse proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel deployment path, making the loopback-bound Node service remotely reachable.
No external reverse proxy request-body limit or filesystem quota absent from the repository imposes a substantially smaller effective aggregate limit.
A multimodal OpenAI or Gemini provider is configured only for the optional heap-exhaustion branch; persistent disk exhaustion does not require an AI provider.
A valid default-role teacher session with grade:write
A valid or teacher-created answer card identifier
Repeated multipart uploads for disk exhaustion
A configured multimodal provider and invocation of knowledge-points analysis for the heap-exhaustion branch
控制措施
Authentication is required when PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled; validation confirmed unauthenticated upload receives HTTP 401.
Write requests under /api/cards require grade:write through cardGate.
Multer limits each individual file to 50 MiB.
Multer limits one upload request to 40 files.
File validation rejects unsupported paper filename extensions and oversized individual files.
Page-index allocation and database inserts occur in a transaction, preventing duplicate index races but not resource accumulation.
Authorized deletion endpoints can remove one page or all pages.
The Node listener binds to 127.0.0.1, although documented reverse proxies and tunnels make it remotely reachable.
CORS uses an origin allowlist, but CORS is not a server-side defense against a direct authenticated HTTP client.
No repository-level aggregate upload-byte limit, cumulative per-card page/byte quota, or paper-route rate limit was found.
盲点
The checkout cannot establish whether a particular deployment has an external Nginx, CDN, Cloudflare, or WAF request-body limit smaller than the application's approximately 2,000 MiB per-request allowance.
The amount of free disk, filesystem quotas, process supervision, and restart behavior are deployment-specific.
The heap crash was demonstrated with --max-old-space-size=128; the exact page count needed to crash a default Node heap varies by Node version and runtime configuration.
The multimodal memory branch requires an OpenAI or Gemini provider, but the independently confirmed persistent disk branch does not.
The validation used one-MiB PDFs to bound resource consumption; it did not submit a full 40-by-50-MiB request.
Repository deletion endpoints permit manual cleanup, but static evidence does not establish monitoring or operator response time during an attack.

## 10. Grading upload bypasses exam-scope authorization

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/c51023c02c9c8191a6f302611d462bd5?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

Grading upload bypasses exam-scope authorization
严重级别
High

概要

This range introduced an authorization regression by removing the exam-scope check from the grading upload route. The remaining cardGate establishes only the broad GRADE_WRITE capability, while validateExamIdsAccess still implements the required object-level visibility check but is no longer invoked before grade persistence.

The POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading route is protected only by the general grade:write capability. It accepts examId from the multipart body and now passes it directly to persistGradingResults without calling validateExamIdsAccess. A subject teacher or head teacher can therefore specify an exam outside their assigned subject or classes and upload valid answer-card images containing chosen student numbers and answers. persistGradingResults verifies only that the exam and student exist; it then changes the target exam's status, creates a scan batch, and upserts student_scores and question_scores for every recognized image. The route also does not require the selected card to match the target exam's card, further simplifying abuse. A successful batch can overwrite multiple students' grades and close the unauthorized exam. The baseline revision explicitly parsed the examId and called validateExamIdsAccess, returning 403 for exams outside the caller's visibility.

验证
 Confirmed the grading endpoint is reachable by a scoped teacher with default grade:write through the actual authentication and cardGate middleware chain, while an exam-scoped endpoint rejects the same target with 403.
 Confirmed multipart examId is attacker-controlled and reaches persistence independently of the selected route card; the reproduction used attacker-card against an exam configured for target-card.
 Confirmed persistGradingResults accepts the existing out-of-scope exam and mutates its scan batch, student score, question score, and workflow status without an equivalent scope check.
 Confirmed validateExamIdsAccess is the applicable object-scope control, is absent from the current grading route, and was explicitly removed by the target revert.
 Reproduced the authorization bypass through the real authenticated HTTP and multipart interface with focused assertions and documented the native-OCR substitution as the remaining proof limitation.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Persistence checks only exam existence and immediately changes its status and creates a grading batch.
- Recognized student numbers are resolved globally, after which the target exam's aggregate and per-question scores are upserted.
- The /api/cards write surface is guarded only by the broad GRADE_WRITE capability, not exam-level scope.
- The grading route accepts attacker-controlled files and examId, but no longer validates that the caller may access that exam.
- The unchecked examId is passed directly into the persistence routine.
- The existing helper enforces teacher exam visibility and returns 403, but the grading route no longer calls it.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts:185](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/1b0d370ba5a81dfe3d8f1edfca158bc276d7fabc/src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts#L185)

~~~~typescript
export async function validateExamIdsAccess(req: express.Request, res: express.Response, examIds: number[]): Promise<boolean> {
  const visibleIds = await getVisibleExamIds(req.user);
  if (visibleIds === null) return true;
  const visible = new Set(visibleIds);
  const denied = examIds.filter((examId) => !visible.has(examId));
  if (denied.length === 0) return true;
  res.status(403).json({ message: "权限不足：考试组包含不可访问的考试" });
  return false;
~~~~


攻击路径分析

After establishing the attack-path facts, the required matrix maps high impact and high likelihood to high unless the critical criteria clearly apply. High impact is supported by direct, executable proof of out-of-scope score replacement, question-score replacement, scan-batch creation, and exam closure. High likelihood is supported by a remotely exposable normal product endpoint, default teacher grade:write permission, attacker-controlled examId, and no object-level authorization at the sink. Hard suppression does not apply: impact is not self-only; the preconditions are plausible; the attacker is a normal scoped teacher rather than an administrator, operator, developer, or host user; and the privilege-escalation delta is the ability to alter exams explicitly outside that teacher's class/subject scope. The issue is not raised to critical because exploitation still requires a trusted teacher credential, knowledge of target identifiers, and successfully recognized input, and the demonstrated effect is within a selected school installation and exam rather than unauthenticated installation-wide compromise. The final reportability decision is therefore reportable at high severity, with no policy suppression.

路径
Authenticated subject/head teacher with default grade:write --Sends authenticated HTTP request--> Publicly tunnelable Express API on loopback port 5174 --Request reaches /api/cards middleware--> cardGate broad capability check --grade:write passes without exam visibility check--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading with files and body examId --Attacker-controlled files are recognized and scored--> Answer-card recognition and grading using URL card --Recognized rows and independent body examId are forwarded--> persistGradingResults with unchecked target examId --Repository upserts scores and changes workflow state--> Out-of-scope exam scores, scan batch, and status mutated

Project-X's React client uses POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading as its normal answer-card grading and persistence workflow. Authentication is enabled by default, and the /api/cards middleware requires grade:write for POST requests; teachers receive that permission by default. However, the gate has no exam context. The route independently reads cardId from the URL, files and examId from the multipart request, grades the files using the selected card, and forwards the unchecked examId to persistGradingResults. Persistence verifies only that the exam and globally resolved students exist. It then changes the target exam's state, creates a scan batch, and upserts student_scores and question_scores. There is no comparison between cardId and exam.card_id. The supplied executable HTTP reproduction used a real authenticated Express route, middleware, repositories, transactions, and SQLite database and demonstrated a class-scoped subject teacher overwriting an out-of-scope score from 3 to 10, writing a question score, creating a completed batch, and closing an exam configured for a different card. The native OCR binary was replaced by a deterministic contract-compatible recognizer, which limits conclusions about crafting particular image pixels but does not undermine the demonstrated authorization and persistence failure after successful recognition. No application secret is needed or exposed; the sensitive flow is attacker-selected grading input into authoritative student score and exam-workflow data. Strong counterevidence consists of loopback binding, required teacher authentication, and the presence of validateExamIdsAccess. It is not dispositive because repository deployment guidance exposes port 5174 through a public tunnel, ordinary teachers receive grade:write, and the affected route does not call the object-level helper. This is therefore a reportable authorization vulnerability rather than a correctness-only defect.

发生可能性
High - The endpoint is a normal first-party grading workflow and can be reached remotely when deployed through the repository-documented Cloudflare tunnel. Teachers receive grade:write by default, no victim interaction is required, and the supplied executable HTTP reproduction demonstrated the complete authentication-to-database path. Preconditions are limited to a scoped teacher account, a target exam ID, existing student numbers, and successfully recognized files. The deterministic recognizer substitution leaves uncertainty about reliable adversarial pixel crafting, but a teacher can also submit otherwise valid recognizable answer cards and redirect their persistence by changing examId, so exploitation remains plausible and low-complexity for the intended insider threat. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The sink is authoritative academic data. A scoped teacher can overwrite aggregate and per-question grades for one or many recognized students in an exam outside the teacher's assigned subject or classes, create an apparently legitimate scan batch attributed to that teacher, and change the exam workflow from active to grading or closed. This crosses an explicit teacher/exam authorization boundary and can materially affect academic outcomes. Confidentiality impact is not required; integrity is high and workflow availability impact is secondary.
假设条件
Production uses the repository's default authentication-enforced mode; PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE has not been explicitly set to 0 or false.
The attacker has a legitimate or compromised subject-teacher or head-teacher account, has completed any mandatory password change, and therefore receives the default grade:write permission.
The attacker knows or can obtain an existing out-of-scope numeric exam ID and one or more existing student numbers.
Uploaded answer-card files must produce successful recognizer output. The supplied executable reproduction substituted a deterministic recognizer, so reliability of crafting particular physical image pixels was not established.
The scope.auth value internal_only denotes a normal authenticated school staff account rather than an administrator-only or unauthenticated endpoint.
Authenticated teacher account with grade:write
Subject-teacher or head-teacher scope that excludes the target exam
Existing card ID accepted by the grading route
Existing out-of-scope exam ID
Uploaded files yielding successful recognition of existing student numbers
控制措施
Authentication is enforced by default unless PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is explicitly set to 0 or false.
Bearer-token or cookie identity is parsed before API routes, and accounts requiring a password change are blocked from other API operations.
cardGate returns 401 to unauthenticated callers and 403 to users lacking grade:write.
Default students lack grade:write; the demonstrated attacker is a teacher.
getVisibleExamIds and validateExamIdsAccess correctly model subject-teacher and head-teacher exam scope for routes that invoke them.
The vulnerable grading route does not invoke validateExamIdsAccess.
CORS uses an origin allowlist, but CORS is not authorization and does not prevent direct authenticated HTTP requests.
Recognition uploads have a 20 MiB per-file limit.
Persistence verifies that the target exam and recognized student exist and uses per-student transactions, but these checks do not establish authorization.
The backend binds to 127.0.0.1, while documented Cloudflare ingress can make the API remotely reachable.
Login throttling exists, but no grading-route rate limit mitigates an already authenticated teacher.
盲点
The executable reproduction used SQLite; MariaDB was not separately exercised, although both paths use the same repository and adapter interfaces.
The native OCR executable was replaced with a deterministic implementation of its successful output contract, so the reproduction does not quantify how reliably arbitrary physical images can force chosen recognition results.
The repository documents public Cloudflare ingress, but the actual deployment configuration for any particular installation is unknown; installations without a tunnel may be limited to localhost or another trusted proxy path.
Static evidence does not establish how easily a scoped teacher can discover a particular hidden exam ID or every target student's number, though both are ordinary school-domain identifiers and the executable reproduction proves that possession is sufficient.
No separate network policy, reverse-proxy access-control configuration, or external identity-aware proxy manifest is present in the inspected repository artifacts.
The precise number of students that can be modified in one practical request depends on recognizer throughput, request handling, and operational limits beyond the per-file size limit.

<a id="结果-11"></a>

## 11. Failed paper conversions leak 50 MB temporary uploads

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/249c894561c08191a8427dde9773ea90?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Failed paper conversions leak 50 MB temporary uploads
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
0439c2a
8:40 PM Jul 4, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced: the commit adds the paper upload and conversion path with success-only temporary-file cleanup. Cleanup must be placed in a finally block covering all post-upload processing, and uploaded content should also be validated by magic bytes before it reaches Sharp or other parsers.

The upload middleware writes each file, up to 50 MB, into the papers/_tmp directory. Validation only checks the attacker-controlled filename extension and file size, so arbitrary data named with an image extension such as .jpg is accepted and passed to Sharp. Sharp rejects malformed image data during metadata extraction or conversion, causing storePaperFile() to throw. The temporary file is deleted only after storePaperFile() returns successfully; the catch block returns HTTP 500 without deleting req.file.path. Multer generates a separate temporary file for every request, and there is no cleanup logic for this directory. A teacher with the default grade:write permission can therefore repeatedly submit malformed, extension-valid files and permanently consume up to 50 MB of disk per request. This can fill the application volume and disrupt scanning, grading, database operation, and other uploads during operationally sensitive periods.

验证
 The paper-upload endpoint is reachable by an ordinary authenticated teacher with the default grade:write permission, and the route has no stricter card ownership or existence check.
 An extension-valid malformed image is accepted by Multer, written under papers/_tmp with a unique name, and can be almost 50 MiB.
 Malformed content reaches Sharp, causes a handled conversion exception/HTTP 500, and bypasses the route's success-only unlink.
 Endpoint-level reproduction showed repeated failures leave distinct complete temporary files with matching sizes and hashes; the exact 50 MiB boundary was also tested as a non-leaking control.
 No repository paper-temp cleanup, aggregate quota, or upload rate limit bounds repeated accumulation; the scheduled cleanup only handles DB-linked scan records.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Validation trusts the supplied filename extension and size without checking file signatures or decodability.
- Extension-valid attacker data reaches Sharp, where malformed content can throw before the route performs temporary-file cleanup.
- Multer persists uploads in a temporary directory and permits files up to 50 MB; filtering delegates to extension-only validation.
- The temporary upload is deleted only after storePaperFile succeeds. Conversion exceptions reach the catch block without unlinking file.path.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/paper-converter.ts:24](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/0439c2a7625be88e492b15c6eaca3f90ac9e17a7/src/apps/answer-card/server/paper-converter.ts#L24)

~~~~typescript
export function validatePaperFile(filename: string, size: number): string | null {
  const ext = path.extname(filename).toLowerCase();
  if (ext === ".doc") return "不支持 .doc 格式，请转为 .docx 后上传";
  if (!ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS.has(ext)) return `不支持 ${ext} 格式，请上传 DOCX/PDF/图片文件`;
  if (size > MAX_FILE_SIZE) return `文件过大（${(size / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)}MB），最大 50MB`;
  return null;
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/paper-converter.ts:49](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/0439c2a7625be88e492b15c6eaca3f90ac9e17a7/src/apps/answer-card/server/paper-converter.ts#L49)

~~~~typescript
export async function compressImage(inputPath: string): Promise<Buffer> {
  let image = sharp(inputPath);
  const metadata = await image.metadata();

  if (metadata.width && metadata.width > 2048) {
    image = image.resize(2048, 2048, { fit: "inside", withoutEnlargement: true });
  }

  const buf = await image.jpeg({ quality: 80 }).toBuffer();
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/routes/paper-routes.ts:18](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/0439c2a7625be88e492b15c6eaca3f90ac9e17a7/src/apps/answer-card/server/routes/paper-routes.ts#L18)

~~~~typescript
const paperUpload = multer({
  dest: path.resolve(process.cwd(), "data", "answer-card", "papers", "_tmp"),
  limits: { fileSize: 50 * 1024 * 1024 },
  fileFilter: (_req, file, cb) => {
    const err = validatePaperFile(file.originalname, 50 * 1024 * 1024);
    if (err) {
      cb(new Error(err));
    } else {
      cb(null, true);
    }
  },
});

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/routes/paper-routes.ts:35](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/0439c2a7625be88e492b15c6eaca3f90ac9e17a7/src/apps/answer-card/server/routes/paper-routes.ts#L35)

  router.post("/api/cards/:cardId/paper", paperUpload.single("file"), async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
    try {
      const cardId = String(req.params.cardId);
      const file = req.file;
      if (!file) {
        res.status(400).json({ error: "未选择文件" });
        return;
      }

      const error = validatePaperFile(file.originalname, file.size);
      if (error) {
        unlinkSync(file.path);
        res.status(400).json({ error });
        return;
      }

      await ensurePaperDir(cardId);
      const dir = paperDir(cardId);

      const { originalPath, pdfPath } = await storePaperFile(
        file.path,
        file.originalname,
        dir
      );

      // 清理 multer 临时文件
      try { unlinkSync(file.path); } catch {}

      // 更新 answer_cards 表
      const db = getMysqlDb();
      const relPath = path.relative(path.resolve(process.cwd(), "data", "answer-card"), originalPath);
      await db.run(
        "UPDATE answer_cards SET has_original_paper = 1, original_paper_filename = ?, original_paper_path = ?, updated_at = datetime('now') WHERE id = ?",
        file.originalname, relPath, cardId
      );

      res.json({
        success: true,
        filename: file.originalname,
        size: file.size,
        pdfAvailable: !!pdfPath,
      });
    } catch (err: any) {
      console.error("[paper] upload failed:", err);
      res.status(500).json({ error: err.message || "上传失败" });
    }
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: impact is shared rather than self-only; preconditions are plausible; and exploitation requires neither administrator/operator access nor a protected local write path. The attacker is an ordinary teacher crossing from authorized paper upload into unauthorized consumption of shared server storage. Network scope is remote because the repository includes public Nginx listeners proxying to the loopback service. The strongest limiting facts are authentication, the per-file Multer bound, loopback binding of Node itself, and the need for repeated requests. Authentication still leaves every default teacher eligible, the reverse proxy defeats localhost-only isolation, and the per-file bound does not cap cumulative retained storage. Impact is therefore high and likelihood is high. Applying the required matrix mechanically yields high rather than critical because the demonstrated effect is availability loss of a single service/filesystem, not administrator takeover, scalable silent grade corruption, fleet compromise, or remote code execution.

路径
Ordinary teacher with default grade:write --Authenticated multipart request with allowed extension--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/paper --Multer persists up to just under 50 MiB--> Multer disk storage in papers/_tmp --Extension-valid content reaches storePaperFile and metadata()--> Sharp metadata parsing failure --Malformed image throws before success-only cleanup--> Catch path omits temporary-file unlink --Unique leaked files accumulate without quota or stale-temp cleanup--> Persistent cumulative disk exhaustion

Attacker story: a compromised or malicious ordinary teacher repeatedly uploads malformed data named malformed.jpg through the normal original-paper workflow. The card RBAC gate requires grade:write for POST requests when authentication is enforced, and that permission is granted to teachers by default. Multer writes each body to papers/_tmp before route processing. Validation checks only the attacker-controlled extension and size, after which Sharp metadata parsing throws. Cleanup occurs only after storePaperFile() succeeds, while the catch path sends HTTP 500 without unlinking the temporary file. The supplied executable validation exercised the unchanged gate, router, converter, and Sharp path over HTTP: unauthenticated access returned 401, while authenticated 52,428,799-byte and 1 MiB malformed .jpg uploads both returned 500 and left byte-identical files totaling 53,481,472 bytes. The strongest counterevidence is that the Node process binds only to 127.0.0.1, authenticated RBAC blocks unauthenticated requests when enabled, and Multer rejects an exact 50 MiB upload while deleting its partial file. These controls are not dispositive: the checked-in Nginx configuration publishes the loopback service on ports 80/443, ordinary teachers possess the required permission, and one-byte-below-limit uploads remain persistent with no cumulative quota, rate limiter, or paper-temp cleanup. No secrets are involved; the crossed boundary is from a teacher-authorized upload operation to availability of the shared application filesystem.

发生可能性
High - The path is direct, remotely exposed by the checked-in reverse-proxy configuration, requires only a normal teacher permission under the conservative auth-enabled assumption, uses an ordinary multipart upload, and needs no race or parser sophistication. An executable HTTP reproduction proved that two malformed uploads produced two persistent, byte-identical files. Repetition and finite disk capacity are required, but each request can add almost 50 MiB and no repository-level aggregate quota, rate limit, or cleanup interrupts accumulation. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The leak is persistent and cumulative at nearly 50 MiB per successful attack request. Exhausting the filesystem used by the shared application can prevent paper uploads and other file-backed scanning or grading operations and may also affect SQLite or logging when they share the volume. Availability during examination workflows is explicitly high value in the supplied threat model. The proven reach is one service and its filesystem, with no demonstrated confidentiality, integrity, fleet-wide, or code-execution effect.
假设条件
Production follows the supplied threat-model assumption that PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled; the conservative attack path therefore uses an authenticated ordinary teacher rather than relying on the repository's unauthenticated compatibility default.
The checked-in Nginx deployment configuration represents a supported public deployment path from ports 80/443 to the loopback Node service on port 5174.
The application filesystem has finite capacity and is not protected by an external per-user storage quota or out-of-repository stale-file cleanup mechanism.
Repeated paper-upload requests are permitted; no repository-level general API upload rate limit was identified.
Network access to POST /api/cards/:cardId/paper through the web service
Under authentication-enforced production assumptions, an ordinary teacher account retaining the default grade:write permission
Repeated multipart uploads with an allowed image filename extension and malformed content below Multer's per-file limit
Enough requests to consume the finite filesystem containing data/answer-card/papers/_tmp
控制措施
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, unauthenticated requests receive 401 and POST requests require grade:write.
Multer imposes a nominal 50 MiB per-file limit; validation demonstrated that an exact 50 MiB body was rejected and did not leave a partial temporary file.
The filename allowlist restricts accepted extensions, but it does not verify content signatures or parser validity.
The normal success path attempts to unlink the Multer temporary file after storePaperFile() returns.
safeId sanitizes card IDs used for paper directories, preventing this path from becoming a directory-traversal issue.
The existing scheduled cleanup covers expired scan-record files only, not papers/_tmp.
No cumulative upload quota, per-principal upload rate limit, or stale paper-temp cleanup is present in the reviewed repository.
盲点
The complete production server could not be launched in validation because the checkout lacked the installed expr-eval dependency; real token verification was therefore established statically, while the unchanged gate, route, Multer, Sharp, HTTP, and filesystem behavior were exercised dynamically.
Actual production disk capacity, free-space monitoring, filesystem quotas, container limits, and external reverse-proxy rate limits are not established by repository evidence.
The checked-in .codex-deploy-tmp Nginx and systemd files demonstrate a public deployment configuration but may not represent every installation.
The degree to which database and other services share the same filesystem depends on deployment and whether SQLite or remote MariaDB is used.
Manual operator deletion can recover space, but no automatic repository control prevents recurrence.
