## 12. Teacher permission rows are bypassed by every valid teacher role

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/2257767ad06881919166100ae73f557d?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Teacher permission rows are bypassed by every valid teacher role
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
992332c
7:57 PM Jul 4, 2026

操作者：Tim-Hanchuan-Yang

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduces a security bug. It adds an administrative permission matrix but appends its enforcement after unconditional returns for every supported teacher subtype. It also persists two capability flags that no server-side authorization check reads, creating ineffective security controls.

The commit introduces `teacher_permissions` rows with independent `can_view_scores`, `can_view_charts`, and `can_view_students` controls and an authenticated administrative API for setting them. However, `getVisibleExamIds()` returns before consulting those rows for every teacher role the account APIs permit: teachers with no subtype return at line 53, grade leaders at line 55, head teachers at lines 59-69, and subject teachers at lines 72-82. The new restriction logic beginning at line 85 is therefore reachable only for an invalid or legacy non-null subtype. Furthermore, that logic queries only `can_view_scores`; the chart and student controls are never consulted anywhere. Consequently, after an administrator creates a global or grade-specific denial, an authenticated teacher can still call analysis endpoints such as `/api/analysis/exams/:examId/overview` and `/students`. A grade leader or compatibility-mode plain teacher receives unrestricted exam access and can disclose scores, identities, rankings, and analytics across the school despite the explicit denial. Head and subject teachers can likewise bypass denials within their pre-existing class scope. Authentication and the generic `grade:read` gate remain in place, so exploitation requires a teacher account, but those controls do not enforce the newly configured administrative boundary. The implementation should calculate the existing role-based scope first and then intersect it with permission-row restrictions before returning. Capability-specific middleware must also enforce `can_view_charts` and `can_view_students` on the corresponding routes.

验证
 The schema and authenticated administrative API can persist explicit teacher denials for all three claimed capabilities.
 Every supported teacher persona returns from getVisibleExamIds before teacher_permissions is evaluated.
 Real analysis score/student routes rely on the bypassed control and reach repositories returning protected records.
 A focused authenticated HTTP reproduction showed access and disclosure after an administrator persisted the denial.
 Repository tracing confirmed there is no separate server-side enforcement of can_view_charts or can_view_students.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Every valid teacher subtype returns before the newly added permission query; the query also considers only `can_view_scores` and ignores the other two controls.
- Score analytics and identifiable student rankings rely on `requireExamAccess`, whose visibility calculation bypasses the configured restrictions.
- Defines the new grade-scoped score, chart, and student visibility controls that the server is expected to enforce.
- Allows an authorized administrator to persist explicit per-teacher permission settings, including denials.
- Shows that the only accepted non-null teacher roles are subject_teacher, head_teacher, and grade_leader, all of which return before permission enforcement.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts:50](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/992332c315ba5240d19044d07a10842a54d0e829/src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts#L50)

~~~~typescript
export function getVisibleExamIds(user: express.Request["user"]): number[] | null {
  if (!user || user.role_name === "admin") return null;
  if (user.role_name !== "teacher") return null;
  if (!user.teacher_role) return null; // plain teacher: all visible (back-compat)

  if (user.teacher_role === "grade_leader") return null;

  const db = getDatabase();

  if (user.teacher_role === "head_teacher") {
    const classIds = db.prepare(
      "SELECT class_id FROM teacher_classes WHERE teacher_id = ?"
    ).all(user.id).map((r: any) => r.class_id) as number[];
    if (classIds.length === 0) return [];
    const rows = db.prepare(
      `SELECT DISTINCT e.id FROM exams e
       JOIN classes c ON c.id = e.class_id
       WHERE e.class_id IN (${classIds.map(() => "?").join(",")})`
    ).all(...classIds) as Array<{ id: number }>;
    return rows.map((r) => r.id);
  }

  if (user.teacher_role === "subject_teacher") {
    if (!user.subject) return [];
    const classIds = db.prepare(
      "SELECT class_id FROM teacher_classes WHERE teacher_id = ? AND (subject = ? OR subject IS NULL)"
    ).all(user.id, user.subject).map((r: any) => r.class_id) as number[];
    if (classIds.length === 0) return [];
    const rows = db.prepare(
      `SELECT DISTINCT e.id FROM exams e
       WHERE e.subject = ? AND e.class_id IN (${classIds.map(() => "?").join(",")})`
    ).all(user.subject, ...classIds) as Array<{ id: number }>;
    return rows.map((r) => r.id);
  }

  // Check teacher_permissions for additional restrictions
  if (user.role_name === "teacher") {
    const db = getDatabase();
    if (hasTable(db, "teacher_permissions")) {
      const restrictions = db.prepare(
        "SELECT grade_id, can_view_scores FROM teacher_permissions WHERE teacher_id = ? AND can_view_scores = 0"
      ).all(user.id) as Array<{ grade_id: number | null }>;
      // If any restriction forbids all grades (grade_id = null), deny everything
      if (restrictions.some((r) => r.grade_id === null)) return [];
      // Filter out exams from restricted grades
      if (restrictions.length > 0) {
        const restrictedGrades = restrictions.map((r) => r.grade_id).filter(Boolean) as number[];
        const db = getDatabase();
        return (db.prepare(
          `SELECT DISTINCT e.id FROM exams e
           JOIN classes c ON c.id = e.class_id
           WHERE e.grade_id NOT IN (${restrictedGrades.map(() => "?").join(",")})`
        ).all(...restrictedGrades) as Array<{ id: number }>).map((r) => r.id);
      }
    }
  }

  return null;
~~~~

~~~~


攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: the impact is not self-only, the preconditions are plausible, the attacker is a lower-privileged teacher rather than an administrator or host operator, and the privilege delta is the bypass of an administrator-defined data-access boundary. Repository deployment artifacts support remote reach through Cloudflare Tunnel despite the loopback bind. Impact is high because broad identifiable student score and ranking data can be disclosed, while likelihood is high because exploitation is a single authenticated request and was reproduced through the real HTTP interface. Applying the required matrix, high impact plus high likelihood remains high because the issue does not satisfy critical criteria such as administrator takeover, remote code execution, complete database compromise, or scalable score corruption.

路径
Authenticated malicious or compromised teacher --supplies valid teacher token and selected examId--> Publicly tunnelled /api/analysis/exams/:examId endpoint --request reaches mounted analysis router--> Generic analysisGate requiring grade:read --teacher has default grade:read permission--> requireExamAccess and getVisibleExamIds --null or legacy-scope result is treated as authorization--> AnalysisRepository score and student queries --database result is serialized to the teacher--> Protected student identity, score, ranking, and analytics response

The product creates teacher_permissions rows containing independent score, chart, and student visibility controls and exposes an authenticated administrator API and UI for managing them. The analysis API is a production route mounted behind a generic grade:read gate. Every teacher receives that generic permission. For a per-exam request, requireExamAccess delegates to getVisibleExamIds. That function returns null for plain teachers and grade leaders and returns an older class/subject-derived list for head and subject teachers before execution can reach the new permission-row query. requireExamAccess interprets null or membership in the old list as authorization. The only server-side permission query reads can_view_scores, and repository-wide references show no authorization read of can_view_charts or can_view_students. The resulting request reaches AnalysisRepository, whose ranking query returns identifiable student numbers, names, component scores, totals, error statistics, and ranks. The supplied executable validation exercised the real authenticated HTTP path with PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE=1, persisted a denial through the admin API, and received HTTP 200 with sensitive student and overview data for all four supported teacher personas. Existing authentication, grade:read checking, and old head/subject scope are meaningful controls, but they do not enforce the newly configured administrative boundary. Loopback binding is also counterevidence to direct socket exposure, but the repository documents and scripts a public HTTPS Cloudflare tunnel to that loopback port.

发生可能性
High - The attack is a direct authenticated GET request using an attacker-controlled examId, with no race, parser exploit, or speculative pivot. Teachers are explicitly modeled as partially trusted attackers, every teacher has grade:read, and the documented deployment exposes the API remotely through a public HTTPS tunnel. The required permission denial is a normal use of the newly added administrative feature rather than an attacker-controlled privileged step. Authentication and the need for a teacher account limit the attacker population but do not make the path unlikely. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - A teacher can bypass an explicit administrator-configured denial and disclose educational records belonging to other students, including identities, student numbers, total and component scores, rankings, error indicators, and performance analytics. Plain teachers and grade leaders can receive unrestricted exam access, making school-wide disclosure plausible; head and subject teachers remain limited by their old class or subject scope. The issue is read-only and does not modify grades or compromise administrator identity, so it does not meet the threat model's critical criteria.
假设条件
Production authentication is enabled with PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE=1, as assumed by the supplied threat model and used by the executable validation.
The attacker possesses a valid account with the teacher role; no administrator credential or ability to modify permission rows is required.
An administrator has configured a global or grade-specific teacher_permissions denial for the attacking teacher through the product's permission-management workflow.
The selected exam contains graded student records and is within the teacher's legacy role scope; plain teachers and grade leaders receive unrestricted legacy scope.
Remote exposure is based on the repository's documented Cloudflare Tunnel deployment. A deployment that does not use the tunnel or another reverse proxy would have lower network exposure.
Valid authenticated teacher bearer token
Administrator-configured teacher_permissions denial
Attacker-selected examId containing protected analysis or student-score data
For head_teacher or subject_teacher, the exam must also be within the pre-existing class or subject scope
控制措施
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, the generic gate rejects unauthenticated requests and requires grade:read.
The administrative permission API itself requires authentication and USER_MANAGE.
Head teachers remain restricted to assigned classes under the old scope calculation.
Subject teachers remain restricted to assigned classes and their subject under the old scope calculation.
Students are separately denied ordinary analysis endpoints by requireExamAccess.
The Node server binds to 127.0.0.1, although the documented deployment intentionally publishes it through Cloudflare Tunnel.
No effective server-side enforcement exists for can_view_charts or can_view_students, and can_view_scores enforcement is unreachable for supported teacher states.
盲点
The supplied executable validation covered SQLite only; MariaDB behavior was not dynamically exercised.
Repository artifacts document public Cloudflare Tunnel exposure, but they do not prove that every deployment or the historical example URL is currently reachable.
The full population and number of exams available to a particular teacher depend on deployment data, teacher subtype, class assignments, subject assignments, and configured denials.
The exact intended distinctions among can_view_scores, can_view_charts, and can_view_students are not specified in a formal policy document, although the schema, admin API, UI labels, and middleware comment clearly represent them as restrictions.
The supplied validation used a minimal unrelated expr-eval loading stub; the affected authentication, permission, middleware, analysis route, and repository paths did not depend on that service.

## 13. Online review accepts forged questions and maximum scores

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/f45c24455e4c8191a59e9e62be86db0d?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

Online review accepts forged questions and maximum scores
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
615f738
3:27 PM Jul 4, 2026

操作者：xiaoxu66609

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This range introduced the vulnerable POST `/api/review/exams/:examId/block-crops/:cropId/submit` route and `ReviewService`. The new implementation passes structurally unvalidated request objects into an upsert, permits insertion of nonexistent questions, and uses attacker-controlled score metadata when recomputing authoritative totals and rankings.

A teacher with grade read/write permission and access to an exam can obtain a valid crop ID from the review queue and submit an arbitrary `scores` array. The route performs only an array/non-empty check before passing the objects to `submitReviewCropScores`. Although the service loads the crop and the answer card, it never verifies that each submitted `questionNumber` is listed in the crop's `question_numbers`, belongs to the crop's block, or even exists on the card. It also trusts the submitted `scoreType`. Most importantly, the client-provided `maxScore` takes precedence over the authoritative card value; for an unknown question, the submitted score itself becomes the maximum. For example, a payload containing `{"questionNumber":999999,"scoreType":"subjective","score":1000000,"maxScore":1000000}` inserts a new full-credit question. Arbitrary score types can also create multiple unique rows for the same question because `score_type` is part of the upsert conflict key. The service then sums every inserted row into the student's total and recomputes exam rankings. Repeating this for crop IDs returned by the queue enables scalable corruption across an accessible exam and can cross objective, subjective, and block boundaries. Validation must derive question membership, type, and maximum score exclusively from the selected crop and its authoritative card block, reject unknown or duplicate questions and non-finite values, and enforce any block-level grading scope before writing.

验证
 Confirm an authenticated, non-admin teacher with normal grade permissions and legitimate exam scope can reach the submit endpoint and obtain a valid crop ID.
 Confirm the request path accepts attacker-controlled questionNumber, scoreType, score, and maxScore without enforcing crop/card membership, type, bounds, duplicates, or finite numeric values.
 Confirm the service and database schema permit nonexistent questions and multiple rows for one question through attacker-selected score_type.
 Dynamically demonstrate through the real HTTP interface that fabricated rows alter authoritative student totals and exam rankings.
 Identify material preconditions and countercontrols, including exam/crop checks and audit records, and distinguish demonstrated integrity impact from untested availability/scalability claims.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The new route checks only that `scores` is a non-empty array, then forwards all attacker-controlled score fields directly to the service.
- The service verifies that the crop belongs to the exam and builds card-wide maximum-score metadata, but does not parse or enforce the crop's question list or block membership.
- Client-supplied `maxScore` and `scoreType` are trusted, unknown questions fall back to using the submitted score as their maximum, and the upsert inserts fabricated question-score rows.
- All resulting question-score rows are incorporated into the authoritative student total, followed by an exam-wide ranking recomputation.

~~~~typescript
  const crop = await db.get(
    "SELECT * FROM answer_block_crops WHERE id = ? AND exam_id = ?",
    params.cropId,
    params.examId
  ) as CropRow | undefined;
  if (!crop) throw new Error("作答切块不存在");
  if (!crop.student_id) throw new Error("该切块未关联学生，无法阅卷");

  const exam = await db.get("SELECT card_id FROM exams WHERE id = ?", params.examId) as { card_id: string | null } | undefined;
  if (!exam?.card_id) throw new Error("考试未关联答题卡");

  const cardRepo = new CardRepository();
  const card = await cardRepo.findById(exam.card_id);
  if (!card) throw new Error("答题卡不存在");

  const maxScoreByQuestion = new Map<number, number>();
  for (const block of card.bodyBlocks) {
    if (block.type === "objective") {
      for (const def of objectiveQuestionDefinitions(block)) {
        maxScoreByQuestion.set(def.questionNumber, Number(def.score ?? 0));
      }
    } else if (block.type === "subjective") {
      for (const question of block.questions ?? []) {
        const qNum = typeof question.number === "number" ? question.number : parseInt(String(question.number), 10);
        if (Number.isFinite(qNum)) maxScoreByQuestion.set(qNum, Number(question.score ?? 0));
      }
    }
  }
~~~~

  const now = new Date().toISOString();
  const nextStatus = params.status ?? "reviewed";
  const upsertCols = [
    "exam_id", "student_id", "question_number", "question_id", "block_id",
    "score", "max_score", "score_type", "manually_modified", "modified_by", "modified_at"
  ];
  const conflictCols = ["exam_id", "student_id", "question_number", "score_type"];
  const updateCols = ["score", "max_score", "manually_modified", "modified_by", "modified_at", "block_id"];
  const upsertSQL = buildUpsertSQL(db.dialect, "question_scores", upsertCols, conflictCols, updateCols);

  let totalScore = 0;
  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
    for (const item of params.scores) {
      const maxScore = item.maxScore ?? maxScoreByQuestion.get(item.questionNumber) ?? item.score;
      const clampedScore = Math.max(0, Math.min(maxScore, item.score));
      await tx.run(
        upsertSQL,
        params.examId,
        crop.student_id,
        item.questionNumber,
        null,
        crop.block_id,
        clampedScore,
        maxScore,
        item.scoreType,
        1,
        params.userId,
        now
      );

攻击路径分析

No hard suppression applies: the impact is not self-only, the preconditions are achievable in normal operation, and the attacker is an ordinary partially trusted teacher rather than an administrator, operator, developer, or host user. Although grade:write is a protected write privilege and teachers can intentionally edit existing grades, the demonstrated privilege delta is the ability to escape the selected crop's authoritative question and maximum-score boundary and insert fabricated canonical data. Network likelihood is high where the documented public HTTPS ingress is used, and the exploit was demonstrated through the real authenticated HTTP workflow. Impact is high because authoritative totals and peer rankings are corrupted; likelihood is high because exploitation requires only a crafted request and a queue-provided crop ID. Applying the required high-impact/high-likelihood matrix yields high rather than critical because the effect is restricted to exams visible to an authenticated grade writer, requires existing crops, is audited, and does not provide global takeover, broad confidentiality loss, or proven cross-exam corruption.

路径
Authenticated subject teacher with grade:write --uses valid bearer or cookie session--> Documented HTTPS ingress to Express API --GET for an accessible exam--> Review queue discloses valid crop ID --supplies returned crop ID with forged JSON--> Review submit route accepts attacker-controlled scores array --forwards objects after only a non-empty-array check--> ReviewService trusts questionNumber, scoreType, score, and maxScore --upserts unverified grading metadata--> question_scores receives fabricated or duplicate-type rows --all rows are summed and rankings recalculated--> student_scores totals and exam rankings are recomputed

Attacker story: an ordinary subject teacher with the default grade permissions opens the normal online-review workflow for an exam they can access, obtains a crop ID from the review queue, and replaces the browser-generated request with forged score objects. The route validates only that scores is a non-empty array. Although ReviewService verifies the crop-exam relationship and loads the answer card, it does not use the crop's stored question_numbers and does not constrain submissions to its card block. It accepts arbitrary scoreType and gives client maxScore precedence; for an unknown question it falls back to the submitted score as maxScore. Because score_type is part of the upsert conflict key, alternate types can also create parallel rows for an existing question. Every resulting row is summed into student_scores and rankings are recomputed. The supplied executable validation used authentication enforcement and a normal subject_teacher account, received HTTP 200, inserted question 999999 for 1,000,000 points plus a second question-1 row with an attacker-defined type, and changed the victim from total 5/rank 2 to total 1000505/rank 1. Existing controls materially narrow the path to authenticated grade writers and visible exams, require a real crop and card, and create audit records; they do not enforce the crop/question/type/maximum integrity boundary. The browser UI normally submits only current.questionNumbers and a derived type, but raw HTTP clients control the JSON and server-side validation cannot rely on that UI behavior. No secret disclosure or code-execution sink is involved.

发生可能性
High - Exploitation is low complexity through an ordinary JSON API request with no race or user interaction. Default teachers have grade:write, the normal queue supplies valid crop IDs, and the executable PoC succeeded with authentication enforcement enabled. Preconditions are limited to a teacher account, an accessible exam, and an existing student crop. Direct Node exposure is loopback-only, but the repository documents public HTTPS proxy deployment. Authentication and exam scoping prevent unauthenticated, student, and cross-exam exploitation, but do not make the in-scope teacher path unlikely. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The confirmed impact is high integrity loss in authoritative educational records. An attacker can add nonexistent questions, manufacture maximum scores, create parallel rows for one question, inflate or otherwise alter student totals, and change the rankings of the victim and peers. The queue makes repetition across crops in an accessible exam practical. The path does not disclose secrets, cross into other exams, execute code, or establish installation-wide administrative control, and audit records make the changes detectable.
假设条件
Production authentication is enabled as required by the supplied threat model, although the submission route independently requires an authenticated user with grade:write.
Remote reach depends on deployment through the repository-documented HTTPS Nginx reverse proxy; the Node process itself binds to 127.0.0.1:5174.
The attacker controls an ordinary teacher account with grade:read, grade:write, and visibility of the target exam.
The target exam is linked to an answer card and has at least one crop associated with a student.
An authenticated teacher account with grade:write
Visibility of the target exam under requireExamAccess
A valid crop ID associated with that exam and a student
An exam linked to a valid answer card
Network access to the HTTP API, directly or through the documented reverse proxy
控制措施
Bearer-token or cookie authentication is resolved by optionalAuth, and requirePermission rejects unauthenticated submissions.
The review route requires grade:write.
requireExamAccess limits scoped subject teachers to exams for their subject and assigned classes.
ReviewService requires the crop ID to belong to the supplied exam and requires an associated student.
ReviewService requires the exam to reference an existing answer card.
The score writes and student-total update run in a transaction.
Scores are nominally clamped, but the clamp is ineffective because the maximum is attacker-controlled or falls back to the attacker-provided score.
Writes are marked manually_modified and answer_overrides records the actor, making corruption auditable but not preventing it.
The Node server binds to loopback, although the deployment guide exposes it through an HTTPS reverse proxy.
盲点
The checkout contains deployment guidance rather than an environment-specific ingress manifest, so whether a particular installation is Internet-accessible cannot be proven statically.
The executable validation used SQLite. MariaDB has equivalent permissive columns and uniqueness semantics, but the PoC was not replayed against MariaDB.
Repetition across many crops and availability effects were not load-tested; the proven impact is one student's total and the corresponding exam rankings.
This commit does not establish a reviewer-assignment or claim-ownership model, so the report does not rely on crossing a designated-reviewer boundary.
A separate manual score-editing route allows grade writers to update existing score rows and limits the incremental ranking-corruption capability attributable only to this endpoint; fabricated questions, types, and maximum metadata remain unique demonstrated effects.
The analysis did not establish whether score changes automatically unpublish already published results at this exact endpoint.

<a id="结果-14"></a>

## 14. Ladder API bypasses exam scope and publication controls

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/48b9de7cd8788191a657d4c51234340b?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Ladder API bypasses exam scope and publication controls
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
b1ac782
4:29 PM Jun 28, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced: line 403 mounts the ladder router for the first time in this range, exposing pre-existing handlers that authenticate callers but omit object-scope and publication authorization. Before this commit those handlers were not registered with the Express application.

This commit makes the previously unreachable ladder router available at `/api/ladder`. The router applies only `authMiddleware`, so a student account with only `score:read`, or a teacher restricted to particular classes and subjects, can access it. The single-exam endpoint passes the attacker-controlled sequential exam ID directly to `getScoreTableData`; it does not invoke `requireExamAccess`, check the user's class or grade, require that a student participated, or verify that the exam is published/closed. The repository query similarly accepts exams in any status and reads every student's name, student number, class, grade, and score. The response returns those identifiers and scores for the top ten students. The group endpoint directly reads all member-exam scores for an arbitrary group and additionally returns per-subject scores, while the cross-exam endpoint accepts attacker-selected exam IDs without `validateExamIdsAccess`. The global ladder switch is enabled by default and is not an object-level authorization control. Consequently, a low-privilege student can enumerate exam/group IDs to obtain peer results, including draft or still-grading exams, and a scoped teacher can cross class, subject, and grade boundaries. The endpoints should enforce the appropriate publication/participation rules for students and `requireExamAccess` or `validateExamIdsAccess` for teachers before loading any scores.

验证
 Confirmed /api/ladder is mounted in production composition and protected only by general authentication plus a global feature switch.
 Confirmed attacker-controlled exam/group IDs reach score queries without role, object-scope, participation, or publication checks.
 Confirmed a normal low-privilege authenticated student can exercise the real HTTP router against seeded out-of-scope draft peer data.
 Confirmed responses disclose identifiable peer scores and both database schemas enable the ladder by default.
 Checked relevant countercontrols: authentication and manual global disablement work, but existing exam visibility middleware is not applied and output is not materially redacted.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The commit mounts `/api/ladder` after only optional identity parsing and outside the exam/analysis permission gates, making the vulnerable router reachable.
- The newly added MariaDB initialization enables the ladder by default, so the global switch does not mitigate the exposure on a normal deployment.
- The called repository loads all students' identifiers and scores for the supplied exam ID without checking exam status, caller participation, grade, class, or teacher visibility.
- The router requires authentication only. Its sole read-side guard is a global feature switch, which defaults open when the setting is absent and performs no role, exam-scope, participation, or publication check.
- An arbitrary exam ID is sent directly to the unrestricted score-table query and the resulting top-ten peer records are returned without `requireExamAccess` or a publication check.
- The group endpoint accepts any group ID and queries identifiable scores for all member exams without validating access to either the group or its exams.
- Cross-exam mode accepts caller-selected exam IDs and invokes the aggregate repository without `validateExamIdsAccess`, allowing scope bypass through another route.
- The response materializes sensitive peer fields including internal ID, student number, name, class, grade, raw score, assigned score, and ranks.

### [src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts:249](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/b1ac78210777fb697a37248af724053c81fe7c2b/src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts#L249)

~~~~typescript
  async getScoreTableData(examId: number, classId?: number, displayMode: "deviation" | "zscore" | "percentile" = "deviation"): Promise<any> {
    const exam = await this.db.get(`SELECT e.name, e.subject, ac.exam_date, e.assigned_formula FROM exams e LEFT JOIN answer_cards ac ON ac.id = e.card_id WHERE e.id = ?`, examId) as any;
    if (!exam) throw new Error("考试不存在");
    const hasAssigned = !!(exam.assigned_formula && exam.assigned_formula !== "");
    const allStudents = await this.db.all(`SELECT ss.student_id, u.student_number, u.name, ss.total_score, ss.objective_score, ss.subjective_score, ss.assigned_score, c.name as class_name, c.id as class_id, g.name as grade_name FROM student_scores ss JOIN users u ON u.id = ss.student_id LEFT JOIN class_students cs ON cs.student_id = ss.student_id LEFT JOIN classes c ON c.id = cs.class_id LEFT JOIN grades g ON g.id = c.grade_id WHERE ss.exam_id = ? ORDER BY ss.total_score DESC`, examId) as any[];
    if (allStudents.length === 0) return { examName: exam.name, subject: exam.subject, examDate: exam.exam_date, hasAssignedScore: hasAssigned, rows: [], totalCount: 0 };
    const gradeRanked = allStudents.map((s: any) => ({ ...s, gradeRank: 0, classRank: 0 }));
    competitionRank(gradeRanked, (r: any) => r.total_score, (r: any, rank: number) => { r.gradeRank = rank; });
    const cg = new Map<string, any[]>();
    for (const s of gradeRanked) { const k = s.class_name ?? "__unassigned__"; if (!cg.has(k)) cg.set(k, []); cg.get(k)!.push(s); }
    for (const g of cg.values()) competitionRank(g, (r: any) => r.total_score, (r: any, rank: number) => { r.classRank = rank; });
    let filtered = gradeRanked;
    if (classId === 0) filtered = gradeRanked.filter((s: any) => s.class_id == null);
    else if (classId !== undefined) filtered = gradeRanked.filter((s: any) => s.class_id === classId);
    const scores = filtered.map((s: any) => s.total_score);
    const mean = scores.reduce((a: number, b: number) => a + b, 0) / scores.length;
    const variance = scores.reduce((a: number, b: number) => a + (b - mean) ** 2, 0) / scores.length;
    const std = Math.sqrt(variance);
    const prevExam = await this.db.get(`SELECT e.id, e.name FROM exams e LEFT JOIN answer_cards ac ON ac.id = e.card_id WHERE e.subject = ? AND e.grade_id = (SELECT grade_id FROM exams WHERE id = ?) AND e.id != ? AND (ac.exam_date IS NULL OR ac.exam_date < (SELECT ac2.exam_date FROM exams e2 LEFT JOIN answer_cards ac2 ON ac2.id = e2.card_id WHERE e2.id = ?)) ORDER BY COALESCE(ac.exam_date, e.created_at) DESC LIMIT 1`, exam.subject, examId, examId, examId) as any;
    let prevRankMap = new Map<number, number>();
    if (prevExam) {
      const prevStudents = await this.db.all(`SELECT student_id, total_score FROM student_scores WHERE exam_id = ? ORDER BY total_score DESC`, prevExam.id) as any[];
      competitionRank(prevStudents, (r: any) => r.total_score, (r: any, rank: number) => prevRankMap.set(r.student_id, rank));
    }
    const rows = filtered.map((s: any) => {
      const prevRank = prevRankMap.get(s.student_id) ?? null;
      const rankChange = prevRank != null ? prevRank - s.gradeRank : null;
      let dv: number | null = null;
      if (displayMode === "deviation") dv = std > 0 ? Math.round((50 + 10 * (s.total_score - mean) / std) * 10) / 10 : 50;
      else if (displayMode === "zscore") dv = std > 0 ? Math.round(((s.total_score - mean) / std) * 100) / 100 : 0;
      else if (displayMode === "percentile") dv = Math.round((1 - (s.gradeRank - 1) / allStudents.length) * 1000) / 10;
      return { studentId: s.student_id, studentNumber: s.student_number, studentName: s.name, className: s.class_name ?? "未知班级", classId: s.class_id, gradeName: s.grade_name ?? null, totalScore: s.total_score, assignedScore: s.assigned_score, gradeRank: s.gradeRank, classRank: s.classRank ?? 0, rankChange, prevRank, prevExamName: prevExam?.name ?? null, displayValue: dv, objectiveScore: s.objective_score, subjectiveScore: s.subjective_score };
    });
    if (classId !== undefined && classId !== 0) rows.sort((a, b) => a.classRank - b.classRank);
    else rows.sort((a, b) => a.gradeRank - b.gradeRank);
    return { examName: exam.name, subject: exam.subject, examDate: exam.exam_date, hasAssignedScore: hasAssigned, rows, totalCount: rows.length };
~~~~

~~~~typescript
const router = express.Router();
router.use(authMiddleware);

// ── 天梯开关 ──

async function isLadderEnabled(): Promise<boolean> {
  const db = getMysqlDb();
  const row = await db.get<{ value: string }>("SELECT value FROM system_settings WHERE `key` = ?", "ladder_enabled");
  return row ? row.value === "1" : true;
}

/** 检查天梯是否开放，管理员始终可以预览 */
async function checkLadderOpen(req: Request, res: Response): Promise<boolean> {
  if (await isLadderEnabled()) return true;
  if (req.user?.role_name === "admin") return true;
  res.status(403).json({ message: "成绩天梯暂未开放" });
  return false;
}
~~~~

### [src/server/routes/ladder.ts:67](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/b1ac78210777fb697a37248af724053c81fe7c2b/src/server/routes/ladder.ts#L67)

~~~~typescript
router.get("/exams/:examId", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  if (!(await checkLadderOpen(req, res))) return;
  try {
    const examId = Number(req.params.examId);
    if (!Number.isFinite(examId)) {
      res.status(400).json({ message: "无效的考试 ID" });
      return;
    }

    const analysisRepo = new AnalysisRepository();
    const scoreTable = await analysisRepo.getScoreTableData(examId, undefined, "percentile");

    if (!scoreTable || scoreTable.rows.length === 0) {
      const resp: LadderResponse = {
        scope: "single",
        scopeName: scoreTable?.examName ?? "",
        studentCount: 0,
        myRank: null,
        myScore: null,
        rows: [],
      };
      res.json(resp);
      return;
    }

    const { top10, myRank, myScore } = LadderService.fromScoreTableRows(
      scoreTable.rows,
      scoreTable.totalCount,
      req.user!.id,
    );

    const resp: LadderResponse = {
      scope: "single",
      scopeName: scoreTable.examName,
      studentCount: scoreTable.totalCount,
      myRank,
      myScore,
      rows: top10,
    };
    res.json(resp);
~~~~

router.get("/exam-groups/:groupId", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  if (!(await checkLadderOpen(req, res))) return;
  try {
    const db = getMysqlDb();
    const groupId = Number(req.params.groupId);
    if (!Number.isFinite(groupId)) {
      res.status(400).json({ message: "无效的考试组 ID" });
      return;
    }

    const group = await db.get<{ name: string; total_score_mode: string }>(
      `SELECT name, total_score_mode FROM exam_groups WHERE id = ?`,
      groupId,
    );
    if (!group) {
      res.status(404).json({ message: "大考不存在" });
      return;
    }

    const members = await db.all<{ exam_id: number; subject: string | null }>(
      `SELECT egm.exam_id, e.subject
         FROM exam_group_members egm
         JOIN exams e ON e.id = egm.exam_id
         WHERE egm.group_id = ?
         ORDER BY egm.sort_order, egm.id`,
      groupId,
    );

    if (members.length === 0) {
      const resp: LadderResponse = {
        scope: "group",
        scopeName: group.name,
        studentCount: 0,
        myRank: null,
        myScore: null,
        rows: [],
      };
      res.json(resp);
      return;
    }

    const memberIds = members.map((m) => m.exam_id);

    const allScores = await db.all<{
      student_id: number;
      exam_id: number;
      total_score: number;
      assigned_score: number | null;
      student_number: string;
      name: string;
      class_name: string | null;
      class_id: number | null;
      grade_name: string | null;
    }>(
      `SELECT ss.student_id, ss.exam_id, ss.total_score, ss.assigned_score,
                u.student_number, u.name,
                c.name as class_name, c.id as class_id,
                g.name as grade_name
         FROM student_scores ss
         JOIN users u ON u.id = ss.student_id
         LEFT JOIN class_students cs ON cs.student_id = ss.student_id
         LEFT JOIN classes c ON c.id = cs.class_id
         LEFT JOIN grades g ON g.id = c.grade_id
         WHERE ss.exam_id IN (${memberIds.map(() => "?").join(",")})`,
      ...memberIds,
    );

router.get("/cross-exam", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  if (!(await checkLadderOpen(req, res))) return;
  try {
    const { mode, examIds, groupId, startDate, endDate } = req.query;

    const request: any = { mode: mode || "week" };
    if (mode === "selected" && examIds) {
      request.examIds = String(examIds)
        .split(",")
        .map((s) => Number(s.trim()))
        .filter((n) => Number.isFinite(n));
    }
    if (mode === "group" && groupId) {
      request.groupId = Number(groupId);
    }
    if (startDate) request.startDate = String(startDate);
    if (endDate) request.endDate = String(endDate);

    const analysisRepo = new AnalysisRepository();
    const crossExamData = await analysisRepo.getCrossExamTotal(request);

### [src/server/services/LadderService.ts:29](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/b1ac78210777fb697a37248af724053c81fe7c2b/src/server/services/LadderService.ts#L29)

~~~~typescript
  static fromScoreTableRows(
    rows: ScoreTableRow[],
    totalCount: number,
    currentStudentId?: number,
  ): { top10: LadderRow[]; myRank: number | null; myScore: number | null } {
    const top10: LadderRow[] = rows.slice(0, 10).map((r) => ({
      rank: r.rank,
      studentId: r.studentId,
      studentNumber: r.studentNumber,
      studentName: r.studentName,
      className: r.className,
      classId: r.classId,
      gradeName: r.gradeName ?? null,
      totalScore: r.totalScore,
      assignedScore: r.assignedScore,
      classRank: r.classRank,
      rankTrend: LadderService.getRankTrend(r.rankChange),
      rankChange: r.rankChange,
      prevRank: r.prevRank,
      percentile: LadderService.percentile(r.rank, totalCount),
    }));
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Hard-suppression checks do not apply: the impact is on other students rather than the attacker alone; the precondition is an ordinary student account rather than administrator, operator, developer, physical, or protected-write access; and the repository documents a realistic remote product workflow. Impact is high because identifiable peer records and draft results cross class and participation boundaries, with analogous group and cross-exam exposure. Likelihood is high because the endpoint is mounted in production composition, the feature defaults on, exploitation requires one authenticated request with a numeric ID, and an executable PoC demonstrated the effect. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact plus high likelihood yields critical only where the critical criteria demand immediate attention; those criteria are not met because this is a confidentiality breach rather than administrator takeover, remote code execution, complete database extraction, or scalable score corruption. The final adjusted criticality therefore remains high.

路径
Authenticated low-privilege student or scoped teacher --Uses a valid ordinary account over the normal web/API channel--> Documented public Cloudflare/reverse-proxy ingress to 127.0.0.1:5174 --Express mounts ladderRoutes at /api/ladder--> GET /api/ladder/exams/:examId with attacker-selected numeric ID --Supplies an arbitrary exam ID--> authMiddleware plus global ladder_enabled flag only --No role, participation, class, subject, publication, or exam-visibility authorization--> AnalysisRepository unrestricted score-table query --Loads and serializes peer identity and score columns--> Cross-class draft peer identities, scores, and rankings

Attacker story: a student logs in normally, enumerates or obtains a numeric exam ID, and requests GET /api/ladder/exams/:examId. Commit b1ac782 mounts the previously unreachable ladder router at /api/ladder. The router authenticates the request but performs no object-level authorization and sends the supplied ID to an unrestricted repository query. That query reads all score rows and associated student identity, class, and grade information, and LadderService returns the top ten. The executable focused HTTP PoC used the repository's real router, authentication service, schema, migrations, repository, and response service. An unauthenticated request received 401, while an ordinary student with only score:read, no target participation, and no own score received 200 with two other-class students' identities and raw and assigned scores from a draft exam. The group and cross-exam routes exhibit the same omission in source. Counterevidence consists of the loopback listener, mandatory authentication, administrator-controlled feature flag, and top-ten cap. It does not defeat reportability because repository-supported deployment exposes the service through a public tunnel or reverse proxy, the feature is enabled by default, authenticated students are the relevant threat actor, and the missing authorization crosses class, participation, teacher-scope, and draft-result boundaries.

发生可能性
High - Exploitation is a single normal authenticated GET request by an expected low-privilege product user. Numeric IDs are attacker-controlled, the feature is enabled by default in both supported databases, no object-level check intervenes, and the documented deployment exposes the API through public HTTPS ingress. The executable PoC confirms the route returns sensitive cross-class draft data under enforced authentication. Authentication, the possibility of an administrator disabling the feature, and uncertainty about an individual deployment's ingress prevent treating exploitation as unconditional, but the preconditions remain realistic and routine. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The flaw exposes identifiable educational records belonging to other students, including names, student numbers, class and grade membership, raw and assigned scores, ranks, and in group mode subject-level scores. The demonstrated target was a draft exam in another class and the attacker had not participated. Arbitrary exam, group, and cross-exam selection makes repeated disclosure possible across the installation. Under the supplied threat model, student bypass of publication controls and broad disclosure of unpublished results is a high-impact confidentiality violation, although it does not modify grades or compromise administrator identity.
假设条件
The deployment follows a repository-supported production pattern in which the loopback Express service is exposed through Cloudflare Tunnel, Nginx, or another reverse proxy; the exact deployed ingress is not present in the checkout.
The attacker has a valid ordinary student or scoped-teacher session, as expected for the normal school web workflow.
A target exam or exam group contains score rows, and the attacker can learn or guess its numeric identifier.
The global ladder feature remains enabled, which is the default in both SQLite and MariaDB initialization.
A valid authenticated low-privilege account
The ladder feature is enabled
A numeric target exam or group identifier
The target contains score records
Network access to the Project-X web/API service
控制措施
authMiddleware rejects requests without a valid bearer token, cookie token, or supported query token.
The ladder_enabled global feature flag can be disabled by an administrator.
Exam and group path parameters are required to parse as finite numbers.
Single-exam and group responses are bounded to the top ten ranked rows.
The Node listener binds to 127.0.0.1, although repository deployment workflows expose it through a tunnel or reverse proxy.
requireExamAccess and validateExamIdsAccess exist elsewhere but are not applied to ladder routes.
No endpoint-specific rate limit, publication-state gate, participation check, class/grade restriction, or teacher-scope authorization protects ladder reads.
盲点
The exact ingress and audience of any specific deployed installation cannot be established from a repository checkout; public reachability is based on the repository-supported Cloudflare/reverse-proxy deployment workflow.
The executable validation mounted the exact production ladder router and dependencies over HTTP but could not import the complete createApp composition because the checked-out node_modules lacked the declared expr-eval package. Source composition independently establishes the production mount.
Only the single-exam endpoint was dynamically exercised. Group and cross-exam variants were source-traced but not separately executed.
The institution's intended policy for displaying leaderboard identities is not encoded in a separate policy artifact. This uncertainty does not explain access to a draft exam in another class by a nonparticipant.
The practical speed of ID enumeration and total number of populated exams depend on deployment data, though neither is required to establish the demonstrated cross-boundary disclosure.

## 15. Unbounded auto-backups let teachers exhaust server disk

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/a0ec603e3cd481919a9c0821e4bae28a?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Unbounded auto-backups let teachers exhaust server disk
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
9c510aa
3:42 PM Jun 28, 2026

操作者：Tim-Hanchuan-Yang

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduced the vulnerability by adding autoBackupOnExamClose() and invoking it unconditionally after every grading persistence operation. The other changed file adds Zod validation to exam-group creation and does not introduce a comparable security issue.

The new autoBackupOnExamClose() function writes a uniquely named full database copy under data/answer-card/backups each time persistGradingResults() completes. The persistence function only checks that the supplied exam exists; it does not require an open-to-closed state transition, verify that any student score was persisted, or prevent an already closed exam from being processed repeatedly. The backups are also outside the existing cleanup job's scope and have no count, age, size, or free-space limit. In authentication-enforced deployments, the affected grading route is available to accounts with grade:write, which teachers receive by default. An authenticated teacher can repeatedly POST to /api/cards/:cardId/grading with an accessible card, an existing examId, and one small or malformed file. Recognition errors are converted into failed grading rows rather than aborting, and persistence still creates a scan batch, closes the exam, and starts a database copy. Requests spaced by at least one millisecond produce distinct backup names. This permits persistent disk exhaustion and concurrent copy I/O amplification proportional to the installation's entire database size, potentially preventing database writes and disrupting scanning or grading. The backup should be created only on an atomic first transition to closed, use SQLite's online backup/VACUUM mechanism, enforce retention and storage limits, and be rate-limited or moved to an administrator-controlled background job.

验证
 Production grading HTTP route is reachable by a default teacher/grade:write principal and accepts attacker-controlled examId plus a minimal upload.
 A failed/malformed recognition result still reaches persistence for an existing exam without requiring a first open-to-closed transition or any persisted score.
 Each repeated persistence invocation schedules a distinct full SQLite main-database-file copy in a persistent directory.
 No retention, quota, deduplication, cleanup, or free-space control bounds backup growth, and copy size scales with the database file.
 Focused realistic execution demonstrated three repeated requests accumulating three equal-size backups despite zero student scores.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Creates a new timestamped full database copy on every call, with no retention, quota, deduplication, or free-space control.
- Persistence requires only that the exam exists and does not ensure this is its first legitimate transition to the closed state.
- The backup is triggered after every persistence run even when all rows lack student IDs and zero scores were persisted.
- POST requests under /api/cards, including the grading endpoint, require grade:write rather than an administrator-only maintenance permission.
- The teacher-reachable grading endpoint accepts uploaded files and a caller-supplied examId.
- Recognition failures are converted to rows, and any nonempty examId still schedules persistence—and consequently a backup—after the response.

~~~~typescript
async function autoBackupOnExamClose(examId: number): Promise<void> {
  const backupDir = path.join(dataDir, "backups");
  await mkdir(backupDir, { recursive: true });
  const ts = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, "-");
  const src = path.join(dataDir, "..", "projectx.db");
  const dst = path.join(backupDir, `projectx_exam${examId}_${ts}.db`);
  try {
    const { copyFile } = await import("node:fs/promises");
    if (existsSync(src)) {
      await copyFile(src, dst);
      console.log(`[AutoBackup] exam ${examId} → ${path.basename(dst)}`);
~~~~

攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: the effect is shared rather than self-only; the attacker is a lower-privileged teacher rather than an administrator, operator, developer, or local host user; and the prerequisites were exercised through the normal authenticated HTTP workflow. Repository documentation supports remote reachability through Cloudflare Tunnel, while the supplied executable PoC confirms repeatable full-database storage growth. Impact is high because eventual disk exhaustion can stop database writes and grading, and likelihood is high because teachers receive the needed permission by default and no rate or retention control intervenes. Applying the required matrix, high impact plus high likelihood is critical only when the separate critical criteria are met; this path affects one school service's availability and does not establish RCE, administrator takeover, database exfiltration, or silent broad score corruption. Therefore the mechanically adjusted and reportable severity remains high.

路径
Remote authenticated teacher --Sends authenticated HTTP requests--> Public Cloudflare Tunnel or reverse proxy to 127.0.0.1:5174 --Forwards to the Express product service--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading guarded only by grade:write --Processes attacker-controlled multipart file--> Recognition result or caught recognition failure --Failure is converted to a row and does not abort persistence--> Unconditional persistGradingResults state reset and scan batch --Closes the exam and schedules backup regardless of persisted score count--> Timestamped copyFile of data/projectx.db --Creates one full-size file per distinct timestamp without cleanup--> Unbounded backup accumulation and shared-service outage

An ordinary teacher can use the normal grading API to make the server repeatedly duplicate its full SQLite database. POST requests under /api/cards require grade:write, but that permission is part of the default teacher role. The grading handler accepts an attacker-controlled examId and multipart files, catches recognition exceptions as failed rows, sends a successful response, and then invokes persistence whenever examId is present. Persistence checks only that the exam exists; it does not require a first open-to-closed transition or any successfully persisted score. It therefore creates a scan batch, marks the exam closed, and schedules autoBackupOnExamClose even when all rows lack student IDs. The backup function uses a millisecond timestamp and copyFile without retention or capacity controls. The supplied executable validation used a real authenticated teacher and three normal HTTP requests; all three had failed recognition and zero saved scores but produced three separate 4,677,632-byte files, each equal to the source database size. The strongest counterevidence is that the Node listener is loopback-only, but repository deployment documentation and start-server.sh explicitly publish it using Cloudflare Tunnel, so loopback binding is not dispositive. MariaDB mode and customized database paths may cause the hard-coded SQLite source lookup to miss, which confines the confirmed issue to default SQLite deployments rather than disproving it. Authentication, the 20 MiB per-file limit, card existence checks, and same-millisecond filename collision also do not prevent the attack: teachers possess the required permission, tiny files suffice, teachers can use their own card/exam, and modestly spaced requests receive unique filenames. The validation environment lacked the native recognizer, but this is not a necessary exploit precondition because the source invokes persistence after either successful recognition or any caught recognition error. No repository cleanup, quota, free-space check, deduplication, or general request rate limit covers these backup files. The backups remain server-side, so this path establishes availability impact rather than unauthorized backup disclosure.

发生可能性
High - The path is reachable through a documented remote product deployment by an ordinary authenticated teacher with default permissions. Preconditions are routine, the attacker can create or use its own card and exam, no per-exam first-close check or rate limit exists, and an executable validation produced three full-size backups from three sequential HTTP requests with zero scores. SQLite is the documented default database mode. Likelihood is reduced only for MariaDB deployments, customized database paths, customized teacher permissions, or untracked external quotas and throttling. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - Each trigger consumes storage equal to the full SQLite database and may overlap copy I/O with later requests. Accumulation is persistent and outside cleanup. Exhausting the shared filesystem can prevent SQLite writes and disrupt scanning, grading, and other school users. The supplied validation directly proved linear full-file accumulation, although it intentionally did not fill the disk. No data-exfiltration or code-execution impact is established.
假设条件
The affected deployment uses the repository's default SQLite mode with the active database at data/projectx.db.
PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled as assumed for production, and the attacker's teacher role retains its default grade:write permission.
The attacker can reach the documented public Cloudflare Tunnel or another operator-configured reverse proxy to the loopback-bound service.
No deployment-external filesystem quota, reverse-proxy rate limit, or backup retention job absent from the repository prevents accumulation.
An authenticated account with grade:write; ordinary teachers receive this permission by default
An existing answer card and exam; a teacher's default card:write and exam:write permissions permit creating its own prerequisites
At least one multipart file and a caller-supplied existing examId
SQLite local mode with the active source database at the default data/projectx.db path
Repeated requests with distinct millisecond timestamps and sufficient time to consume available storage
控制措施
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, cardGate rejects unauthenticated callers and requires grade:write for POST requests.
The default teacher role nevertheless has grade:write, so the permission gate does not separate grading authority from backup or host-storage authority.
The handler requires an existing card, a nonempty upload, and an existing exam before persistence proceeds.
Multer limits each uploaded recognition file to 20 MiB, but the attack works with one small file and backup growth is based on database size rather than upload size.
The server binds directly to 127.0.0.1, while repository deployment scripts and documentation expose it through a public Cloudflare Tunnel.
The cleanup scheduler runs every 24 hours for scan and recognition artifacts, but generated database backups are outside its scope.
The hard-coded source path confines confirmed accumulation to default SQLite deployments; MariaDB mode normally has no matching active SQLite source.
盲点
Static repository evidence cannot establish the actual deployment's database mode, PROJECTX_DB_PATH, ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR, role customizations, disk capacity, filesystem quotas, or reverse-proxy throttling.
The bounded validation proved three equal-sized backups but intentionally did not drive the host to ENOSPC; final outage and concurrent I/O degradation are inferred from demonstrated linear accumulation.
The validation environment lacked the native recognizer, although source flow shows that both successful recognition and caught recognition errors reach persistence.
No repository-backed route was established for the attacker to download these generated backups; confidentiality impact is not claimed.
A custom external cleanup or monitoring process outside the repository could reduce persistence or detection time.

## 16. Ladder endpoints expose arbitrary students' scores

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/906b3e28aa80819184af341b10ab2378?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Ladder endpoints expose arbitrary students' scores
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
cf09692
9:26 AM Jun 28, 2026

操作者：NaOH1g

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduced the vulnerable `/api/ladder` router and mounted it outside the established analysis authorization path. Existing analysis score-table routes use the analysis permission gate and `requireExamAccess`, while the new routes require only a valid session and therefore bypass those controls.

All ladder routes are protected only by `authMiddleware`; the feature-enabled check is not an object-scope authorization control. The router is mounted before the existing exam and analysis RBAC gates, so those gates do not apply. Consequently, any authenticated student can request an arbitrary sequential exam ID with `GET /api/ladder/exams/:examId`, even when the student did not participate and the exam belongs to another grade or is still being graded. That endpoint calls `getScoreTableData(examId, undefined, ...)`, whose undefined class scope selects every score for the exam, and returns the top ten students' IDs, student numbers, real names, classes, grades, exact scores, and ranks. The group endpoint similarly queries every score for every member exam without checking the group's grade or the caller's relationship to it, and additionally returns exact per-subject scores. The cross-exam endpoint accepts caller-selected exam IDs, group IDs, or arbitrary date ranges and invokes `getCrossExamTotal` without the `visibleExamIds` option used by the existing analysis route. This permits scalable enumeration across exams and disclosure of unpublished or out-of-scope educational records. Even if showing a same-grade leaderboard is intended, the API must derive and enforce the student's grade, require participation and an appropriate released/closed state, and apply the existing teacher exam visibility rules and `grade:read` permission for non-student access.

验证
 Confirm /api/ladder is production-mounted and reachable with a normal authenticated student session when the ladder is enabled.
 Confirm no role, grade:read, exam-scope, participation, or release-state control applies between the mount and the three data handlers; verify the feature switch as the closest control.
 Confirm caller-selected exam, group, and date/list identifiers reach broad score queries without authorized visible-exam, class, or grade constraints.
 Confirm responses expose other-grade students' identifiable exact scores and ranks from a still-grading exam.
 Reproduce through focused local HTTP requests using the real affected routers, authentication/session flow, permission middleware, repository, and isolated database, with negative controls.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The ladder router is mounted before the exam and analysis RBAC gates, so it does not inherit `grade:read`, `exam:read`, or the associated scoped authorization.
- With the undefined class scope supplied by the ladder route, the repository loads every student's identifiable score record for the selected exam.
- The router requires authentication, but its only additional data-access check is the global feature switch; it does not enforce role, permission, grade, class, participation, exam visibility, or release state.
- An arbitrary caller-supplied exam ID is passed to the full score-table query without authorization, and the resulting top-ten peer records are returned.
- The group leaderboard directly selects all students' scores and identifying information for every member exam, without filtering by the caller's grade or authorized exams.
- The group response exposes student IDs, student numbers, names, class and grade membership, exact totals, ranks, and per-subject scores.
- The cross-exam endpoint accepts attacker-controlled exam IDs, group IDs, and date ranges, then queries and returns rankings without supplying an authorized visible-exam set.

### [src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts:249](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/cf09692d1df1d604cb51ae509954ca28ce2b8293/src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts#L249)

~~~~typescript
  async getScoreTableData(examId: number, classId?: number, displayMode: "deviation" | "zscore" | "percentile" = "deviation"): Promise<any> {
    const exam = await this.db.get(`SELECT e.name, e.subject, ac.exam_date, e.assigned_formula FROM exams e LEFT JOIN answer_cards ac ON ac.id = e.card_id WHERE e.id = ?`, examId) as any;
    if (!exam) throw new Error("考试不存在");
    const hasAssigned = !!(exam.assigned_formula && exam.assigned_formula !== "");
    const allStudents = await this.db.all(`SELECT ss.student_id, u.student_number, u.name, ss.total_score, ss.objective_score, ss.subjective_score, ss.assigned_score, c.name as class_name, c.id as class_id, g.name as grade_name FROM student_scores ss JOIN users u ON u.id = ss.student_id LEFT JOIN class_students cs ON cs.student_id = ss.student_id LEFT JOIN classes c ON c.id = cs.class_id LEFT JOIN grades g ON g.id = c.grade_id WHERE ss.exam_id = ? ORDER BY ss.total_score DESC`, examId) as any[];
    if (allStudents.length === 0) return { examName: exam.name, subject: exam.subject, examDate: exam.exam_date, hasAssignedScore: hasAssigned, rows: [], totalCount: 0 };
    const gradeRanked = allStudents.map((s: any) => ({ ...s, gradeRank: 0, classRank: 0 }));
    competitionRank(gradeRanked, (r: any) => r.total_score, (r: any, rank: number) => { r.gradeRank = rank; });
    const cg = new Map<string, any[]>();
    for (const s of gradeRanked) { const k = s.class_name ?? "__unassigned__"; if (!cg.has(k)) cg.set(k, []); cg.get(k)!.push(s); }
    for (const g of cg.values()) competitionRank(g, (r: any) => r.total_score, (r: any, rank: number) => { r.classRank = rank; });
    let filtered = gradeRanked;
    if (classId === 0) filtered = gradeRanked.filter((s: any) => s.class_id == null);
    else if (classId !== undefined) filtered = gradeRanked.filter((s: any) => s.class_id === classId);
~~~~

const router = express.Router();
router.use(authMiddleware);

// ── 天梯开关 ──

function isLadderEnabled(): boolean {
  const db = getDatabase();
  const row = db.prepare("SELECT value FROM system_settings WHERE key = ?").get("ladder_enabled") as { value: string } | undefined;
  return row?.value === "1";
}

/** 检查天梯是否开放，管理员始终可以预览 */
function checkLadderOpen(req: Request, res: Response): boolean {
  if (isLadderEnabled()) return true;
  if (req.user?.role_name === "admin") return true;
  res.status(403).json({ message: "成绩天梯暂未开放" });
  return false;

    if (!Number.isFinite(examId)) {
      res.status(400).json({ message: "无效的考试 ID" });
      return;

    const scoreTable = await analysisRepo.getScoreTableData(examId, undefined, "percentile");

    if (!scoreTable || scoreTable.rows.length === 0) {
      const resp: LadderResponse = {
        scope: "single",
        scopeName: scoreTable?.examName ?? "",
        studentCount: 0,
        myRank: null,
        myScore: null,
        rows: [],

      return;

    const { top10, myRank, myScore } = LadderService.fromScoreTableRows(
      scoreTable.rows,
      scoreTable.totalCount,
      req.user!.id,
    );

    const resp: LadderResponse = {
      scope: "single",
      scopeName: scoreTable.examName,
      studentCount: scoreTable.totalCount,
      myRank,
      myScore,
      rows: top10,
    };
    res.json(resp);

攻击路径分析

No hard suppression applies: impact is not self-only, preconditions are plausible, the attacker is a lower-privileged student rather than an operator or administrator, and the flaw is in a real runtime product workflow. Impact is high because the verified boundary crossing discloses identifiable, exact, and potentially unpublished academic results across grades, with scalable selection of exams and date ranges. Likelihood is high because a valid student account, a default-enabled feature, and simple remote GET requests are sufficient. Applying the required matrix gives high impact plus high likelihood; critical is not warranted because the result is confidentiality-only, each response is top-ten bounded, and there is no administrator takeover, code execution, complete database extraction, or score-integrity compromise. The original high severity is therefore retained.

路径
Authenticated student with only score:read --supplies examId, groupId, examIds, or dates--> Caller-controlled ladder exam, group, or cross-exam request --valid session satisfies authMiddleware; default-enabled flag satisfies checkLadderOpen--> Authentication and feature flag pass, but analysis RBAC and exam-scope checks are absent --route calls repository or direct SQL without authorized grade/exam scope--> Unscoped score queries join student identity, class, grade, and scores --identity and exact score fields are serialized in the leaderboard response--> Top-ten identifiable educational records from unauthorized exams returned to attacker

Attacker story: a normal student signs in, guesses or selects an integer exam or group ID, and requests a ladder endpoint. The router accepts the valid session and, because the ladder is enabled by default, queries all scores for the selected scope. It never verifies that the student participated, belongs to the target grade, may see the exam, or that grading has finished. The supplied executable HTTP validation used the repository's real routers, middleware, repositories, and SQLite schema and showed a nonparticipating student in Attacker Grade receiving HTTP 200 responses containing two Victim Grade students' identities and exact scores from an exam in grading status through all three interfaces. Unauthenticated, feature-disabled, self-score, and grade:read-protected negative controls correctly failed. The strongest counterevidence is that the test guide intentionally defines a student-facing grade top-ten feature and permits a nonparticipant to see no personal rank; this supports same-grade leaderboard intent but not unrestricted cross-grade or unreleased-exam disclosure. Responses are limited to ten leaders per requested scope, but arbitrary IDs, lists, groups, and date ranges make repeated disclosure scalable. This is therefore a real confidentiality authorization vulnerability rather than a ranking correctness issue.

发生可能性
High - Exploitation requires only a normal student session and ordinary HTTP GET requests. The feature is enabled by default, identifiers are caller-supplied integers, and no victim interaction or race is needed. The executable validation reproduced all three variants and their security effect, with negative controls confirming that the result was caused by the missing authorization rather than disabled authentication. Likelihood is high for production deployments exposed through the proxy or tunnel assumed by the supplied threat model; the loopback-only direct bind and absence of an ingress manifest are residual deployment uncertainties. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The issue exposes identifiable educational records—real names, student numbers, internal IDs, class and grade membership, exact scores, and ranks—across grade and publication boundaries. Group mode also exposes exact per-subject results. Caller-selected IDs and date ranges permit repeated collection across exams. This matches the supplied threat model's high-impact case of students obtaining broad unpublished results, although it does not modify grades, take over accounts, or expose the complete database in one request.
假设条件
The attacker has a valid ordinary student account and bearer token or authentication cookie; no teacher or administrator privileges are required.
The ladder feature is enabled. Migration version 9 enables it by default, although an administrator can disable it.
At least one target exam or exam group contains score records, and the attacker can select or enumerate its integer ID or choose a date range containing it.
Remote network scope assumes the production deployment described by the supplied threat model, where a reverse proxy or Cloudflare tunnel exposes the loopback-bound service. The repository contains no deployment manifest proving the exact ingress.
Authentication enforcement remains enabled in production as required by the supplied threat model.
Valid low-privilege student session
Ladder feature enabled
Target exam or group has score records
Knowledge or enumeration of an exam ID, group ID, selected exam list, or date range
控制措施
authMiddleware rejects missing or invalid sessions
The ladder_enabled feature flag can block non-admin access
Changing the feature flag is administrator-only
Responses are limited to the top ten students per requested scope
Established analysis routes use grade:read and exam visibility checks, but these controls do not wrap /api/ladder
No role, grade, class, participation, exam-visibility, or release-state authorization protects ladder data routes
No general API rate limit limits repeated ID or date-range enumeration
盲点
The repository directly binds the Node server to localhost and contains no checked deployment manifest establishing the external proxy, tunnel hostname, TLS port, or access policy; remote exposure relies on the supplied production threat model.
The full createApp process was not executed in validation because the checkout's installed dependencies lacked expr-eval. The executable PoC instead mounted the exact affected routers, middleware, repositories, and database through local HTTP.
Repository documentation establishes intended student visibility of a grade top-ten leaderboard but does not specify whether nonparticipation, exact-score display, or release-state behavior is approved policy. Cross-grade and grading-state disclosure are nevertheless contrary to the supplied threat model.
The single-exam response has a rank versus gradeRank mapping defect that omits overall rank and produces a null percentile; this narrows those response fields but does not prevent identity, exact-score, class-rank, or grade disclosure.
The analysis does not establish how students discover exam or group IDs through the UI, although integer IDs are accepted directly and repeated enumeration is technically possible.

## 17. Student AI endpoint allows unbounded billable LLM requests

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/58123da473708191aeda992e2b1c0021?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Student AI endpoint allows unbounded billable LLM requests
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
aef25aa
12:16 AM Jun 26, 2026

操作者：NaOH1g

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced. The baseline scores router had no student-accessible operation that caused an external LLM invocation. This commit adds the new student AI route and directly forwards each accepted request to /analysis/run without any rate limiter, quota, cache, or in-flight concurrency bound.

The new POST /api/scores/me/exams/:examId/ai-analysis route is protected only by ordinary authentication and a check that the caller has a score for the selected exam. After passing that reusable check, a student can submit the request an unlimited number of times, including concurrently, and can select any configured model. Every request is forwarded to the LLM sidecar with a 120-second timeout. The sidecar can perform up to eight billable provider round trips per analysis, each allowing up to 4096 output tokens. The UI's local generating flag is not a security control because an attacker can call the API directly. A single legitimate student can therefore consume the school's LLM quota or incur substantial charges and can fill the sidecar's worker pool and the Node server's outstanding-request capacity, making AI analysis unavailable during school operations. The internal sidecar bearer key does not provide per-user throttling because the Node server acts as the trusted caller. In this exact revision the route also uses different environment-variable names from the established LLMCLIENT_* variables, so correctly configured internal authentication may cause the endpoint to fail with 401; however, the sidecar explicitly accepts requests when its internal key is unset, as permitted by the default configuration, and fixing that functional mismatch would activate the same unbounded request path. The route needs a server-side per-user/per-IP quota, bounded global concurrency, request deduplication or caching, and preferably explicit authorization and cost accounting.

验证
 Confirm an authenticated student with a recorded score can reach the claimed POST route through the production router composition; production mounting was source-verified and the exact auth/scores routers were exercised over HTTP.
 Confirm each accepted request initiates a distinct /analysis/run operation under concurrency with no applicable limiter, quota, deduplication, cache, or global in-flight bound; 12 requests produced 12 simultaneous sidecar calls.
 Confirm attacker-controlled model selection reaches the sidecar/provider path and one analysis can cause repeated provider calls with the claimed token bound; gpt-5.5-pro was forwarded and the real loop made 8 calls with max_tokens=4096 each.
 Establish sidecar authentication/configuration preconditions; actual FastAPI tests confirmed key-unset acceptance and configured-key rejection without the bearer token, while source tracing confirmed the environment-name mismatch.
 Demonstrate the behavior through a bounded realistic interface and assess credible cost/capacity impact without contacting an external provider; HTTP concurrency and deterministic provider-loop reproductions completed successfully.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The directly invoked sidecar implementation can execute up to eight external-provider iterations per request, with max_tokens set to 4096 for each iteration.
- The scores router requires authentication but does not apply an AI-specific rate limiter or stronger capability gate.
- The new student route performs only a reusable hasScore check and starts a fresh 120-second LLM request for every call, accepting a caller-selected model with no quota or concurrency control.
- Each completed provider operation is returned directly; there is no caching, deduplication, accounting, or cooldown before another request can be made.

        if model.provider == "deepseek" and model.thinking:
