### [src/server/routes/scores.ts:104](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/aef25aa642c17ea910bd7f62ad1fc25110036b35/src/server/routes/scores.ts#L104)

~~~~typescript
/** POST /api/scores/me/exams/:examId/ai-analysis — 学生单场考试 AI 分析（绕过教师端 analysisGate） */
router.post("/me/exams/:examId/ai-analysis", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  const examId = Number(req.params.examId);
  if (!Number.isFinite(examId) || examId <= 0) {
    res.status(400).json({ message: "无效的考试 ID" });
    return;
  }
  if (!scoreRepo.hasScore(req.user!.id, examId)) {
    res.status(403).json({ message: "你未参加该考试" });
    return;
  }

  try {
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 120_000);
    const response = await fetch(`${LLM_CLIENT_BASE}/analysis/run`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: {
        "Content-Type": "application/json",
        ...(LLM_INTERNAL_KEY ? { Authorization: `Bearer ${LLM_INTERNAL_KEY}` } : {}),
      },
      body: JSON.stringify({
        examId,
        model: typeof req.body?.model === "string" ? req.body.model : undefined,
        locale: "zh-CN",
      }),
      signal: controller.signal,
    });
    clearTimeout(timeout);

    if (!response.ok) {
      let message = `LLM service returned ${response.status}`;
      try {
        const body = await response.json() as { detail?: string; message?: string };
        message = body.detail || body.message || message;
      } catch {
        const text = await response.text().catch(() => "");
        if (text) message = text;
      }
      res.status(response.status >= 400 && response.status < 500 ? response.status : 502).json({ message });
      return;
    }

    res.json(await response.json());
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Impact is high because an ordinary student can amplify each request into as many as eight billable provider operations and hold application requests open for up to 120 seconds, creating school-wide cost/quota and availability effects. Likelihood is high because the attack is a direct authenticated HTTP loop with no race, enumeration, privileged role, victim interaction, or long speculative chain; the supplied PoC demonstrated twelve concurrent accepted requests and twelve simultaneous sidecar calls. The loopback binding and environment-variable mismatch are the strongest counterevidence, but they reduce deployment universality rather than making exploitation unlikely or unachievable: remote Node exposure is part of the supplied production threat model, and key-unset operation is explicitly supported. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact plus high likelihood yields high rather than critical because the demonstrated effect is bounded to cost and availability of one installation and does not establish administrator takeover, code execution, database compromise, or score corruption.

路径
Authenticated student with one recorded exam score --Sends arbitrarily many authenticated sequential or concurrent POST requests--> Express POST /api/scores/me/exams/:examId/ai-analysis on Node port 5174 --Accepts the request when the caller has any score for the exam--> Reusable student_scores existence check --Forwards every accepted request independently with a 120-second timeout and attacker-selected model--> Python llmclient POST /analysis/run on loopback port 8766 --Resolves a configured model and may issue up to eight model calls, each with max_tokens=4096--> Configured external LLM provider using school-controlled API credentials --Consumes metered provider resources while long-running concurrent requests consume application capacity--> Provider cost/quota consumption and Node/sidecar availability exhaustion

A legitimate student with one recorded score can automate the normal student AI endpoint and cause one trusted sidecar request for every submitted POST. The route is mounted at /api/scores before the teacher analysisGate, uses only ordinary authentication plus ScoreRepository.hasScore(), accepts a caller-selected model, and waits as long as 120 seconds per request. The sidecar then invokes run_analysis, whose OpenAI-compatible path allows eight provider iterations with max_tokens=4096 per iteration. No applicable rate limiter, quota, cache, request coalescing, cooldown, or concurrency bound is present. The supplied executable validation used the production auth and scores routers with a non-billable mock sidecar: twelve concurrent requests from an eligible student all succeeded and all twelve were simultaneously forwarded; unauthenticated and no-score controls returned 401 and 403. A second reproduction exercised the real provider loop with its network client stubbed and observed eight calls at 4096 max tokens. The strongest counterevidence is configuration-dependent reachability: the Node listener and documented sidecar command bind to loopback, and this route reads LLM_CLIENT_URL/LLM_INTERNAL_KEY while the established integration and sidecar use LLMCLIENT_URL/LLMCLIENT_INTERNAL_API_KEY. A correctly configured nonempty sidecar key therefore makes this exact route return 401 until the mismatch is fixed. This does not make the issue unachievable because an empty internal key is explicitly documented as supported, the threat model includes remotely exposed Node deployments, and correcting the functional mismatch alone activates the same unbounded path. No provider secret is disclosed to the student; the security issue is unauthorized amplification through the trusted service's billing and execution authority.

发生可能性
High - Once the student-facing deployment and optional AI integration are enabled, exploitation requires only a normal student session, one legitimate exam score, and straightforward repeated or concurrent HTTP requests. The executable validation demonstrated twelve-way one-to-one forwarding with all requests accepted. Likelihood is qualified by the loopback defaults and the internal-key variable mismatch, but these are not unrealistic blockers: the supplied threat model includes proxy/tunnel exposure, an unset key is documented as supported, and fixing the mismatch without adding limits leaves the path immediately exploitable. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The resource amplification is substantial: every accepted low-privilege request initiates fresh trusted analysis, can remain outstanding for 120 seconds, and can reach up to eight provider calls with 4096 output tokens each. Unlimited concurrency can consume school-funded provider quota or charges and can deny the AI workflow by occupying Node requests and sidecar workers. This affects other users and school resources, so it is not self-only. No confidentiality breach or grade modification is established.
假设条件
The school deploys the Node service so student clients can reach it, directly on a school network or through the reverse-proxy/tunnel deployment described by the supplied threat model.
The optional Python LLM sidecar is running with at least one configured provider model and usable provider API key.
The sidecar internal key is unset as explicitly supported for local operation, or the route's LLM_CLIENT_*/LLM_INTERNAL_KEY naming mismatch is corrected without simultaneously adding throttling.
Provider usage is metered or finite enough that repeated model calls can consume quota or incur cost.
A valid low-privilege student session
At least one student_scores row for the selected exam
A running and provider-configured LLM sidecar
Sidecar authentication that accepts the route's request, either because LLMCLIENT_INTERNAL_API_KEY is unset or because forwarding configuration is aligned
Network reachability to the student-facing Node API
控制措施
authMiddleware rejects missing or invalid student sessions with 401.
ScoreRepository.hasScore restricts use to exams for which the caller has a student_scores row and returns 403 otherwise.
The sidecar accepts only known configured model identifiers and rejects models lacking a configured provider key.
The Node-to-sidecar request has a 120-second timeout, bounding one request's Node wait time but not request count or aggregate concurrency.
LLMCLIENT_INTERNAL_API_KEY can protect the sidecar from direct callers, but it does not impose per-student limits when Node is the trusted caller.
The React UI disables its button while one request is generating, but this is not a server-side security control.
The Node and sidecar defaults bind to loopback, requiring an intended proxy/tunnel or local application access for remote student use.
盲点
No reverse-proxy, Cloudflare tunnel, load-balancer, or network-policy manifest is present in the checkout; public reachability relies on the supplied deployment threat-model context.
A deployment that sets only the established nonempty LLMCLIENT_INTERNAL_API_KEY currently receives 401 from this route because the route reads a different variable name.
The optional sidecar and provider credentials may not be enabled in every installation.
The validation did not contact a real provider, incur charges, or sustain a production outage; provider-call amplification and concurrent forwarding were demonstrated with external network calls stubbed.
The eight-call provider reproduction demonstrates a reachable upper bound driven by repeated tool calls, not the expected number of calls for every model response.
Actual provider prices, account quotas, Node connection limits, sidecar worker count, upstream proxy limits, and provider-side rate limits are not specified in repository artifacts.
The full production index was not dynamically loaded in validation because an installed dependency was missing; production mount order was verified statically and the real auth and scores routers were exercised in the HTTP harness.

概要

The commit fixes a non-security JavaScript syntax failure, but in doing so introduces the reachable security behavior. Before the change, the browser could not parse or execute the inline script; after all four quote corrections, the script runs and the pre-existing unsafe combination of query-controlled API_BASE, localStorage token retrieval, and automatic authenticated initialization becomes exploitable.

At the baseline revision, malformed string literals in the inline script cause a JavaScript parse error, so none of the script executes. This commit repairs all four malformed literals and makes the complete script executable, but the now-reachable initialization code trusts api_base/apiBase from the page URL without validating its origin. The client also reads px_token from same-origin localStorage and automatically calls API.me() during DOMContentLoaded. apiGet() attaches that token as an Authorization header to API_BASE. Consequently, an unauthenticated attacker can send a previously authenticated user a link such as https://school.example/Grade-Analysis-System-mobile.html?api_base=https://attacker.example. The attacker's endpoint can approve the browser's CORS preflight and then receive the victim's bearer token in the request to /api/auth/me. The token can be replayed against the real school API until it expires or is revoked, allowing access and modifications with the victim's student, teacher, or administrator privileges. This is especially consequential for teacher or administrator sessions because it can expose educational records or permit grade and account changes. API_BASE must be restricted to the page origin or an explicit trusted-origin allowlist, and bearer tokens should not be persisted in localStorage or sent to a query-selected destination.

验证
 Verify commit causality: all relevant quote fixes make the previously unparsable classic script parse and execute, while the parent revision remains inert.
 Verify attacker control and absence of a relevant origin control: api_base/apiBase accepts an arbitrary HTTPS origin without same-origin or allowlist validation.
 Verify the credential source and automatic sink: a stored px_token reaches an Authorization-bearing request to the selected origin on DOMContentLoaded without another user action.
 Verify realistic reachability and impact prerequisites: deployment documentation exposes the standalone URL, login persists the token, and server middleware accepts it as a replayable user session.
 Complete an end-to-end browser/CORS capture and live-token replay against a deployed Project-X instance; unavailable browser libraries and absence of a live victim installation prevented this final integration step.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- API_BASE is taken directly from attacker-controlled query parameters, while a bearer token is read from localStorage and attached to fetches to that unrestricted destination.
- API.me() resolves to apiGet('/api/auth/me'), providing the automatic cross-origin request used to exfiltrate the token.
- Successful login responses are deliberately persisted as px_token in same-origin localStorage, creating the credential subsequently exposed by a crafted URL.
- This is the first changed malformed literal whose correction contributes to making the previously unparsable inline script executable.
- This changed quote correction removes another parse error from the same global inline script.
- This changed quote correction is required for the complete inline script to parse.
- The final changed quote correction allows the complete script to parse and makes the token-forwarding initialization reachable.
- On every page load with a stored token and supplied API_BASE, initialization automatically invokes API.me(), causing the bearer token to be sent to the selected host.

攻击路径分析

No hard suppression applies: the impact is not self-only; the attacker requires no account, local access, protected write access, or operator privilege; and the documented public deployment plus normal mobile login make the preconditions plausible. The factual calibration is high impact because a captured staff token can provide broad confidentiality and integrity access, and high likelihood because a remote attacker needs only a crafted link, a CORS-enabled endpoint, and one click from a victim with the page-created localStorage token. Under the required matrix, high impact multiplied by high likelihood yields high unless the critical criteria clearly demand immediate critical treatment. Critical is not warranted because exploitation still depends on optional deployment of the standalone page, an active exact-origin token, victim interaction, and the victim having a sufficiently privileged role. The original high rating is therefore retained.

路径
Unauthenticated remote attacker with crafted link and CORS-enabled HTTPS endpoint --induces victim to open crafted same-origin URL--> Deployed Grade-Analysis-System-mobile.html --automatically invokes API.me() during initialization--> DOMContentLoaded -> API.me() -> apiGet('/api/auth/me') --sends Authorization: Bearer token after attacker-approved CORS preflight--> Attacker HTTPS endpoint receiving Authorization header --attacker replays captured token--> Project-X bearer-token authentication and role permissions --resolves token to victim user and applies that role's permissions--> Victim identity and student, teacher, or administrator privileges

The repaired inline script is executable and exposes a credential-forwarding path in a documented mobile workflow. Grade-Analysis-System-mobile.html derives API_BASE directly from api_base/apiBase, retrieves px_token from localStorage, and automatically executes API.me() during DOMContentLoaded. apiGet() then sends that token in an Authorization header to API_BASE + '/api/auth/me'. Because the selected destination belongs to the attacker, the attacker can authorize the browser's CORS preflight and receive the credential. The server accepts the same opaque token as bearer authentication and derives the user's identity and role from it, making the captured token replayable until expiry or revocation. Repository documentation supports public deployment through a Cloudflare tunnel or Nginx, while the mobile page's login flow creates the required localStorage token during ordinary use. Counterevidence narrows but does not negate the finding: the HTML requires a documented deployment copy step, the victim must click a link and have an active exact-origin mobile token, and users relying exclusively on the main client's HttpOnly cookie are unaffected. The server's role-based authorization remains active, but it treats a replayed token as the victim and therefore only bounds impact to that victim's privileges rather than preventing exploitation.

发生可能性
High - The attacker is remote and unauthenticated, controls the only required input through a URL, and can satisfy CORS because the selected destination is attacker-operated. The page automatically emits the request after one victim click. Deployment and token-state prerequisites are realistic: the repository explicitly documents public hosting of this file, and its normal login flow stores px_token. Likelihood is reduced by the optional static deployment step, exact-origin token requirement, eight-hour normal expiry, and the fact that main-client cookie-only sessions are unaffected, but these conditions do not require operator, developer, local, or privileged attacker access. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The exposed value is a replayable authentication credential, not merely profile data. Bearer authentication resolves it to the victim's identity and role, so compromise of a teacher or administrator session can cross the unauthenticated-to-privileged identity boundary and expose educational records or authorize grade, user, and configuration changes available to that role. Impact is conditional on the victim's privileges and remaining token lifetime, but privileged staff are realistic users of the school system.
假设条件
The documented mobile HTML deployment workflow is used, making Grade-Analysis-System-mobile.html reachable through the school deployment or public tunnel.
The victim previously logged in through this mobile page, leaving a still-valid px_token in localStorage for the page's exact origin.
The victim opens an attacker-crafted URL containing api_base or apiBase but no overriding token parameter.
The attacker operates an HTTPS endpoint that permits the victim page origin and Authorization header in its CORS preflight response.
A stolen token is replayed before its normal expiry or server-side revocation.
The standalone mobile HTML is deployed on a victim-accessible origin.
The victim has a valid px_token stored by that page in same-origin localStorage.
The victim opens a crafted link selecting the attacker's endpoint through api_base or apiBase.
The attacker-controlled endpoint allows the browser's CORS preflight and Authorization header.
The token remains unexpired and unrevoked when captured and replayed.
控制措施
Project-X generates cryptographically random 32-byte opaque tokens rather than predictable session identifiers.
Normal non-persistent tokens expire after eight hours and can be invalidated by logout, password reset, or server-side revocation.
The mobile logout function removes px_token from localStorage.
Server-side authentication and role/permission checks constrain replay to the stolen victim's privileges.
An Authorization header causes a browser CORS preflight, but this is not an effective control because the request destination is attacker-controlled and can allow the victim origin and Authorization header.
The Node service binds to 127.0.0.1, although repository documentation exposes it through a public Cloudflare tunnel or Nginx reverse proxy.
The standalone page requires a documented deployment/static-copy step and is not automatically present in the checkout's dist/client.
Users authenticated only through the main client's HttpOnly cookie and without px_token under the mobile page origin do not satisfy the exploit precondition.
No restrictive CSP connect-src policy or API-origin validation was found for the affected mobile page.
盲点
No live Project-X deployment or full browser-to-attacker-server-to-Project-X replay was exercised in this static stage; the supplied executable harness demonstrated the repository script's fetch destination and Authorization header.
Repository artifacts document public Cloudflare and Nginx deployment, but they cannot establish which installations actually copied and exposed the standalone HTML.
The checkout does not establish the number or role distribution of users who authenticate through this mobile page rather than the main HttpOnly-cookie client.
The attacker-controlled endpoint must return valid browser CORS preflight headers; this behavior is under attacker control but was not tested in a full browser here.
Broad teacher and administrator consequences follow from bearer-token identity and repository permission gates, but every privileged endpoint was not individually replay-tested.
External proxy headers or deployment-level CSP not represented in the repository could reduce exploitability, although no such mandatory control is established by repository artifacts.

## 19. Unbounded score ranges enable authenticated server DoS

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/4159f92ce0648191b69ccc95b5fb24f4?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=)

创建 Pull Request
Unbounded score ranges enable authenticated server DoS

High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交

6:41 PM Jun 19, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced. Before this commit, overview generation always used five fixed ranges and therefore performed a bounded five count queries regardless of stored score maxima. This commit makes both allocation and database work linear in attacker-influenced fullScore without introducing a corresponding score limit or range-count limit.

The new generateDistributionRanges() iterates from zero to fullScore in increments of 10 without validating or capping fullScore. getExamOverview() derives fullScore by summing question_scores.max_score, invokes this helper synchronously, and then executes a separate database query for every generated range. Card update requests accept objective and subjective question scores without a finite-value or upper-bound check, and grading or answer regrading propagates those card values into question_scores.max_score. Consequently, a teacher with the normal card, exam, and grading permissions can set a question score to a large finite JSON number such as 1000000000, cause it to be persisted through grading or regrading, and request /api/analysis/exams/:examId/overview. The request attempts to construct 100 million range objects before issuing up to 100 million synchronous SQLite queries, exhausting memory or blocking the single Node event loop and making the entire school server unavailable. requireExamAccess on the overview endpoint does not mitigate this because the attacker can use an exam within their legitimate scope. The range count should be capped independently of stored data, score maxima should have server-side finite and reasonable upper bounds, and distribution counts should preferably be computed with one grouped SQL query rather than one synchronous query per bucket.

验证
 A normal authenticated teacher can submit and persist a very large finite card score without a server-side numeric upper bound.
 Production regrading propagates that card score into question_scores.max_score for a card-linked exam.
 The same teacher can invoke overview, where fullScore is derived uncapped from persisted max_score.
 Source tracing and two bounded measurements establish one range allocation and one synchronous SQL count per ten score points.
 A tiny authenticated input containing score 1000000000 caused the real overview path to terminate the Node process with heap exhaustion; lower values demonstrated seconds of synchronous amplification.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The card update endpoint accepts and normalizes the caller-supplied card without imposing a maximum on question scores.
- The overview route invokes the vulnerable repository calculation synchronously on the main Node request path.
- The introduced helper allocates one range per ten points with no validation or upper bound on fullScore.
- fullScore is derived directly from the sum of persisted question max_score values and is not capped before controlling work.
- Every dynamically generated range causes an additional synchronous database query, amplifying CPU and database cost.
- Answer regrading derives grades from card definitions and persists grade.maxScore into question_scores, providing a path for an oversized card score to reach the distribution calculation.

### [src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts:54](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/7606e6463cf73218339e7d45b03fc352ded01b33/src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts#L54)

~~~~typescript
/** 按 10 分一段动态生成分数段，末段截止于满分 */
function generateDistributionRanges(fullScore: number): Array<{ range: string; min: number; max: number }> {
  const step = 10;
  const ranges: Array<{ range: string; min: number; max: number }> = [];
  for (let min = 0; min < fullScore; min += step) {
    const max = Math.min(min + step - 1, fullScore);
    ranges.push({ range: `${min}-${max}`, min, max });
  }
  return ranges;
~~~~

攻击路径分析

The final policy pass does not trigger hard suppression: impact is not self-only, prerequisites are plausible, and the attacker is an ordinary lower-privileged teacher explicitly included in the threat model rather than an administrator, operator, developer, or local host user. Repository deployment guidance supports remote reachability through HTTPS/Nginx despite the direct loopback bind. Impact is high because attacker-controlled persisted data can crash or substantially block the shared runtime, and likelihood is high because the complete normal HTTP workflow was demonstrated with standard teacher permissions. Under the required matrix, high impact multiplied by high likelihood yields high unless critical criteria demand escalation. Critical is not warranted because authentication and teacher write permissions are required, reach is one service rather than a fleet, and no durable data compromise, code execution, or administrator takeover is shown. The finding therefore remains reportable at high severity.

路径
Authenticated ordinary teacher --uses normal teacher write permission--> PUT /api/cards/:cardId with oversized finite score --normalization imposes no finite upper bound--> CardRepository persists uncapped score --card is linked to the target exam--> PUT /api/exams/:examId/answers regrading --grade.maxScore is upserted--> question_scores.max_score receives attacker-controlled maximum --SUM(max_score) becomes fullScore--> GET /api/analysis/exams/:examId/overview --synchronous repository method runs in request path--> Unbounded range allocation and one synchronous query per bucket --O(fullScore/10) objects, queries, and response entries--> Node heap exhaustion or prolonged event-loop blocking

Attacker story: a malicious or compromised ordinary teacher edits a normal answer card and assigns one objective question a finite score of 1000000000. normalizeCard does not cap that value, and CardRepository writes it to SQLite. The teacher then invokes the normal answer-regrading route, which obtains grade.maxScore from the card and upserts it into question_scores.max_score. When the teacher requests the exam overview, getExamOverview sums max_score into fullScore, generates one range for each ten points, and synchronously performs one count query per range. The supplied executable validation used authenticated production HTTP routes: 1000000 produced 100000 buckets, a 6.16 MB response, about 3.8 seconds of synchronous latency, and 370.1 MiB RSS; 1000000000 attempted 100000000 buckets and terminated a heap-constrained Node process with an out-of-memory error. No secrets are exposed or required beyond the teacher's normal authenticated session; the security impact is shared-service availability. The strongest scoping counterevidence is that startServer binds only to 127.0.0.1, but this is not dispositive because deploy-guide.md explicitly recommends a public HTTPS Nginx reverse proxy to that loopback port. The strongest authorization counterevidence is that RBAC and requireExamAccess protect the endpoints, but the stock teacher role already has every required permission and the demonstrated attack stays within a legitimately visible exam. The empty-exam early return is a real mitigating precondition, but an exam with at least one graded student is routine. Finally, the crash reproduction used a 128 MiB heap cap, so it does not establish the exact default-heap failure point; nevertheless, the source-level unbounded allocation and query loop plus the bounded 370.1 MiB measurement establish major resource amplification independently of that cap.

发生可能性
High - The path is remotely invocable in the documented public reverse-proxy deployment and was exercised through real authenticated HTTP routes. It requires only a stock teacher account and ordinary card/regrading/analytics operations, not administrator or host privileges. The malicious request is small, the oversized value is persisted, and no difficult race or victim interaction is required. The card-linked exam and at-least-one-score prerequisites are routine in the intended workflow. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The unbounded allocation and synchronous query loop execute in the shared Node request process. Heap exhaustion terminates that process, while large but non-crashing values consume hundreds of MiB and block the event loop for seconds or longer. This can make the examination platform unavailable to all teachers, students, and scanners using that service during grading operations. Confidentiality and integrity impact are not established.
假设条件
Production enables PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE as required by the supplied threat model.
The attacker controls an ordinary teacher account with the repository's default teacher permissions.
A card is linked to an exam in the teacher's legitimate scope and that exam has at least one student_scores row.
The school may expose the loopback-bound Node service through the documented public HTTPS reverse-proxy deployment.
The exact failure threshold varies with the Node heap and host memory, but the attacker-controlled work remains linear in fullScore.
An authenticated ordinary teacher account with grade:write, exam:write, and grade:read permissions
A writable answer card linked to an accessible exam
At least one student_scores row, because an empty exam returns before range generation
A grading or answer-regrading operation that copies the card question score into question_scores.max_score
A request to GET /api/analysis/exams/:examId/overview
控制措施
Production authentication can be enforced with PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE.
Card, exam, and analysis route prefixes have role-permission gates.
The overview route applies requireExamAccess to the requested exam.
The JSON request body is limited to 8 MB, but this does not bound a numeric value.
Overview generation returns early when gradedCount is zero.
The backend binds to 127.0.0.1 by default, although documented deployments expose it through Nginx.
No score upper bound or independent distribution-bucket cap protects the vulnerable work factor.
No analytics rate limit is evident, and a single request is sufficient for the demonstrated impact.
盲点
The repository contains deployment guidance but no environment-specific manifest proving that a particular installation is publicly reachable.
The out-of-memory reproduction intentionally used a 128 MiB V8 heap; the exact crash threshold and recovery behavior under a production heap or process supervisor are deployment-dependent.
The executable validation exercised the SQLite-backed server; other configured database modes may have different per-query costs, although range allocation remains unbounded before query completion.
Host-level memory limits, reverse-proxy timeouts, and process restart policies are not established by repository artifacts.
Authentication is disabled by default in this code path, but the analysis conservatively follows the supplied production assumption that authentication is enabled.

概要

Introduced. Before this commit, overview generation used five constant ranges and issued five distribution queries regardless of the stored maximum score. The new loop and per-range query make request cost proportional to an unbounded database value.

The commit replaces five fixed distribution buckets with `generateDistributionRanges(fullScore)`. `fullScore` is calculated by summing `question_scores.max_score`, and neither the database schema nor the card normalization and grading path imposes an upper bound on objective-question scores. A teacher with grading permissions can therefore define an objective question with a very large finite score, grade a recognized student so that this maximum is persisted, and request the exam overview. A full score of 1,000,000,000 causes the synchronous request handler to allocate approximately 100 million range objects before it even begins executing one SQL `COUNT` query per range. This can exhaust the process heap or block the event loop, taking down all API functionality. The malicious score remains in the database, so the condition can be retriggered after restart. The client-side filtering occurs only after a response and does not bound server work. Validate and cap every question and aggregate maximum score, reject non-finite values, and generate a fixed maximum number of buckets—preferably using one aggregate SQL query rather than one query per bucket.

验证
 Confirm overview uses persisted SUM(max_score) as an uncapped loop bound and performs O(fullScore/10) allocations and queries.
 Confirm a non-admin teacher-reachable card/grading path accepts an excessive finite objective score and propagates it to question_scores.max_score.
 Confirm the overview endpoint is authenticated/authorized but reachable by that teacher and synchronously invokes the vulnerable repository path.
 Dynamically reproduce through the closest feasible production interfaces and repository harness, showing the persisted excessive score causes an authenticated HTTP request to terminate the server with heap OOM.
 Check schema/runtime controls and persistence: no score cap exists, at least one graded student is required, and the malicious value survives and crashes a restarted process.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Card normalization accepts any JavaScript number as a subjective score without checking finiteness or imposing a maximum; objective normalization similarly delegates without a bound.
- The grading persistence path writes generated per-question maximum scores directly into `question_scores`, which supplies the vulnerable aggregate.
- Introduces an unbounded loop that allocates one object for every ten points of the attacker-influenced full score.
- Derives `fullScore` directly by summing persisted `max_score` values without finite-value or upper-bound validation.
- Generates all ranges synchronously and then executes a separate SQL query for every generated range, amplifying both memory and CPU/database consumption.
- Objective question normalization copies the submitted question score without finite-value or magnitude validation.

### [src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts:54](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/31d080039fce59f6d10699e9bc63a109707b3939/src/server/repositories/AnalysisRepository.ts#L54)

~~~~typescript
/** 按 10 分一段动态生成分数段，末段截止于满分 */
function generateDistributionRanges(fullScore: number): Array<{ range: string; min: number; max: number }> {
  const step = 10;
  const ranges: Array<{ range: string; min: number; max: number }> = [];
  for (let min = 0; min < fullScore; min += step) {
    const max = Math.min(min + step - 1, fullScore);
    ranges.push({ range: `${min}-${max}`, min, max });
  }
  return ranges;
~~~~

攻击路径分析

No severity suppression applies. The effect is not self-only: an authorized teacher can terminate the shared API process for every user. The attacker is not an administrator, operator, developer, or local host user; the necessary capabilities are default teacher permissions in a normal production workflow, and the service-wide availability effect exceeds the teacher's intended grading authority. Preconditions are plausible and were substantially exercised by the supplied executable PoC. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact combined with high likelihood yields high unless the critical criteria are satisfied. Critical is not warranted because exploitation requires a teacher account and grading preconditions, affects one service instance, and demonstrates availability loss rather than administrator takeover, broad data compromise, remote code execution, or silent mass score corruption.

路径
Authenticated ordinary teacher --uses default teacher CARD_WRITE permission--> PUT /api/cards/:cardId with score=1000000000 --normalization imposes no finite magnitude cap--> CardRepository persists unbounded objective score --grading copies configured score into maxScore--> Objective grading emits maxScore=1000000000 --production grading INSERT writes q.maxScore--> question_scores.max_score stores malicious value --repository sums persisted maximum scores--> SUM(max_score) becomes fullScore --controls loop count and query count without a cap--> Unbounded range allocation and per-range SQL queries --exhausts heap or blocks the event loop/database--> Node process heap exhaustion and shared API outage

Attacker story: a malicious or compromised ordinary teacher uses the normal card-editing API to assign an objective question a very large finite score. The default teacher role includes card and grading read/write permissions, and a legacy teacher without a specialized teacher_role can see all exams. normalizeCard and objective-question normalization do not impose a score bound; CardRepository writes the value, objective grading copies it into maxScore, and the production grading loop writes it to question_scores. When an authorized user requests the exam overview after at least one student has been graded, AnalysisRepository sums the malicious maximum and creates one JavaScript range object per ten points before issuing one COUNT query per range. A score of 1,000,000,000 therefore attempts approximately 100,000,000 allocations. The supplied executable validation exercised the authenticated card-write route, real grading logic and persistence shape, and authenticated overview route; the overview request terminated the server with a V8 heap-out-of-memory failure, and the persisted rows allowed the failure to be retriggered after restart. No secret is needed or exposed; the affected asset is shared runtime availability. The strongest counterevidence is that the Node server itself binds only to loopback, the input requires a teacher's protected grading workflow, requireExamAccess limits specialized teachers, and overview returns early when no student has been graded. These facts are not dispositive: the repository documents public HTTPS reverse proxy deployment, the required privileges are assigned to the routine teacher role rather than administrators or operators, the service-wide crash exceeds authorized grading effects, and a normal recognized-student grading event satisfies the graded-row condition. The PoC used a bounded 128 MiB heap and did not run native OCR end to end, but attempting 100,000,000 JavaScript objects is also beyond realistic default heaps, and the vulnerable HTTP entry and crash sink were exercised with authentication enforcement enabled.

发生可能性
High - The documented production architecture supports remote HTTPS access, and the required permissions belong to the routine teacher role rather than an administrator or host operator. The attacker uses ordinary JSON card editing, grading, and overview workflows; no race, secret extraction, native exploit, or victim interaction is required. A graded student is required, but that is normal operation, and executable validation demonstrated authenticated acceptance, persistence, a process OOM, and post-restart retriggering. Likelihood is reduced from an unauthenticated case by the need for a teacher credential and authorized exam workflow, but remains high for the threat model's malicious or compromised-teacher actor. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The vulnerable loop executes in the shared Node API process and can exhaust its heap before returning a response. This terminates all API functionality for teachers, students, administrators, and scanner workflows handled by that process. The malicious maximum remains in the database, allowing an authorized request to retrigger the outage after restart. Availability during grading windows is explicitly high-value in the supplied threat model. The finding does not establish confidentiality loss, code execution, or permanent grade destruction.
假设条件
The production deployment enables PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE as required by the supplied threat model.
The Node API is made remotely reachable through the documented public HTTPS Nginx reverse proxy, while the process itself remains bound to 127.0.0.1:5174.
The attacker has a normal teacher account with the default card and grading permissions and can modify a card associated with an exam they can grade.
At least one recognized student is graded after the malicious card score is saved, creating student_scores and question_scores rows for the exam.
An authenticated teacher account with CARD_WRITE, GRADE_WRITE, and GRADE_READ permissions
Access to a card and exam in the teacher's authorized scope
Submission of a very large finite objective-question score through PUT /api/cards/:cardId
Grading and persistence of at least one recognized student's result
A request to GET /api/analysis/exams/:examId/overview
控制措施
optionalAuth parses API identities before business routes
makeGate can require authentication and role permissions when PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled
Card writes require GRADE_WRITE through cardGate
Analysis reads require GRADE_READ through analysisGate
requireExamAccess limits specialized teachers to visible exams
The default server socket is loopback-only and intended to be placed behind an HTTPS reverse proxy
getExamOverview returns before distribution generation if there are no graded students
No score-magnitude validation or aggregate fullScore cap protects the vulnerable path
No general rate or concurrency limit protects the overview endpoint
盲点
The checkout cannot establish whether a particular installation actually follows the documented public Nginx deployment; the process itself binds to loopback.
Authentication enforcement is environment-controlled and defaults off in code, although the supplied threat model requires it in production and the validation used enforcement enabled.
Native image recognition was not exercised end to end; validation instead used real grading functions with a realistic recognized-student result and the production persistence SQL shape.
The crash was reproduced with a bounded 128 MiB V8 heap. Exact failure timing under a default heap depends on runtime limits, but allocating approximately 100,000,000 objects remains practically exhausting.
The validated database path was SQLite; equivalent constraints and runtime behavior for MariaDB were not dynamically tested.
No repository infrastructure manifest establishes horizontal replicas, external process supervision, restart policy, or upstream circuit breakers that could alter outage duration.

## 21. AI analysis endpoint permits unbounded paid model requests

代码仓库

聊天
概要

This commit introduced the paid AI-analysis endpoint and provider loop without any abuse controls. The existing generic analysis gate does not mitigate repeated or parallel requests, and no limiting mechanism exists in either the Node proxy or the Python sidecar.

POST /api/analysis/exams/:examId/ai-analysis accepts an attacker-selected configured model and immediately forwards the request to the Python sidecar. Its only production authorization is the generic grade:read gate, which every teacher receives by default; there is no per-user rate limit, global concurrency limit, request deduplication, queue, cooldown, or usage budget. Each accepted request can keep the upstream connection open for 120 seconds. The sidecar then performs synchronous paid-provider calls in a loop of up to eight rounds, with a 4096-token limit and high reasoning effort, and the model catalog includes a selectable pro model. A compromised or malicious teacher can therefore send many parallel requests to consume provider funds or quota and exhaust sidecar threads/connections, denying AI analysis to legitimate teachers. If the compatibility setting PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is left disabled, the same route is reachable without authentication, making exploitation still easier. Add user and installation-level rate limits, a small global concurrency queue, provider spending/token budgets, request cancellation, and audit records; AI invocation should also require a dedicated permission rather than generic score-read access.

验证
 Reachability/authentication: verified unauthenticated requests are blocked when enforcement is enabled, while a default teacher with grade:read can invoke the endpoint; source confirms the gate is bypassed when enforcement is disabled.
 Trigger and abuse controls: 45 parallel teacher requests were all accepted and forwarded with the attacker-selected model; no application rate limit, AI-specific concurrency limit, deduplication, cooldown, or budget was encountered.
 Cost amplification: production provider-loop execution with a mocked SDK produced eight gpt-5.5-pro calls, each using max_tokens=4096 and reasoning_effort=high.
 Availability behavior: the realistic HTTP test reached the observed 40-worker FastAPI/AnyIO capacity and queued additional requests, demonstrating attacker-controlled sidecar worker saturation.
 Preconditions and impact: valid-exam and configured-provider-key requirements were identified; availability exhaustion was dynamically demonstrated, while real monetary charges remain conditional and were intentionally not incurred.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The caller-selectable catalog includes high-reasoning and pro OpenAI models, increasing the potential cost of unbounded invocations.
- One endpoint invocation can make up to eight paid provider calls, each allowing 4096 output tokens and using high reasoning effort.
- All analysis endpoints, including paid AI invocation, are protected only by the generic grade:read permission and the gate is bypassed entirely when authentication enforcement is disabled.
- The newly introduced route accepts the caller-selected model and forwards every request with a 120-second timeout, without rate, concurrency, quota, or deduplication controls.

攻击路径分析

The final policy pass does not suppress the finding. It is not self-only, unachievable, operator-only, developer-only, physical-access-only, admin-only, or dependent on a protected write path. An ordinary teacher is a lower-privileged in-scope actor, and the security delta is the ability to consume a shared paid and availability-sensitive resource without an AI-specific authorization or abuse control. Impact is high because shared worker saturation was directly reproduced and provider quota or cost amplification is supported by an up-to-eight-call production loop. Likelihood is high because the intended network endpoint accepts low-complexity parallel requests from every default teacher, with executable validation confirming reachability and overlap. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact plus high likelihood yields high rather than critical because the demonstrated effect is confined to one installation's AI service and provider resources, monetary harm is conditional, and no broader system takeover, score corruption, or sensitive-data compromise was shown.

路径
Malicious or compromised default-role teacher --Sends parallel POSTs with valid examId and attacker-selected model--> Express POST /api/analysis/exams/:examId/ai-analysis --POST is authorized using grade:read, or bypasses the gate when enforcement is disabled--> Generic grade:read analysis gate --Express forwards every accepted request and waits up to 120 seconds--> FastAPI POST /analysis/run --Configured model invokes synchronous provider create calls--> Configured paid LLM provider and up-to-eight-round tool loop --Parallel work occupies shared workers and repeated rounds consume provider resources--> Shared AI worker saturation, queued requests, quota or monetary exhaustion

The affected service chain is the Express examination application on port 5174, its loopback FastAPI LLM sidecar on port 8766, and configured external LLM providers. POST /api/analysis/exams/:examId/ai-analysis is a production teacher workflow, but its only enforced capability is grade:read, granted to every default teacher. The route accepts the caller's model choice and forwards each request with a 120-second timeout after only exam-existence and classId-format checks. The sidecar verifies that the model is in the catalog, that its provider key exists, and that the database exists, then synchronously executes a loop capable of eight 4096-token, high-reasoning provider calls. Supplied executable validation used the real Express authentication/gate/proxy and FastAPI route with only the paid provider operation stubbed: an unauthenticated request was rejected under enforced authentication, while all 45 parallel default-teacher requests were accepted and forwarded with gpt-5.5-pro; 40 provider operations ran simultaneously and the rest queued. A separate executable provider-loop reproduction reached eight calls with max_tokens=4096 and high reasoning effort. The strongest counterevidence is that Express and the documented sidecar bind to loopback, authentication enforcement blocks anonymous access when enabled, an optional bearer key protects direct sidecar access, Express aborts its fetch after 120 seconds, and FastAPI/AnyIO showed an implicit 40-worker ceiling. These controls are not dispositive: the production threat model allows remote publication of Express, an ordinary teacher passes the gate, the Node service legitimately crosses the sidecar key boundary, the timeout does not limit request admission or spending, and the runtime ceiling allowed every request to enter while saturating all observed workers. A funded provider and accepted model are prerequisites for monetary harm, and no real provider was contacted during validation; therefore billing magnitude remains conditional, while shared-worker saturation and queuing were directly demonstrated.

发生可能性
High - Likelihood is high for an AI-enabled deployment because the attacker needs only a normal teacher account, a valid exam, and repeated ordinary HTTP requests; teachers are an explicit in-scope threat actor and the endpoint is intended for their routine use. The attack requires no race, parser exploit, administrator action, or direct access to provider secrets. Executable validation proved that a default teacher can submit 45 overlapping requests and that every request reaches the sidecar. Loopback binding and absent repository-owned ingress reduce deployment certainty, while the supplied production threat model establishes realistic remote publication through a proxy or tunnel. If authentication is disabled under the documented compatibility default, likelihood increases further, but the rating does not rely on that weaker configuration. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The impact is installation-wide rather than self-only: a teacher can occupy the shared AI sidecar's synchronous worker capacity, delay legitimate analyses, and repeatedly cause up to eight expensive provider operations per accepted request. The executable HTTP reproduction demonstrated complete admission of 45 parallel requests, saturation of all 40 observed workers, and queuing. Provider quota and monetary exhaustion are strongly supported by the production call loop but remain conditional on a funded key, provider acceptance, actual output, and billing. The supplied threat-model calibration explicitly treats authenticated low-privilege AI-cost or sustained availability exhaustion as high impact.
假设条件
The installation uses the documented AI-analysis workflow with the Python sidecar running and at least one funded provider API key configured.
A valid exam exists, as required by the endpoint before it forwards a request.
The conservative production case has PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE enabled and the attacker controls or compromises an ordinary teacher account; when enforcement is disabled, authentication is not required.
The supplied threat model's production deployment model applies: the loopback-bound Express service may be published through an operator-managed reverse proxy or Cloudflare tunnel.
Monetary impact requires the selected catalog model to be accepted and billed by the configured provider; shared sidecar saturation does not require proof of actual provider billing.
A reachable Project-X HTTP service
A valid exam identifier
A running LLM sidecar with access to the Project-X database
At least one configured provider API key
A teacher account with grade:read when PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, or no account when it is disabled
控制措施
PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE can require authentication and return 401 or 403
The production-style validation confirmed unauthenticated access was blocked when enforcement was enabled
The default teacher still satisfies the generic grade:read gate
The sidecar can require LLMCLIENT_INTERNAL_API_KEY for direct requests
Express and the documented sidecar bind to loopback by default
The sidecar rejects unknown models, models without configured provider keys, and missing databases
Express aborts its sidecar fetch after 120 seconds
FastAPI/AnyIO imposed an observed implicit ceiling of 40 simultaneous synchronous worker operations, but queued rather than rejected excess requests
The documented AI report uses aggregate score data and does not intentionally send student names
No application-level per-user rate limit, bounded AI queue, spending budget, cooldown, request deduplication, or AI-specific permission was found on this path
盲点
No repository-owned reverse-proxy, Cloudflare tunnel, load-balancer, or firewall manifest establishes the exact production ingress; remote exposure relies on the supplied threat model and operator deployment.
The validation replaced the paid external-provider operation with a synchronous stub, so actual billing, model-ID acceptance, provider-side quotas, provider-side rate limits, and real latency were not measured.
The observed 40-worker ceiling is runtime-dependent and may differ across AnyIO, FastAPI, Python, or deployment configurations.
A 120-second Express abort does not by itself prove whether the underlying provider SDK call is cancelled; cancellation behavior was not established.
The maximum of eight provider calls requires repeated tool-call responses from the model; many normal analyses may terminate earlier.
The repository does not reveal provider account budgets, credit limits, or the financial scale of a specific installation.

## 22. Unbounded grading-progress streams enable connection exhaustion

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduced the vulnerable progress-listener maps and the indefinitely open SSE route. The previous revision had no grading-progress stream that could be used to retain arbitrary response objects and connections.

The newly introduced GET /api/cards/:cardId/grading/progress/:batchId route starts an SSE response without verifying that the card exists, that the batch is active, or that the batch belongs to the requesting user. For every attacker-selected batch ID, it stores a handler closure that retains the Express response in the process-global gradingProgressListeners map. If no grading request emits a done or error event for that ID, the server provides no timeout or other server-side cleanup; cleanup occurs only when the client disconnects. An authenticated teacher with the generic card:read permission can therefore open many requests such as /api/cards/nonexistent/grading/progress/unique-N and deliberately keep the connections alive. This consumes Node and reverse-proxy sockets, file descriptors, and heap until legitimate scanning and grading traffic is denied service. The attack is also unauthenticated in installations using the application's default non-enforcing authentication mode, although production guidance requires enforcement. The endpoint should accept only server-created active job identifiers bound to the authenticated user and card, reject unknown jobs, impose per-user/IP stream limits, and close idle streams after a bounded lifetime.

验证
 Route accepts attacker-controlled cardId/batchId through the production middleware with the claimed low privilege; enforced mode was exercised using a teacher token, and default non-enforcement is confirmed in source.
 Nonexistent card and unknown batch identifiers are accepted without active-job, ownership, or card validation.
 Each held request registers a process-global listener closure retaining the response, with no server timeout, cap, or idle expiration.
 A request-level reproduction held 2,000 unknown-ID connections for 15 seconds, increasing server descriptors by exactly 2,000 and RSS by about 59 MiB.
 Cleanup behavior was characterized: resources remain while the attacker holds connections, descriptors return to baseline on disconnect, and done/error cleanup cannot occur for never-created batches.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- These process-global maps retain attacker-selected batch IDs and listener closures containing live response objects.
- The new SSE route returns 200 and registers an indefinitely lived listener for any batch and card ID, with no job validation, stream limit, or server-side expiration; cleanup depends on the client closing the connection.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:127](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/f3560f9aabdacccb48dd6f80d234c343b3d35c39/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L127)

~~~~typescript
const gradingProgressListeners = new Map<string, Set<(event: GradingProgressEvent) => void>>();
const gradingProgressSnapshots = new Map<string, GradingProgressEvent>();
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:491](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/f3560f9aabdacccb48dd6f80d234c343b3d35c39/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L491)

~~~~typescript
  app.get("/api/cards/:cardId/grading/progress/:batchId", (req, res) => {
    const batchId = safeId(paramValue(req.params.batchId));

    res.writeHead(200, {
      "Content-Type": "text/event-stream",
      "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
      Connection: "keep-alive",
      "X-Accel-Buffering": "no"
    });

    const handler = (event: GradingProgressEvent) => {
      res.write(`data: ${JSON.stringify(event)}\n\n`);
      if (event.type === "done" || event.type === "error") {
        res.end();
      }
    };

    if (!gradingProgressListeners.has(batchId)) {
      gradingProgressListeners.set(batchId, new Set());
    }
    gradingProgressListeners.get(batchId)!.add(handler);

    const snapshot = gradingProgressSnapshots.get(batchId);
    if (snapshot) {
      handler(snapshot);
    }

    req.on("close", () => {
      const listeners = gradingProgressListeners.get(batchId);
      if (listeners) {
        listeners.delete(handler);
        if (listeners.size === 0) gradingProgressListeners.delete(batchId);
      }
    });
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Hard suppression does not apply: the impact is not self-only in the shared-server deployment, the preconditions are plausible, and the attacker needs at most a normal teacher identity rather than administrator, operator, developer, physical, or protected-write-path privilege. The verified path crosses from generic card-read access to control over shared process resources. Impact is high because the service retains sockets, file descriptors, response objects, closures, and heap without an application bound, with a measured increase of 2,000 descriptors and about 59 MiB RSS. Likelihood is high because the route is remotely reachable in the supplied production threat model, accepts simple attacker-controlled IDs, and is usable by a default teacher or anonymously when enforcement remains disabled. Applying the required matrix gives high impact plus high likelihood; critical is not selected because impact is availability-only, the bounded validation did not demonstrate complete outage, and actual exhaustion depends on deployment and proxy limits. The original high severity is therefore retained.

路径
Remote caller or authenticated teacher --opens many held HTTP connections--> Public proxy or tunnel to loopback Express API --forwards GET /api/cards/.../grading/progress/...--> Optional authentication and generic card:read gate --allows by default or accepts teacher card:read--> Arbitrary grading-progress SSE route --registers response-capturing closure for unverified batchId--> Process-global gradingProgressListeners map and open responses --retains resources until attacker-controlled disconnect--> Shared Node and proxy connection, file-descriptor, and heap exhaustion

This is a genuine runtime availability vulnerability in a product grading workflow, not merely a correctness defect. The client normally creates a progress ID and opens the affected EventSource before submitting grading work, but the server does not maintain or validate server-created jobs. Any caller reaching the route controls cardId and batchId; cardId is unused, and safeId only strips characters. For every batchId, the handler adds a closure capturing the Express response to a process-global map and provides no stream cap, idle timeout, absolute lifetime, or unknown-job rejection. Unknown IDs never receive done or error events, leaving cleanup under attacker control through connection closure. In enforced mode, an unauthenticated request is rejected, but a default teacher account has the generic card:read permission and reaches the handler. Authentication enforcement is disabled by default, so compatibility-mode deployments expose the same path without authentication. No protected secret is read or disclosed; the security consequence is exhaustion of shared runtime resources. The executable validation used the normal HTTP interface with authentication enforcement enabled and a teacher token: 2,000 retained requests increased file descriptors by exactly 2,000 and RSS by approximately 59 MiB, and all remained open until the client released them. Strongest impact counterevidence is that the bounded test did not produce a complete outage and a normal request still completed quickly; disconnect cleanup restored descriptors to baseline. This limits certainty about the deployment-specific outage threshold but does not negate the proven unbounded, linear retention. The loopback bind and possible upstream limits similarly reduce exposure certainty without providing an application-side bound.

发生可能性
High - The attack uses ordinary GET requests, arbitrary path values, no race, and no expensive prerequisite. In enforced production mode, a default teacher has the required card:read permission; in default compatibility mode, no account is required. The supplied threat model establishes a realistic remotely reachable proxy or tunnel deployment. A complete attack does require maintaining many connections, and unknown reverse-proxy limits plus the repository's loopback-only bind create deployment uncertainty, but the successful 2,000-connection HTTP reproduction demonstrates practical reachability and stable retention. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The issue affects availability of the shared Express process serving grading, scanning, cards, exams, and analysis. The executable reproduction proves attacker-controlled linear consumption: 2,000 retained requests caused 2,000 additional server file descriptors and approximately 59 MiB additional RSS until release. At deployment-dependent limits this can deny legitimate grading and scanning during operationally sensitive windows. Confidentiality and integrity are not affected, and the bounded test did not itself force an outage, but the threat model explicitly treats sustained low-privilege runtime resource exhaustion as high impact.
假设条件
Production uses the shared Express service through the reverse-proxy or Cloudflare-tunnel deployment described by the supplied threat model, although no ingress configuration is committed in the repository.
Authentication is enforced in a properly configured production deployment; the principal attacker story therefore uses a legitimate or compromised teacher account with card:read.
The attacker can maintain many concurrent TCP connections and no unobserved upstream connection limit stops the attack before application resources are materially consumed.
The exact connection count required for denial of service depends on production file-descriptor limits, memory limits, and reverse-proxy capacity.
Network reachability to the Express API, normally through the deployment proxy or tunnel described in the supplied threat model
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, a valid account whose role has card:read, including the default teacher role
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is disabled, no authenticated identity is required
Ability to open and deliberately retain many concurrent HTTP connections
Application or upstream connection limits high enough to admit a resource-significant number of streams
控制措施
optionalAuth parses valid bearer or query-string tokens but does not reject anonymous requests
PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE can enable 401 and 403 enforcement
Enforced GET access to /api/cards requires card:read
safeId strips characters outside letters, digits, underscore, and hyphen
done and error events delete the matching listener set
req close removes the individual listener and empty set
terminal-event snapshots are deleted after 60 seconds
The Node server binds to 127.0.0.1
No application-side stream rate limit, concurrency cap, job validation, ownership check, idle timeout, or absolute lifetime is present
盲点
No reverse-proxy, Cloudflare tunnel, load-balancer, or network-policy configuration is committed; public exposure is based on the supplied required threat model rather than a repository ingress manifest.
Production reverse-proxy connection limits, request timeouts, per-IP controls, and buffering behavior are unknown.
Production process file-descriptor limits, container or service memory limits, Node runtime settings, and host capacity are unknown.
The validation proved linear resource retention and cleanup behavior but did not force complete service denial at 2,000 connections.
The validation exercised the Express service over loopback; it did not measure additional resource consumption or earlier limits at a real reverse proxy.
A desktop-only Electron deployment uses an ephemeral loopback server and may make the effect local or self-impacting rather than remotely reportable.

## 23. Batch grading enables unauthenticated resource exhaustion

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/8551b3c86768819195fc15f6bff0a1bc?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

Batch grading enables unauthenticated resource exhaustion

High

提交
4f92791
9:19 PM Jun 10, 2026

操作者：chuh

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

Introduced: the new batch objective-grading route multiplies the existing per-file upload allowance by 200 without an aggregate byte limit, authentication, rate/concurrency control, or temporary-file cleanup. Because upload middleware runs before card validation, even requests for nonexistent cards leave attacker-controlled files on disk.

The commit adds POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading/objective using recognitionUpload.array("files", 200). The shared Multer configuration limits each individual file to 20 MiB but imposes no reasonable aggregate request-size limit, so one request can write approximately 4 GiB to the recognition upload directory. Multer processes and stores the entire multipart body before the route handler checks whether the card exists, meaning an unauthenticated attacker can use any arbitrary card ID and does not need to discover a valid card. Uploaded files are never removed on success, recognition failure, invalid card ID, or client disconnect. For a valid card, the handler also serially invokes the native recognizer for every file; its per-process timeout is 30 seconds, allowing one batch to schedule up to roughly 100 minutes of native work. Concurrent batches can therefore consume disk, CPU, process slots, and request capacity, causing sustained denial of service during scanning or grading operations. The prior single-file recognition route had a related retention weakness, but this commit introduces a distinct 200-file batch amplification path.

验证
 Confirm the route is reachable without authentication and middleware order persists multipart files before card lookup.
 Confirm production Multer limits permit up to 200 disk-backed files at 20 MiB each without an aggregate-byte control.
 Reproduce through the HTTP interface that an invalid card ID stores attacker-controlled files and returns 404.
 Confirm completed uploads are not cleaned up on invalid-card and relevant success/failure paths; separately account for Multer's upload-phase abort cleanup.
 Trace valid-card files into serial native recognition and verify the 30-second per-process timeout and absence of route-level concurrency controls.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The disk-backed recognition uploader applies only a 20 MiB per-file limit and has no aggregate-byte, MIME, or magic-byte restriction; stored files are not configured for automatic cleanup.
- The newly introduced unauthenticated route permits 200 files. Multer executes before the handler's card existence check, so all files are persisted even when cardId is invalid.
- Every uploaded file is sent to the native recognizer, while neither success nor failure paths delete the uploaded files or enforce batch/concurrency quotas.

攻击路径分析

The established facts produce high impact and high likelihood for the in-scope reverse-proxy or tunnel deployment. Applying the required matrix gives high unless the critical criteria independently demand critical. They do not: the attack affects availability of one service or possibly its host filesystem, with no proven RCE, administrator takeover, sensitive-data extraction, or silent grade corruption. Therefore the original high rating is retained. The rating is conditional on the supplied remote deployment model; a strictly local Electron-only installation would materially reduce likelihood and could be suppressed as localhost-only, but that counterexample does not override the stated production threat model.

路径
Unauthenticated remote attacker --Sends credential-free multipart HTTP requests--> Operator reverse proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel --Forwards traffic to the loopback Express listener--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading/objective --Upload middleware runs before card lookup--> Disk-backed Multer array accepting up to 200 files at 20 MiB each --Persists completed uploads without aggregate-byte enforcement--> Persistent recognition upload directory --Files remain after 404, success, and recognition-error paths--> Disk, process, CPU, and request-capacity exhaustion

This is a real resource-exhaustion vulnerability in the product's batch objective-grading workflow, not merely a correctness defect. The React client normally submits multiple selected images to the affected route, establishing it as an intended runtime path. The route has no authentication middleware and installs recognitionUpload.array("files", 200) before readCard(). Its disk storage configuration limits each file to 20 MiB but has no aggregate-byte limit, so a single completed request can persist almost 4 GiB. An executable HTTP validation sent two 11 MiB files without credentials to a nonexistent card, received the application's 404 response, and observed both files remaining after shutdown; another request demonstrated acceptance and retention of 200 parts. A valid-card validation also showed serial recognizer invocation and retention after HTTP 200. The strongest impact counterevidence is that each file and each request are finitely bounded, recognition within one request is serial rather than parallel, and Multer removes partial files when a request aborts during multipart parsing. Those controls do not cover completed requests, repeated requests, or completed uploads handed to the handler. The strongest exposure counterevidence is the direct 127.0.0.1 bind and local-desktop documentation. Consequently, remote reportability depends on the reverse-proxy or tunnel deployment specified by the supplied threat model; under that in-scope deployment, no credential, valid card, race, or privileged precondition is required for persistent disk consumption.

发生可能性
High - Under the supplied public proxy or tunnel deployment, exploitation requires only ordinary unauthenticated multipart HTTP requests. A card ID is not required for the proven persistent-disk path, and the executable validation reached the real route without credentials and retained completed files. Repetition is straightforward because no rate, quota, or concurrency control exists. The primary uncertainty is deployment exposure: the repository itself binds to loopback and documents a local desktop mode, so likelihood would be low or non-reportable for installations that are never proxied. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - Repeated completed requests can persist attacker-controlled data until the service or host filesystem is exhausted, making answer-card design, scanning, or grading unavailable. One request can approach 3.91 GiB before multipart overhead. For valid cards, up to 200 sequential 30-second native invocations can additionally hold a request for approximately 100 minutes and consume CPU and process capacity. The demonstrated impact is availability loss; repository evidence does not establish confidentiality loss, grade corruption, code execution, or fleet-wide impact.
假设条件
The service is deployed according to the supplied production threat model, with its loopback listener published through a reverse proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel.
No out-of-repository proxy rule adds authentication, aggregate request-size limits, upload quotas, or rate limits to this endpoint.
The Node process has write access to a finite ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR, as required by the normal card and recognition workflow.
HTTP reachability to the Express service through the deployment's reverse proxy or tunnel
Ability to transmit one or more completed multipart requests
A valid card ID only for the optional native-recognizer amplification branch; persistent disk consumption works with an arbitrary nonexistent card ID
控制措施
Direct server listener is bound to 127.0.0.1; remote reachability requires a proxy or tunnel.
Multer limits each individual recognition upload to 20 MiB.
The array middleware limits the named files field to 200 files per request.
safeId removes non-alphanumeric, underscore, and hyphen characters from cardId before constructing the upload directory.
Multer 2.0.2 removes partial uploads when a request aborts while multipart parsing is still active.
Native recognizer invocations use argument arrays rather than a shell and have a 30-second per-process timeout.
Files within one request are processed serially, limiting that request to one active recognizer child at a time.
No authentication, authorization, aggregate request-byte limit, completed-upload cleanup, request rate limit, storage quota, or global recognition-concurrency control is present in the affected service.
盲点
No reverse-proxy, Cloudflare Tunnel, container, Kubernetes, or load-balancer manifest exists at the analyzed commit, so actual public exposure and any external body-size or rate controls cannot be verified from this checkout.
The repository does not establish the capacity, quota configuration, partitioning, or blast radius of the production filesystem.
The native Windows recognizer was unavailable in the Linux validation environment; the approximately 100-minute workload follows from the verified serial loop and source-visible 30-second timeout rather than a full 200-process production stress test.
Multer's upload-phase abort cleanup was validated as counterevidence; retention after a disconnect that occurs only after multipart completion was not independently stress-tested.
Concurrent batch behavior, host process limits, reverse-proxy request timeouts, and production monitoring or automatic cleanup outside this repository are unknown.
