## 24. Unbounded OCR DPI enables unauthenticated memory exhaustion

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/dd298a78fd4c81918f01179a624f42b8?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
创建 Pull Request
Unbounded OCR DPI enables unauthenticated memory exhaustion
严重级别
High

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
577623c
8:04 PM Jun 10, 2026

操作者：chuh

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
补丁
聊天
概要

This commit introduces the vulnerable recognition route and native processing path. Previously, the server did not expose a request-controlled DPI to an OpenCV allocation. The new route's positive-only validation, combined with the native recognizer's unchecked dimension calculation and unrestricted process spawning, introduces the denial-of-service condition.

The newly added `/api/cards/:cardId/recognition/objective` route has no authentication middleware and accepts `dpi` from the multipart body or query string. It validates only that the value is finite and positive. An unauthenticated attacker can obtain a usable card through the likewise unauthenticated card-creation route, upload a small image containing the expected six markers, and request an excessive but valid integer DPI. The DPI is passed directly to the native recognizer. The recognizer computes dimensions as `page_mm / 25.4 * dpi` and passes those dimensions to `cv::warpPerspective`, which allocates a color destination image and processing intermediates. For example, 3000 DPI produces approximately 24,803 by 35,079 pixels, requiring about 2.6 GB for one three-channel output matrix alone. The Node wrapper permits unlimited parallel child processes; its 30-second timeout occurs only after allocation and does not impose memory, process, or concurrency limits. Repeated concurrent requests can therefore exhaust RAM and CPU, trigger system-wide paging or OOM termination, and make the examination service unavailable. The DPI should be constrained to a small supported range, output pixel count should be checked before allocation, recognition should have bounded concurrency, and the route should require appropriate authorization and rate limiting.

验证
 Reachability and prerequisites: verified credential-free card creation and recognition requests through the real Express interface; confirmed the created default layout contains required markers and objective fields.
 Input/control flow: verified dpi=3000 passes the positive/finite route check and reaches a spawned recognizer as the exact --dpi 3000 argument, with no native upper bound.
 Vulnerable sink: traced the DPI-derived 24,803 x 35,079 dimensions into cv::warpPerspective and calculated a 2,610,193,311-byte three-channel destination matrix.
 Resource controls and concurrency: verified three HTTP requests spawned three overlapping child PIDs and found no concurrency, process, memory, rate, or pixel-count limit; the 30-second timer starts only after spawning.
 Native runtime impact: did not execute the Windows/OpenCV production binary or observe OOM/service failure because no binary or compatible OpenCV build environment is present; exact host disruption remains environment-dependent.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The unchecked DPI-derived dimensions are used to allocate the OpenCV warped image.
- The native code accepts any positive DPI and derives potentially enormous output dimensions without a maximum-dimension or pixel-count check.
- An unauthenticated caller can create a valid card needed to reach the recognition processing path.
- The new unauthenticated route reads attacker-controlled DPI and checks only that it is finite and greater than zero before invoking the native recognizer.
- Each request spawns a new native process with the supplied DPI; there is no concurrency or resource limit, and the timeout acts only after 30 seconds.

### [native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/layout_io.cpp:221](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/577623c7269489e827fdcd523f409c9677b2c387/native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/layout_io.cpp#L221)

~~~~cpp
std::pair<int, int> a4_pixel_size(double width_mm, double height_mm, int dpi) {
    if (dpi <= 0) {
        throw std::runtime_error("DPI must be positive");
    }
    return {
        static_cast<int>(std::llround(width_mm / 25.4 * dpi)),
        static_cast<int>(std::llround(height_mm / 25.4 * dpi)),
    };
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/recognition.ts:44](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/577623c7269489e827fdcd523f409c9677b2c387/src/apps/answer-card/server/recognition.ts#L44)

~~~~typescript
export async function recognizeObjectiveAnswers(request: RecognitionRequest): Promise<RecognitionResult> {
  const exePath = resolveRecognizerExe();
  const args = [
    "--image",
    request.imagePath,
    "--layout",
    request.layoutPath,
    "--page",
    String(request.pageNumber),
    "--dpi",
    String(request.dpi)
  ];
  if (request.debugDir) {
    args.push("--debug-dir", request.debugDir);
  }

  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const child = spawn(exePath, args, {
      windowsHide: true,
      stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"]
    });

    const stdoutChunks: Buffer[] = [];
    const stderrChunks: Buffer[] = [];
    let timedOut = false;
    const timeout = setTimeout(() => {
      timedOut = true;
      child.kill();
    }, 30_000);

    child.stdout.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => stdoutChunks.push(chunk));
    child.stderr.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => stderrChunks.push(chunk));
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The mechanically established facts are impact=high and likelihood=high. The required matrix maps that pair to critical only when immediate critical criteria are met; otherwise it maps to high. Critical is not justified because the demonstrated consequence is availability loss affecting a single service or host, the real native OOM was not executed, and remote exposure depends on the supplied reverse-proxy/tunnel deployment model. Hard suppression does not apply: impact is not self-only, the vulnerable route is not privileged or operator-only, its prerequisites are plausible, and the supplied threat model establishes a realistic unauthenticated remote path. The result remains a reportable high-severity vulnerability.

路径
Unauthenticated remote attacker --Sends unauthenticated HTTP requests--> Production reverse proxy or tunnel --Forwards requests to the loopback service--> Unauthenticated card creation and recognition routes --Accepts dpi > 0 and invokes recognition--> Node recognizer wrapper spawning one child per request --Spawns an unrestricted child with --dpi--> Native unchecked DPI-to-dimensions calculation --Supplies unchecked output dimensions--> OpenCV warpPerspective allocation --Allocates multi-gigabyte matrices concurrently--> Service or host resource exhaustion

This is a real runtime resource-exhaustion vulnerability in the answer-card recognition product path. Express is composed without authentication middleware, and both card creation and objective recognition are unauthenticated. The recognition route converts attacker-controlled dpi input with Number and accepts every finite value greater than zero. The wrapper then starts a separate native recognizer for each request and passes the DPI unchanged. Native code only rejects non-positive DPI, calculates pixel dimensions from the page's physical size, and supplies those dimensions to cv::warpPerspective. For the default A4 layout, 3000 DPI requests a 24,803 by 35,079 three-channel destination consuming about 2.43 GiB before intermediates. The executable validation artifact confirmed unauthenticated card creation, exact --dpi 3000 propagation, and three overlapping children, although it deliberately used a harmless stand-in recognizer and did not reproduce an actual OOM. Existing controls—a 20 MiB upload limit, marker and homography prerequisites, exception handling, and a 30-second kill timer—do not bound destination dimensions or prevent concurrent allocation. No secrets are involved; the crossed boundary is from an unauthenticated network request into native host resource consumption. The strongest exposure counterevidence is the explicit 127.0.0.1 binding and local-only README/Electron workflow. Remote reportability therefore depends on the supplied production reverse-proxy/tunnel model; without that deployment condition, this would ordinarily be a low-likelihood localhost issue.

发生可能性
High - Under the supplied production proxy/tunnel model, exploitation is remote and unauthenticated. Card creation, layout retrieval, and PDF rendering are also unauthenticated, making the marker-image prerequisite achievable, while the DPI parameter and request concurrency are directly attacker-controlled. The runnable validation proved the HTTP-to-child boundary and overlapping spawns. Likelihood is reduced in deployments that use only the packaged loopback Electron workflow, and external proxy or OS quotas remain unknown, but those controls are not present in repository code. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - A single 3000 DPI request for the default A4 page asks OpenCV for an approximately 2.43 GiB three-channel destination matrix before processing intermediates. Multiple overlapping child processes can multiply memory and CPU pressure, induce paging or OOM termination, and disrupt recognition or other services on the same host. Availability during scanning and grading is operationally important under the supplied threat model. The exact failure behavior is host-dependent and no real native OOM was executed, so the finding does not establish fleet-wide or persistent compromise.
假设条件
The deployment follows the supplied production threat model and forwards the loopback-bound Node service through a reverse proxy or Cloudflare tunnel; no ingress configuration is present in the analyzed commit itself.
The native answer-card recognizer is installed or configured through ANSWER_CARD_RECOGNIZER_EXE as expected by the packaged production workflow.
The attacker can submit a decodable image containing at least four suitably positioned markers. This is plausible because the unauthenticated API can create a default card and return its layout and rendered PDF.
No external reverse-proxy rate limit, OS job object, cgroup, or equivalent memory/process quota exists outside the repository; such deployment controls cannot be established from the checkout.
The loopback service is made remotely reachable by the production reverse proxy or tunnel described in the supplied threat model
The native recognizer executable is installed
The attacker creates or identifies a card containing objective options
The uploaded image is decodable and yields at least four marker matches
One or more recognition requests specify a large positive integer DPI
控制措施
The server binds to 127.0.0.1; remote reachability requires the reverse proxy or tunnel from the supplied production threat model.
Multer limits each recognition source upload to 20 MiB.
Recognition requires an existing card and layout with objective options.
The native path requires a decodable image, suitable marker candidates, at least four marker matches, and a valid homography.
The wrapper attempts to kill each child after 30 seconds, but only after the child has started and may already have allocated memory.
Native recognition catches std::exception and may return a failed result for some OpenCV allocation failures.
There is no application authentication, authorization, DPI maximum, output-pixel limit, rate limit, concurrency limit, process quota, or memory quota.
盲点
The analyzed commit contains no reverse-proxy, Cloudflare tunnel, load-balancer, or other public-ingress manifest; remote exposure comes from the supplied production threat model.
The committed server and Electron configuration bind only to loopback, so a deployment that strictly follows only those artifacts would not expose the route directly to remote attackers.
The native Windows recognizer was not present as a runnable binary and could not be built in the Linux validation environment because OpenCV development dependencies were unavailable.
The executable PoC used a harmless stand-in recognizer; it proved unauthenticated parameter propagation and concurrent child creation but not an actual allocation failure, OOM kill, or service outage.
OpenCV and Windows memory-allocation behavior, available RAM/pagefile, and the number of requests needed for disruption vary by deployment.
External reverse-proxy throttling, endpoint protection, Windows job objects, container limits, or host-level quotas are not represented in the repository and could reduce exploitability.

## 25. Participant search bypasses teacher student-list scope

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/70ed04f7151c8191bacb370fd84b2fd6?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Participant search bypasses teacher student-list scope
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
7069716
6:17 PM Sep 1, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. This commit adds the participant-search route and its global search helper. The helper receives examId as _examId but never uses it, while the route does not apply the existing can_view_students permission-matrix gate.

An authenticated teacher with access to any exam and the ordinary GRADE_READ permission can call GET /api/exams/:examId/participant-search. The route applies requireExamAccess and a coarse role permission, but omits the established makeViewPermissionGate("can_view_students") control. More importantly, searchStudentsForExam deliberately ignores its examId argument and queries every active student account without joining the target exam, its participant roster, the teacher's assigned classes, or the teacher permission matrix. Consequently, a subject or head teacher can retrieve IDs, names, and student numbers belonging to other classes and grades. The LIMIT 20 clause does not prevent enumeration because an attacker can repeatedly query student-number prefixes or name fragments, and the threat model states there is no general API rate limit. This conflicts with the existing exam student-search implementation, which both applies can_view_students and limits results to students scored in the specified exam. The result is a teacher-to-student/class authorization boundary bypass and disclosure of a potentially school-wide student directory.

验证
 Route is authenticated yet reachable by a non-admin teacher with exam access and ordinary GRADE_READ/EXAM_READ permissions.
 Route omits the fine-grained can_view_students gate used by the comparable exam student-search endpoint; the control endpoint returned HTTP 403.
 Attacker-controlled q reaches a query over all active students, and examId/class/grade/teacher scope is not used in that query.
 Real HTTP requests returned IDs, names, and student numbers for students in another class and grade.
 LIMIT 20 does not enforce authorization scope; two prefix searches enumerated all 25 seeded out-of-scope students.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The newly added route is available to teachers with coarse GRADE_READ permission but does not enforce the can_view_students permission-matrix gate before returning student identities.
- The helper ignores _examId and searches the complete active-student users table, with no exam, class, grade, or teacher-scope restriction.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:2453](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/70697163f9c456677f8ad4499b65b832e9032810/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L2453)

  // 原实现走 /api/users（USER_MANAGE 管理员专属），教师 403 后前端静默空白；
  // 此接口对可读考试的教师开放，只搜学生角色且启用中的账号。
  app.get("/api/exams/:examId/participant-search", requireExamAccess, requirePermission(PERMISSIONS.GRADE_READ), async (req, res, next) => {
    try {
      const examId = Number(req.params.examId);
      if (!Number.isInteger(examId) || examId <= 0) {
        res.status(400).json({ message: "无效的考试 ID" });
        return;
      }
      const q = (req.query.q as string ?? "").trim();
      if (!q) {
        res.json({ ok: true, students: [] });
        return;
      }
      const students = await searchStudentsForExam(getMysqlDb(), examId, q);
      res.json({ ok: true, students });

### [src/server/services/examParticipants.ts:39](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/70697163f9c456677f8ad4499b65b832e9032810/src/server/services/examParticipants.ts#L39)

~~~~typescript
export async function searchStudentsForExam(
  db: DbAdapter,
  _examId: number,
  q: string
): Promise<Array<{ id: number; name: string; student_number: string | null }>> {
  const keyword = (q ?? "").trim();
  if (!keyword) return [];
  const escaped = keyword.replace(/[\\%_]/g, (m) => `\\${m}`);
  const rows = await db.all(
    `SELECT u.id, u.name, u.student_number
     FROM users u
     WHERE u.role_id = ? AND u.is_active = 1
       AND (u.student_number LIKE ? ESCAPE '\\' OR u.name LIKE ? ESCAPE '\\')
     ORDER BY u.student_number
     LIMIT 20`,
    ROLE_IDS.STUDENT, `${escaped}%`, `%${escaped}%`
  ) as Array<{ id: number; name: string; student_number: string | null }>;
  return rows.map((r) => ({ id: r.id, name: r.name, student_number: r.student_number }));
~~~~


攻击路径分析

No hard suppression applies: the impact is not self-only; the preconditions are plausible; the attacker is a lower-privileged teacher rather than an administrator, operator, developer, or local host user; and the issue crosses an explicit student-list and class/grade authorization boundary in a production workflow. Repository deployment guidance supports remote reachability even though the process binds to loopback. Impact is medium because identifiable student directory records can be enumerated across the installation, while grades, credentials, write integrity, and availability are unaffected. Likelihood is high because ordinary teachers have the required coarse permissions by default and the real-interface PoC succeeded. Applying the required matrix mechanically, impact=medium and likelihood=high yields medium, retaining the original severity.

路径
Documented public HTTPS tunnel or reverse proxy --Teacher reaches the normal web/API service and signs in--> Authenticated subject/head teacher with an accessible exam --Chooses any accessible exam and controls examId and q--> GET /api/exams/:examId/participant-search?q=<prefix> --Coarse authorization checks succeed--> EXAM_READ, requireExamAccess, and GRADE_READ pass --No student-list permission-matrix check is applied--> Missing can_view_students permission gate --examId is ignored and q reaches a parameterized global search--> Unscoped SELECT from all active student users --Up to 20 identities per request are returned and prefixes permit enumeration--> Cross-class and cross-grade student directory disclosure

Attacker story: a subject teacher, or an attacker controlling that teacher's account, signs in through the normal web service and selects any exam the account is allowed to access. The attacker sends participant-search requests with successive student-number prefixes or name fragments. The route passes the global EXAM_READ gate, requireExamAccess, and GRADE_READ, but does not consume the can_view_students permission matrix. Its helper deliberately receives the exam ID as _examId and queries all active student-role rows without an exam-participant, class, grade, teacher-class, or teacher-permission join. The HTTP response directly exposes each matching student's ID, name, and student number. The supplied executable PoC exercised the real authenticated Express interface with production-default authentication, obtained HTTP 200 and 20 out-of-scope records, and enumerated all 25 seeded outsiders with two prefixes; the comparable protected search returned HTTP 403 and an inaccessible exam also returned HTTP 403. No credentials or application secrets are returned, and there is no executable sink or write effect. The strongest scope challenges are the loopback bind, the documented need for administrator-defined cross-grade rosters, authentication, effective exam-access enforcement, active-student filtering, parameterized SQL, and the 20-row cap. Public tunnel/proxy documentation defeats the localhost-only challenge; the administrator-oriented cross-grade workflow does not justify bypassing an explicit can_view_students=0 decision for ordinary teachers; and prefix enumeration defeats the row cap as a boundary. This is therefore a reportable confidentiality authorization bypass rather than a correctness-only defect.

发生可能性
High - Exploitation requires only a normal authenticated teacher with access to one exam and default teacher read permissions. The route is used by the ordinary web workflow, documented deployments can expose it remotely through a public tunnel or proxy, attack complexity is low, and the supplied executable PoC verified cross-scope disclosure and prefix enumeration. Authentication and exam visibility narrow the attacker population, but the required privilege and conditions are realistic for the threat model. | Remote network vector
影响程度
Medium - The endpoint discloses identifiable school-directory data—stable account IDs, names, and student numbers—across configured teacher, class, grade, and student-list authorization boundaries, potentially at installation-wide scale through repeated searches. The endpoint does not expose grades, passwords, answer images, or secrets and does not modify records, so confidentiality impact is meaningful but not high.
假设条件
The deployment contains more than one class or grade and active student accounts outside the attacker's assigned scope.
The attacker has a legitimate or compromised teacher account with the ordinary EXAM_READ and GRADE_READ capabilities and access to at least one exam.
Remote exposure depends on following the repository's documented Cloudflare Tunnel or Nginx deployment guidance; the Node process itself binds to loopback.
Student names, student numbers, and stable account IDs are protected school-directory data under the supplied threat model.
An authenticated teacher account
EXAM_READ and GRADE_READ permissions
Access to any valid, non-deleted exam
A non-empty attacker-controlled q query matching active student records
Out-of-scope student accounts in the same installation
控制措施
Authentication enforcement is enabled by default and requests without a valid identity are rejected.
The global /api/exams gate requires EXAM_READ for this GET endpoint.
requireExamAccess rejects exams outside the teacher's visible exam set and hides soft-deleted exams.
The route additionally requires GRADE_READ.
Forced-password-change accounts are blocked from other API routes.
Only active users with the student role are returned.
The SQL uses bound parameters and escapes LIKE wildcard characters, preventing SQL injection and direct wildcard-only dumping.
Each request is limited to 20 rows.
The missing control is makeViewPermissionGate("can_view_students"), which is present on the comparable student-search endpoint.
No endpoint-specific or general API rate limit prevents repeated prefix enumeration.
盲点
The supplied dynamic validation used the supported SQLite backend; MariaDB was not separately executed, although the shared middleware and adapter-level query path make a dialect-specific authorization difference unlikely.
Actual deployments may omit the documented public tunnel or reverse proxy, reducing network scope to the local host or school network.
The number and sensitivity of real student records depend on each school's deployment and directory size.
Cross-class and cross-grade explicit rosters are documented as an administrator use case, so the intended candidate-search breadth should be clarified; this does not resolve the demonstrated bypass of can_view_students for ordinary teachers.
No production audit logs or real school data were inspected.

## 26. Remote sync failure permits grading with stale card data

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/b1eaf2a22084819191b7d7e4e4829617?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Remote sync failure permits grading with stale card data
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
afb50c9
1:06 PM Aug 31, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This commit introduces a grading-integrity fail-open in the new scanner synchronization path. Remote authentication failures and reachable 404 responses fail closed, but availability failures fall back to an unversioned local card and the caller discards the fallback source before starting local grading.

When a remote server is configured, fetchSynced() treats every network error, timeout, HTTP 5xx response, and even successful responses with invalid JSON as a reason to silently fetch the same object from the local embedded server. Although the helper returns a source indicator, fetchCardByIdSynced() discards it. ScannerApp therefore cannot distinguish an authoritative remote card from an offline cached card: it imports the returned full card into the local database and enters the workspace. The workspace subsequently grades images through the local /api/cards/:cardId/grading endpoint, which uses that local card's layout and answer key. A realistic sequence is that a card is changed or deleted on the school server, its older definition remains on the scanner, and an adjacent/on-path attacker blocks the remote detail request immediately before selection. HTTPS does not prevent traffic from being dropped. The scanner then accepts the stale definition without a per-card warning or confirmation; if the list was fetched remotely just before the interruption, the footer can also continue to say that remote synchronization succeeded. This can produce plausible-looking but incorrect OCR alignment or objective scores under the unchanged card ID. The authoritative detail fetch should fail closed when remote mode is configured, or return and enforce freshness metadata such as a version/checksum. Explicit offline use should require confirmation that identifies the cached card version, and uploads should carry that version so the server can reject mismatched scans.

验证
 Remote-configured card-detail retrieval falls back to the local endpoint for network failure, HTTP 5xx, and invalid JSON, while 401/403/404 fail closed.
 The card-detail caller discards fallback provenance and permits local import/workspace entry without a stale-cache warning, confirmation, or freshness check.
 A same-ID stale local card is accepted and the local grading path uses its layout and answer key; the reproduction demonstrated a 10-versus-0 score difference.
 Focused execution of the real sync/import/grading modules and the repository's existing smoke harness reproduced the relevant behavior and controls.
 Preconditions and reachability were established: configured remote, divergent local cache, operator selection/grading, and an unauthenticated on-path attacker able to interrupt the detail request.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The workspace grades imported images through the embedded local card endpoint, making the cached card definition authoritative for the displayed grading result.
- All non-authentication/non-404 remote failures silently fall back to local data, and fetchCardByIdSynced discards the returned source indicator.
- Card selection accepts the potentially stale fallback card, upserts it locally, and enters the workspace without checking its source, version, or freshness.

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/lib/scannerSync.ts:28](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/afb50c9a5b3ccec0ff147d5263048db16e26443c/src/apps/answer-card/client/lib/scannerSync.ts#L28)

~~~~typescript
async function fetchSynced<T>(localPath: string): Promise<{ data: T; source: SyncSource }> {
  const base = getRemoteBase();
  if (base) {
    const remotePath = toRemotePath(localPath);
    try {
      const res = await remoteScannerFetch(remotePath, {
        headers: getStoredApiKey() ? { "X-Api-Key": getStoredApiKey()! } : undefined,
      });
      if (!res.ok) {
        const status = res.status;
        let msg = res.statusText;
        try { const body = (await res.clone().json()) as any; msg = body?.message || body?.error || msg; } catch {}
        // 401/403 鉴权失败不回退；404 权威删除不回退；网络/5xx 才回退
        if (status === 401 || status === 403) {
          throw Object.assign(new Error(msg), { status, remoteAuthFailed: true });
        }
        if (status === 404) {
          throw Object.assign(new Error(msg), { status, remoteNotFound: true });
        }
        throw Object.assign(new Error(msg), { status });
      }
      return { data: (await res.json()) as T, source: "remote" };
    } catch (e: any) {
      if (e?.remoteAuthFailed || e?.remoteNotFound || e?.status === 401 || e?.status === 403 || e?.status === 404) throw e;
      // 网络错误 / 5xx / 超时 -> 静默回退本地
    }
  }
  const data = await fetchJson<T>(localPath);
  return { data, source: base ? "offline-cache" : "local" };
}

export const fetchCardsSynced = () => fetchSynced<any[]>("/api/cards?limit=500");
export const fetchCardByIdSynced = async (id: string) => (await fetchSynced<any>(`/api/cards/${encodeURIComponent(id)}`)).data;
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/ScannerApp.tsx:128](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/afb50c9a5b3ccec0ff147d5263048db16e26443c/src/apps/answer-card/client/ScannerApp.tsx#L128)

~~~~tsx
        onSelectCard={async (cardId) => {
          if (validatingCardId) return;
          setValidatingCardId(cardId);
          try {
            const card = (await fetchCardByIdSynced(cardId)) as { title?: string };
            // 远端新建的卡不在本机库：直扫/阅卷只读本机服务，先进本机库（幂等 upsert）
            if (card && typeof (card as { id?: string }).id === "string") {

~~~~


攻击路径分析

The separate policy pass does not trigger hard suppression: impact is not self-only, the attacker does not need operator or administrator privilege, and the preconditions are plausible rather than unachievable. The authorized operator's click is victim interaction, while the attacker-controlled action is unauthenticated network interruption. Repository and executable evidence establish the full fallback-to-grading chain, but exposure is limited to an internal Electron scanner workflow and the attacker needs an adjacent/on-path position plus a divergent cache. Impact is high because the stale layout or answer key can alter a batch of grading results; likelihood is medium because of the local-network vector and timing/state preconditions. Applying the mandated matrix mechanically, high impact multiplied by medium likelihood yields medium adjusted criticality, so the original medium rating is retained.

路径
Unauthenticated adjacent/on-path attacker --drops or resets connection--> Outbound authenticated remote card-detail request --network error is caught--> Broad catch in fetchSynced --silently fetches local object--> Older same-ID card in embedded local database --source discarded; stale card accepted--> Local card and layout upsert --workspace grades against local definition--> Embedded local OCR and grading endpoint --stale answer key/layout determines scores--> Incorrect displayed/exportable grading result

Attacker story: while a school scanner has a current remote list but an older same-ID card cached locally, an adjacent/on-path attacker interrupts the card-detail request as an operator selects that card. The attacker needs no Project-X credentials and need not defeat HTTPS confidentiality; availability interference is sufficient. scannerSync catches all detail failures other than 401/403/404, obtains the local object, and then fetchCardByIdSynced removes the offline-cache provenance. ScannerApp upserts that object and enters the workspace, whose local grading endpoint uses the stale layout and answer key. The supplied executable reproduction imported the real synchronization and grading modules and demonstrated a 10-point stale-key result versus 0 under the authoritative key, while also confirming fallback for network errors, HTTP 500, and malformed JSON. This is a security-relevant integrity failure rather than only an accidental correctness bug because an unauthenticated actor across the network trust boundary can deliberately select stale trusted state. Strong counterevidence is that fallback is documented as intentional, list-level UI can show '离线·显示缓存', server health is separately indicated, 401/403/404 fail closed, the remote route has strong API-key/JWT authorization, and the embedded service is loopback-only. These controls do not resolve the finding: detail provenance is discarded, list status is not changed by detail fallback, health can lag or remain positive under selective failure, and no control blocks grading or validates a card revision. The repository proves incorrect local display and CSV-exportable results, but not direct persistence of those stale scores into the central score database; remote background upload carries cardId without a card version and may be processed separately by the authoritative server.

发生可能性
Medium - The attack requires no Project-X credentials but does require an adjacent/on-path position capable of interrupting outbound traffic, a divergent same-ID local cache, a remote change or deletion, and operator selection and grading during the failure window. These are conjunctive but realistic conditions for a managed school scanner with an intentional offline mode. Under the required network-scope weighting, the local-network vector and plausible preconditions produce medium likelihood.
影响程度
High - The stale card directly controls OCR geometry, objective answer keys, per-question scores, and total scores. The executable validation demonstrated a deterministic 10-versus-0 score divergence, and the workspace can process a batch and export its score table to CSV. This supports high potential grading-integrity impact, although direct mutation of the authoritative central score database was not demonstrated.
假设条件
The Electron scanner is configured for remote synchronization with a valid scanner/full API key.
The scanner's embedded database contains an older card under the same ID, while the authoritative remote card has subsequently changed or been deleted.
An adjacent or otherwise on-path attacker can drop or reset the remote card-detail request during card selection; alternatively, the remote service returns a 5xx response.
A scanner operator selects the affected card and starts grading before authoritative connectivity is restored or the stale definition is noticed.
The high-impact assessment assumes the locally displayed or CSV-exported batch grading result is consumed in the school's grading workflow; direct persistence of that stale result into the central score database is not established.
Remote scanner mode and server URL are configured
A valid scanner/full API key or other authorized remote-sync identity is present
A divergent same-ID card remains in the scanner's local database
The attacker can interrupt the authoritative detail request after or around card selection
The operator proceeds with image recognition and grading using the selected card
控制措施
Remote scanner synchronization is protected by scanner/full API-key authentication or an authenticated non-student JWT identity.
401 and 403 remote responses are propagated rather than falling back.
Authoritative 404 card-detail responses are propagated, leaving the operator on the selection page and refreshing the list.
The card-list footer can distinguish remote, local, and offline-cache list sources.
A separate server status indicator polls remote health.
The embedded Express service binds to 127.0.0.1, and the Electron renderer uses context isolation, sandboxing, and disabled Node integration.
API keys are sent in X-Api-Key and matched against hashed server-side records, although the scanner keeps its key in localStorage with a 30-day expiry.
HTTPS can protect request confidentiality and authenticity but cannot prevent an on-path actor from dropping the connection.
There is no detail-level provenance enforcement, immutable card revision, upload revision binding, or blocking offline confirmation.
盲点
No deployment manifests establish whether the configured remote school endpoint is Internet-facing, VPN-only, or restricted to a campus LAN.
The supplied executable reproduction mocks transport boundaries while importing production synchronization and grading modules; it does not perform a real packet drop against a packaged Electron build or run the native OCR executable.
Repository evidence does not establish how commonly scanner databases retain cards that diverge from the authoritative server.
The local workspace proves displayed and CSV-exportable stale scores, but direct persistence of those scores into the central authoritative score tables is not shown.
Background remote upload sends images and cardId without a revision; the reviewed path does not establish whether later server-side processing corrects, rejects, or duplicates the locally observed result.
The probability that an operator notices the connectivity indicator, stale card content, or anomalous scores during review is unknown.

## 27. MariaDB demo import creates fixed-password privileged users

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/21326d21a1488191bcd906f1c163d604?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
MariaDB demo import creates fixed-password privileged users
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
274b282
9:44 PM Aug 18, 2026

操作者：NaOH1g

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced: MariaDB administrators can now import fixed-credential demo teacher accounts into the live authentication and RBAC domain. The accounts should be restricted to an explicit non-production mode, use random one-time passwords with mandatory change, or be assigned a tightly scoped demo-only role. A post-creation warning is insufficient.

Before this range, POST /api/db/import-demo explicitly rejected MariaDB deployments. The commit removes that guard and invokes seedDemoData() directly, making the operation available on centralized MariaDB installations that may be exposed through the documented reverse-proxy or tunnel deployment model. The seed creates demo-teacher and demo-teacher-2 with the fixed password teacher123. It does not set teacher_role or password_change_required, so MariaDB defaults make each account active and immediately usable without a password change. An unauthenticated network attacker who knows the documented credentials can therefore log in whenever an administrator has imported demo data. Because these are plain teacher accounts, the compatibility behavior returns unrestricted exam visibility, and the default teacher role grants card, exam, and grade read/write capabilities. This can expose identifiable papers and unpublished academic records or permit broad score and exam modification. The import endpoint itself is correctly admin-only, so exploitation requires an administrator to use the feature; however, its warning about predictable credentials is returned only after the accounts have already been created and is not an enforcement mechanism. The risk is specifically introduced for MariaDB deployments by removal of the prior dialect rejection; SQLite already had the same unsafe demo-account behavior.

验证
 Confirm the commit newly allows POST /api/db/import-demo to execute on MariaDB without a production-mode restriction; the parent guard was removed and the HEAD route directly calls seedDemoData().
 Confirm the MariaDB seed creates predictable active teacher accounts without mandatory password change; fixed password insertion and MariaDB defaults establish this, and the resulting account state was observed dynamically on the shared path.
 Confirm the account authenticates through the production login flow and receives the claimed RBAC permissions; HTTP login returned 200, no reset requirement, and all six card/exam/grade read/write permissions.
 Confirm a plain seeded teacher can affect data outside the demo dataset; the account enumerated and successfully modified a separate non-demo unpublished formal exam.
 Use the strongest bounded realistic test and document gaps; the real Express interface was exercised end-to-end, while exact MariaDB execution was unavailable and is explicitly identified as the remaining proof gap.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- A teacher with no teacher_role receives null visibility restrictions, meaning all exams are visible under compatibility semantics.
- The default teacher role includes read and write access for cards, exams, and grades.
- Omitted fields default to password_change_required=0, is_active=1, and a null teacher_role, making the seeded users immediately active plain teachers.
- The changed route now supports MariaDB and immediately runs seedDemoData(); the predictable-credential warning is only sent after successful account creation.
- Creates two teacher-role users with the fixed password teacher123 while omitting password_change_required and teacher_role.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts:49](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/274b2823616075d9f5a70037e6ac429e2c47570e/src/apps/answer-card/server/middleware.ts#L49)

~~~~typescript
/**
 * Returns the set of exam IDs visible to the current teacher.
 * - admin / grade_leader → null (all visible)
 * - head_teacher → own classes + created exams
 * - subject_teacher → own subject + classes + created exams
 * - plain teacher (no teacher_role) → null (back-compat)
 *
 * #178 双模式：quiz（晨测）考试对教师全量可见（放开精细权限），
 * formal（大考）继续按 teacher_role + teacher_permissions 精细过滤。
 */
export async function getVisibleExamIds(user: express.Request["user"]): Promise<number[] | null> {
  if (!user || user.role_name === "admin") return null;
  if (user.role_name !== "teacher") return null;
  if (!user.teacher_role) return null; // plain teacher: all visible (back-compat)
~~~~

/**

### [src/server/services/DemoDataService.ts:332](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/274b2823616075d9f5a70037e6ac429e2c47570e/src/server/services/DemoDataService.ts#L332)

~~~~typescript
  const teacherPasswordHash = await hashPassword("teacher123");
  const created = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
    const gradeResult = await tx.run("INSERT INTO grades (name, sort_order, is_demo) VALUES (?, ?, 1)", "高一(演示)", 1);
    const gradeId = Number(gradeResult.lastInsertRowid);
    const class1Result = await tx.run("INSERT INTO classes (grade_id, name, sort_order, is_demo) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 1)", gradeId, "演示1班", 1);
    const class1Id = Number(class1Result.lastInsertRowid);
    const class2Result = await tx.run("INSERT INTO classes (grade_id, name, sort_order, is_demo) VALUES (?, ?, ?, 1)", gradeId, "演示2班", 2);
    const class2Id = Number(class2Result.lastInsertRowid);
    const insertTeacher = buildInsertIgnore(tx.dialect, "users", ["username", "password_hash", "name", "role_id", "subject", "is_demo"]);
    await tx.run(insertTeacher, "demo-teacher", teacherPasswordHash, "演示教师", ROLE_IDS.TEACHER, "数学", 1);
    await tx.run(insertTeacher, "demo-teacher-2", teacherPasswordHash, "演示教师乙", ROLE_IDS.TEACHER, "数学", 1);
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The technical consequence remains high: the fixed credential yields a normal teacher identity with broad read/write privileges and unrestricted exam visibility, and the executable validation demonstrated reading and modifying unrelated non-demo exam data through real HTTP routes. The final policy pass does not suppress the issue as privileged-only because the administrator is not the attacker: the privileged import creates an enduring authentication weakness that a lower-privileged, unauthenticated remote attacker subsequently exploits, producing a verified identity and authorization escalation. However, likelihood is medium rather than high because exploitation depends on a deliberate administrator import in a MariaDB production deployment, continued account presence, network reachability, and absence of a conflicting username. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact multiplied by medium likelihood results in medium adjusted criticality.

路径
Unauthenticated remote attacker --Submits documented fixed credentials--> Public POST /api/auth/login --bcrypt verification succeeds for an active account--> Active MariaDB demo-teacher account with fixed password teacher123 --Login returns a session without forced password change--> Authenticated teacher session with card/exam/grade read-write permissions --Compatibility visibility logic returns null restriction--> Null teacher_role interpreted as all exams visible --RBAC and object-scope checks permit broad reads and writes--> Unrelated non-demo exam data and mutation endpoints

Attacker story: after a school administrator uses the MariaDB-capable demo-import button, an unauthenticated Internet user who knows the repository-documented credentials logs in as demo-teacher/teacher123. seedDemoData hashes that fixed password and inserts two normal teacher-role users without teacher_role, password_change_required, or is_active. MariaDB defaults make them active, exempt from mandatory password change, and plain teachers. The default teacher role grants card, exam, and grade read/write permissions, and getVisibleExamIds treats a teacher without teacher_role as unrestricted. The supplied executable validation exercised the real HTTP login, listing, and PATCH paths with authentication enabled, observed passwordChangeRequired=false and the expected permissions, read an unrelated non-demo draft formal exam, and modified it. That dynamic run used SQLite, but the MariaDB-specific reachability is supported statically by removal of the former dialect rejection, use of getMysqlDb, MariaDB INSERT IGNORE generation, and explicit MariaDB defaults. Countercontrols reduce likelihood but do not break the post-import attack path: import is admin-only and hidden under a developer submenu, login is rate-limited, accounts can be cleared, and the response warns against production use. The warning arrives after creation, the known password needs no guessing, and neither is_demo nor the clear operation limits access while the accounts remain.

发生可能性
High - Once the accounts exist, exploitation is a simple remote login with a known username and password and does not require brute force or prior authentication. Public exposure is a documented deployment model. Likelihood is reduced to medium because a highest-privilege administrator must first deliberately invoke a developer-mode demo import on MariaDB, the UI requires confirmation, the account may later be cleared, and a pre-existing username collision can alter the result. These are meaningful but plausible operational preconditions rather than an unachievable chain. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - A successful attacker obtains installation-wide teacher privileges rather than access limited to demo records. Repository RBAC grants card, exam, and grade read/write capabilities, and null teacher_role removes formal-exam visibility restrictions. This can disclose identifiable papers and unpublished academic records and permit modification of exam metadata and grading-related data. The impact crosses another user's identity and school-record boundaries and is not self-only.
假设条件
A privileged administrator invokes the product's demo-import workflow on a MariaDB-backed deployment.
The deployment is reachable by prospective users through the documented HTTPS reverse-proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel model.
The demo-teacher username is not already occupied by a conflicting row that causes MariaDB INSERT IGNORE to retain different credentials.
Authentication remains enabled, as it is by default; disabling authentication would make the fixed credential unnecessary rather than mitigate the broader exposure.
Administrator deliberately invokes POST /api/db/import-demo
Deployment uses the newly supported MariaDB path
Created demo account remains active and has not been removed with clear-demo
Web/API login surface is network-reachable
No incompatible pre-existing demo-teacher username collision
控制措施
POST /api/db/import-demo requires authenticated USER_MANAGE and SYSTEM_MANAGE permissions.
The import control is visible only to administrators under a developer-mode submenu and has a confirmation dialog.
Login verifies bcrypt hashes and active-account state.
Login is limited to 60 attempts per IP and 10 attempts per account per 15 minutes.
Normal API access requires authentication by default and enforces role permissions.
Accounts marked password_change_required are blocked from non-authentication APIs.
Demo rows are marked is_demo and an administrator-only clear-demo endpoint can remove them.
The server binds 127.0.0.1, although documented public reverse proxies and tunnels expose that listener.
The import response warns that the credentials are predictable and should not be used in production, but only after account creation.
盲点
The executable PoC validated the shared HTTP, authentication, RBAC, visibility, and mutation chain against SQLite because no MariaDB server or container runtime was available.
MariaDB-specific reachability is established statically through the removed route guard, dialect adapter, INSERT IGNORE generation, and schema defaults; completion of the entire seed against every supported MariaDB version was not dynamically observed.
Repository documentation proves supported public ingress patterns but cannot establish whether any particular deployment is currently Internet-exposed.
The repository cannot establish how often administrators use the developer-mode import workflow on production MariaDB installations.
INSERT IGNORE can preserve an existing demo-teacher row, so exploitability depends on the final state of that username in a specific installation.

## 28. Demo import installs documented school-wide teacher credentials

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/dc1ff6aaf29c81919f8cb436769fb268?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Demo import installs documented school-wide teacher credentials
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
57bc7c4
9:33 PM Jul 31, 2026

操作者：xiaoxu66609

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. Before this range, the demo seed was developer-operated test tooling. This range adds a server-side `DemoDataService`, an administrator-facing production endpoint, and a web UI action that install the fixed-credential accounts into the live application database. The accounts inherit ordinary teacher authorization rather than a demo-only scope.

On SQLite deployments, an administrator can now import demo data through the normal web UI and production API. The import creates `demo-teacher` and `demo-teacher-2` with the fixed password `teacher123`. The inserted rows omit both `teacher_role` and `password_change_required`, so the accounts are active plain-teacher accounts and are not forced to change their password. The credentials are disclosed by the API response and repository documentation. This is not limited to the imported demo exams: `getVisibleExamIds` intentionally returns unrestricted visibility for teachers with no `teacher_role`, and the default teacher role includes card, exam, and grade write permissions. After an administrator performs the apparently benign demo import, an unauthenticated Internet attacker can log in using the documented credentials, read school-wide records, modify scores and answer keys, or delete real exams. The administrator-only guard protects initiating the import but does not protect the powerful accounts subsequently exposed through the public login surface. Generate random one-time credentials, require password replacement, scope demo teachers strictly to demo exams/classes, or prevent these accounts from authenticating outside an explicit isolated demo mode.

验证
 Production SQLite demo import is reachable through the authenticated API/UI and creates fixed, publicly disclosed credentials: confirmed by HTTP 200 import response, source, UI, and README.
 Created demo teachers are active, authenticate with teacher123, and are not forced to change password: confirmed by database fields and HTTP 200 login with passwordChangeRequired=false.
 Their effective authorization is an ordinary plain teacher with broad permissions and no demo-only scope: confirmed by login permissions and the teacher_role=null visibility logic.
 A demo teacher can access and mutate non-demo academic data through real routes: directly demonstrated by GET, PATCH, and DELETE of an administrator-owned non-demo exam.
 Preconditions and limiting controls were assessed: import is administrator-only, SQLite-only, shown under developer mode, and reversible via clear-demo, but none protects the active fixed-credential account between import and cleanup.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The normal administration UI presents the operation as a non-overwriting demo-data import without warning that it enables publicly known privileged credentials.
- The imported accounts have no `teacher_role`; this compatibility rule consequently grants them visibility to all exams, not only demo exams.
- Ordinary teacher accounts receive card, exam, and grade write permissions, making compromise of the fixed demo account capable of altering or deleting real academic data.
- This range exposes the seed operation as a live administrator API and explicitly returns the fixed teacher username and password.
- The production service hashes the constant password `teacher123` and creates two teacher accounts without a scoped `teacher_role` or forced-password-change flag.

攻击路径分析

The inherent technical severity remains high because the verified unauthenticated-to-teacher transition exposes and permits destructive modification of non-demo academic records. Hard suppression does not apply: although account creation requires an administrator, the privilege-escalation delta is the issue—the administrator imports sample data but does not authorize an arbitrary remote user to obtain the resulting teacher identity. The condition is therefore not self-only, operator-only exploitation, or an unachievable protected write path. However, the admin interaction, SQLite restriction, developer-mode placement, cleanup option, loopback bind, and deployment-dependent public proxy make likelihood medium rather than high. Applying the required matrix mechanically, high impact multiplied by medium likelihood yields medium adjusted criticality.

路径
Administrator invokes SQLite demo import --seedDemoData inserts the fixed-credential teacher--> Active demo-teacher account with fixed teacher123 password --bcrypt verification accepts teacher123--> POST /api/auth/login --returns a teacher token with no forced password change--> Authenticated plain-teacher session --teacher_role is null--> getVisibleExamIds returns unrestricted null scope --requireExamAccess treats null as all exams visible--> Real exam read, patch, delete, score, and answer routes --reads results and mutates or deletes real academic records--> School-wide confidentiality, integrity, and availability impact

Project-X maps a React administration UI to an Express API backed by SQLite or MariaDB. On SQLite, the live /api/db/import-demo route calls DemoDataService, which bcrypt-hashes the constant teacher123 and inserts demo-teacher and demo-teacher-2 as active teachers. It omits teacher_role and password_change_required, whose schema defaults are NULL and 0. The API response and README disclose the credentials. A network attacker can therefore authenticate through the public login route after import without being forced to replace the password. The identity receives the default teacher write permissions, and the legacy plain-teacher rule returns null from getVisibleExamIds; requireExamAccess treats that value as authorization for every exam. Real exam endpoints then expose results and permit update or deletion, while score-editing routes permit grade and answer-key changes. The supplied isolated executable reproduction confirmed login and GET/PATCH/DELETE of a non-demo exam. Counterevidence is meaningful: only an administrator can initiate import, the UI nests it under developer mode, MariaDB rejects the endpoint, login is rate-limited, the service binds to loopback, and administrators can clear demo data. These controls lower probability but do not remove the post-import unauthenticated-to-teacher privilege escalation on deployments exposed through the repository's documented reverse proxy.

发生可能性
High - Once the account exists, exploitation is remote, low-complexity, requires no prior attacker account, and uses publicly documented credentials; the executable reproduction confirmed the path. Likelihood is reduced to medium because an administrator must first activate an explicitly developer-mode import, the deployment must use SQLite, the account must remain uncleared and unrotated, and actual public ingress is deployment-dependent despite being documented. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The imported identity can cross from demo data into non-demo school records. Repository routes support reading exam results, changing exam metadata, deleting exams, modifying student scores, and changing answer keys. This creates high confidentiality and integrity impact and potentially high availability impact for one school installation. It does not itself grant administrator authority or compromise a fleet.
假设条件
Remote classification assumes the operator follows the documented Nginx HTTPS reverse-proxy deployment; the Node process itself binds to 127.0.0.1:5174.
The SQLite administrator has invoked the product's demo-import action and has not subsequently cleared the demo data, disabled the accounts, or changed their passwords.
The installation retains the repository's default teacher permissions and plain-teacher compatibility semantics; administrators can customize role permissions.
The affected service is reachable by the attacker through the deployment's proxy or equivalent ingress.
SQLite deployment; the production endpoint rejects MariaDB
An authenticated administrator with USER_MANAGE and SYSTEM_MANAGE invokes POST /api/db/import-demo, directly or through the developer-mode UI
The imported demo accounts remain active with their fixed passwords unchanged
The Express web/API service is reachable through the documented reverse proxy or another ingress
Teacher permissions and plain-teacher visibility have not been administratively narrowed
控制措施
The import router requires authentication and USER_MANAGE, and the endpoint additionally requires SYSTEM_MANAGE.
The demo import endpoint rejects MariaDB and is limited to SQLite.
The administration UI hides the operation from non-admin users and places it under a developer-mode/high-risk section.
The UI presents a confirmation dialog before import.
Passwords are stored as bcrypt hashes rather than plaintext in the users table.
POST /api/auth/login is limited to ten attempts per IP per fifteen minutes.
Authentication issues random opaque session tokens and supports HttpOnly SameSite=Lax cookies.
A forced-password-change middleware exists, but it does not engage because imported accounts default password_change_required to 0.
Exam routes invoke requireExamAccess, but the control authorizes all exams when teacher_role is absent.
An administrator-only clear-demo endpoint deletes is_demo users and data.
The Node service binds to 127.0.0.1:5174, while deployment documentation recommends exposing it through an HTTPS reverse proxy.
盲点
Repository evidence documents public reverse-proxy deployment, but it cannot establish whether a specific installation is Internet-accessible.
Administrators can customize role permissions; the effective permissions of a particular deployment are not available statically.
The supplied reproduction covered login and non-demo exam GET/PATCH/DELETE; score and answer-key writes are source-traced but were not separately executed.
No runtime logs are available to establish how often production administrators use the demo-import feature or how long demo accounts remain installed.
The deployment guide suggests a HOST setting, while this commit's startServer implementation binds explicitly to 127.0.0.1; remote reach therefore depends on a reverse proxy or equivalent ingress.
Static repository analysis cannot determine whether an operator has independently rotated, disabled, or firewall-restricted the demo accounts.

## 29. Missing sheets can bind another student's page to the prior student

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/e4876018b58c819183dd38df7c718cf8?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Missing sheets can bind another student's page to the prior student
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
62ed9dc
12:24 PM Jul 19, 2026

操作者：Toyama Kasumi

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. Before this range, a page without recognized student digits remained unidentified. This commit adds ordinal-based batch wrapping and unconditional within-group student-ID inheritance, turning a missing-sheet condition into cross-student page attribution rather than leaving the page unrecognized.

`mapScanPageToLayout` derives the student group and layout page solely from the physical scan ordinal and the expected number of sheets. It does not verify a student boundary or detect missing, duplicated, or reordered sheets. `runOcrOnSession` then assigns any page without its own recognized student number to the ID previously recorded for the calculated group and gives that inherited identity a confidence of 0.9. The native recognizer intentionally does not look for a student number when the selected layout page has no student-number cells, so this mismatch can complete without an identity-recognition failure. For example, with a two-page single-sided card, scanning student A's first page, omitting A's second page, and then scanning student B's first page causes B's page to be treated as group 0/layout page 2. Because layout page 2 has no student-number cells, the page inherits A's ID and its recognition result and answer crops are persisted under A. This can be induced by a student submitting an incomplete packet or can arise from an ADF double-feed. It can corrupt A's score, lose B's answers, and disrupt the remaining batch without an explicit page-count or identity-boundary error. The implementation should fail closed or resynchronize at verified first-page identities rather than inferring identity solely from ordinal position; packet boundaries or machine-readable page/student identifiers should be validated before persisting grades and crops.

验证
 Confirm production mapping assigns group/layout solely from scan ordinal and permits a missing-sheet shift.
 Confirm a shifted first page selected as an ID-less later layout can complete without an identity failure.
 Confirm OCR orchestration inherits the preceding calculated-group ID and persists recognition and crops under it.
 Reproduce the two-page single-sided A1/B1 sequence through a focused production-service and database harness; native execution was replaced at its documented process boundary and the limitation is recorded.
 Confirm downstream grouping, HTTP result combination, and linked main-database score persistence consume the incorrect identity without a packet-boundary rejection.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- When the selected layout page has no student-number cells, recognition reports `not_present` and does not fail, allowing a physically different first page to pass under a non-first-page layout.
- Session results are grouped by the persisted student identity, combined into a student score, cached, and forwarded for persistence to the main exam database.
- The new mapping computes student groups and layout pages exclusively from the physical page ordinal and expected sheet count, with no validation of packet boundaries or missing sheets.
- Pages lacking their own recognized ID blindly inherit the calculated group's ID, receive confidence 0.9, and are persisted with that identity.
- Recognition scores and answer-block crops are persisted after identity inheritance; crops receive the potentially incorrect student number.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/scanner-service.ts:38](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/62ed9dc221bb1d5a69fb1a93334c489b4e089882/src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/scanner-service.ts#L38)

~~~~typescript
export function mapScanPageToLayout(
  physicalPage: number,
  side: "front" | "back",
  layoutPageCount: number,
  sided: "single" | "double"
): ScanPageMapping {
  const sidesPerSheet = sided === "single" ? 1 : 2;
  const sheetsPerStudent = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(layoutPageCount / sidesPerSheet));
  const sheetIndex = Math.max(0, physicalPage - 1);
  const groupIndex = Math.floor(sheetIndex / sheetsPerStudent);
  const sheetWithinGroup = sheetIndex % sheetsPerStudent;
  const sideOffset = sidesPerSheet === 1 || side === "front" ? 1 : 2;
  const layoutPage = sheetWithinGroup * sidesPerSheet + sideOffset;
  return { groupIndex, layoutPage, unusedSide: layoutPage > layoutPageCount };
~~~~

      const recognizedStudentId = recognition.studentId?.status === "ok" ? recognition.studentId.value : null;
      if (recognizedStudentId) {
        studentIdsByGroup.set(groupIndex, recognizedStudentId);
      }
      const inheritedStudentId = studentIdsByGroup.get(groupIndex) ?? null;
      const studentId = recognizedStudentId ?? inheritedStudentId;
      const inherited = !recognizedStudentId && Boolean(inheritedStudentId);
      if (inherited) {
        recognition.studentId = { status: "inherited", value: inheritedStudentId, source: "inherited" };
      }
      const studentConf = recognizedStudentId ? 0.9 : inherited ? 0.9 : 0.0;

攻击路径分析

The calibrated impact is high because the verified chain crosses student identities and reaches answers, crops, combined grades, and conditionally authoritative exam score rows. Likelihood is medium because omission or ADF loss is operationally plausible and a student can supply the triggering packet without software privileges, but exploitation depends on layout structure, scan ordering, successful mismatched-page registration, and lack of operator detection. Under the required matrix, high impact multiplied by medium likelihood yields medium. Hard suppression does not apply: this is not self-only, developer-only, or dependent on privileged access to the host or scanner. Although an authorized operator performs the scan, the adversarial input comes from a lower-privileged student's expected submission workflow and produces a verified cross-student identity and grade-integrity violation. The severity therefore remains medium rather than being suppressed or raised.

路径
Student A submits packet with an expected sheet missing --attacker-controlled omission enters a legitimate operator workflow--> Operator scans A followed by student B through the normal TWAIN workflow --scanner-provided page number is stored and used as the sole packet-position signal--> Physical page ordinal is mapped to group 0 and A's later layout page --wrong page-specific layout is selected--> Native recognizer sees no student-number cells and returns not_present without identity failure --not_present identity permits group inheritance--> runOcrOnSession inherits A's ID with confidence 0.9 and marks successful recognition done --inherited student ID is passed to OCR-result and crop persistence--> B's answers and crops are persisted under A's identity --persisted student_id controls grouping and downstream user lookup--> Results are grouped and potentially written to A's linked-exam score

A student can exploit an ordinary school scanning workflow by omitting an expected sheet from a multi-page packet before packets are scanned consecutively. The service stores the scanner driver's ordinal and calculates student groups and layout pages from that ordinal alone. If student B's first page occupies the slot expected for student A's later page, and that later layout has no student-number cells, the native recognizer deliberately returns studentId=not_present without failing. The service then inherits A's previously recognized ID, gives it confidence 0.9, marks the page done, and persists B's answers and crops under A. Grouping and downstream score persistence trust that stored identity. The executable validation harness exercised production mapping, scanner storage, grading, crop persistence, grouping, the results endpoint, and main score persistence; it stored both records, including B's physical page, as student 11111. The strongest technical counterevidence is that the shipped Windows recognizer was not run: the harness substituted a deterministic executable at the documented recognizer process boundary. This lowers confidence but is not dispositive because the exact C++ branch returns not_present when the selected layout page has no student digits, and the generated layouts had identical registration markers. A second qualification is that the current results endpoint uses a legacy file-backed card read; if that copy is absent, this particular main-score forwarding path returns 404. The incorrect scan identity, recognition result, confidence, answers, and crop attribution occur earlier and remain reachable. Authentication, localhost binding, and scanner-disable flags protect the operator API but do not validate the hostile physical packet supplied to an authorized scan.

发生可能性
Medium - The trigger is plausible in normal school operations: students can submit incomplete packets and ADF double-feeds can omit sheets without application access. Deliberate exploitation requires a multi-page layout with page-local identity only on the first page, favorable packet order, successful registration under the wrong page layout, and an operator who does not notice the omission. Localhost binding, scanner authentication, and server-package defaults do not block the hostile-paper path, but the physical workflow and configuration dependencies prevent a high-likelihood rating. The executable integration harness demonstrated all orchestration and persistence sinks, while use of a recognizer stub leaves a bounded native-image feasibility gap.
影响程度
High - The flaw crosses a student identity boundary and can silently associate one student's answers, handwritten answer crops, and grading data with another student. The persisted identity drives combined grading and can update authoritative student_scores and question_scores for a linked exam. A missing sheet can also shift interpretation of later packets in the batch. This is high integrity impact even though operators may detect anomalies and rescan, and the current legacy-card dependency can block one downstream main-score path in some installations.
假设条件
The affected card has multiple pages or sheets per student, with a student-number region on the first page but not on at least one later page.
Student A's first page is recognized successfully, student A's next expected sheet is missing, and student B's first page follows it in the same uninterrupted scan batch.
Registration markers and answer geometry allow student B's first page to be processed using the later-page layout; the generated two-page layout in the validation artifact had identical registration markers.
A teacher or scanner operator performs the ordinary scanning workflow and does not manually detect the missing sheet before OCR and result aggregation.
Main-exam score persistence additionally requires a readable card definition, a linked non-closed exam, and a user matching the inherited student number; incorrect scan records and answer-crop persistence do not require all of these downstream conditions.
A multi-page answer card whose later layout page has answer cells but no student-number cells
A valid recognized ID on student A's first page
Student A's expected subsequent sheet being omitted through deliberate incomplete submission, misordering, or ADF double-feed
Student B's first page immediately following the omission in the physical scan sequence
The mismatched physical page passing marker and image recognition under the selected later-page layout
An operator completing the ordinary scan and aggregation workflow
控制措施
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, scanner routes require a scanner-scoped API key or an authenticated account with GRADE_WRITE.
The service binds to 127.0.0.1 by default.
The server-only Ubuntu package defaults PROJECTX_ENABLE_SCANNER to 0.
The scanner can be explicitly disabled and then returns 404 for /api/scanner.
The native recognizer requires registration-marker roles and fails identity recognition when a selected layout page actually contains student-number cells but they cannot be read.
Pages beyond the configured layout-page count are treated as unused sides.
OCR exceptions are marked failed and pages without an assigned student ID are shown for review.
These controls do not detect a missing packet sheet; an inherited ID is assigned confidence 0.9 and the UI treats it as done.
盲点
The validation environment could not execute the shipped Windows PE recognizer because Wine and Linux OpenCV development files were unavailable. A deterministic executable was substituted at the documented ANSWER_CARD_RECOGNIZER_EXE boundary.
There is no end-to-end capture from a real TWAIN scanner proving that a particular physical first-page image passes the shipped recognizer under a later-page layout, although the C++ branch and identical generated registration markers support that result.
The validated main-exam persistence path supplied a legacy file-backed card copy because the results endpoint calls readCard; repository-backed installations without that copy may return 404 before combined result forwarding.
The exact frequency with which deployed card templates place student-number cells only on page one is not established beyond the production layout generated in validation.
Repository artifacts cannot establish operator procedures such as manual page counting, visual packet separation, post-scan reconciliation, or mandatory rescan policies.
Optional Cloudflare Tunnel deployment is documented, but actual ingress, scanner feature flags, and authentication settings for any installation are unknown and are not needed for the physical-input path.

## 30. Scanner remote-mode guide exposes credentials over HTTP

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/bb4738134740819191f3b5e307143d73?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Scanner remote-mode guide exposes credentials over HTTP
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
047b91e
9:12 PM Jun 28, 2026

操作者：xiaoxu66609

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. Although the client already accepted arbitrary HTTP server URLs, this range newly adds end-user instructions that actively recommend a plaintext LAN endpoint for the remote scanner workflow. That operational guidance makes transmission of high-value credentials and educational records without transport protection a supported deployment path.

The commit newly documents `http://192.168.1.100:5174` as the example server address immediately alongside the scanner API key, without requiring HTTPS or warning that HTTP is suitable only for loopback development. The client accepts that URL without validating its scheme and uses it as the base for all API traffic. Login requests transmit the username and password in the JSON body, while subsequent requests attach the scanner API key and bearer token as headers. Remote-mode uploads also send student answer-card images over the same connection. An attacker able to observe or modify traffic on the school LAN can therefore capture reusable teacher or administrator credentials, sessions, and scanner keys, read answer images, or tamper with requests. A captured scanner key can authenticate to the scanner upload routes, while captured user credentials or tokens grant the victim account's broader privileges. The guide should use an HTTPS example and explicitly require TLS for every non-loopback server; the client should also reject plaintext remote URLs except an intentional loopback-only development mode.

验证
 Verify that this commit newly places the plaintext non-loopback example and API-key instructions in an end-user-reachable guide.
 Verify that the closest URL control accepts the documented HTTP URL without TLS enforcement, loopback restriction, or warning.
 Demonstrate through the production transport helper that login credentials and the scanner API key reach an HTTP receiver in plaintext.
 Demonstrate that subsequent bearer/API-key requests and multipart answer-image content use the same plaintext base.
 Establish realistic reachability and impact by tracing the shipped scanner bundle, login token issuance, scanner upload routes, and reusable API-key authentication.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- All JSON requests to the configured base URL attach reusable bearer and scanner API-key credentials without enforcing a secure URL scheme.
- The login request sends the user's identifier and password through the configured API base, so following the HTTP example exposes account credentials in transit.
- Remote mode creates scanner sessions and uploads each scanned answer image through the same authenticated connection.
- Newly added remote-mode instructions pair a scanner API key with an explicit plaintext HTTP server example and provide no HTTPS requirement.

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/auth/api.ts:48](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/047b91efd57e23785101164081700bdbead80710/src/apps/answer-card/client/auth/api.ts#L48)

~~~~typescript
export async function fetchJson<T>(url: string, options?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
  const token = getAuthToken();
  // v1.6.0: 同时支持 Api-Key header
  const storedApiKey = (() => { try { return localStorage.getItem("projectx_api_key"); } catch { return null; } })();
  const headers = new Headers(options?.headers);
  if (token && !headers.has("Authorization")) {
    headers.set("Authorization", `Bearer ${token}`);
  }
  if (storedApiKey && !headers.has("X-Api-Key")) {
    headers.set("X-Api-Key", storedApiKey);
  }
  const response = await fetch(apiUrl(url), { ...options, headers });
~~~~
