      const createRes = await authFetch("/api/scanner/upload/sessions", {
        method: "POST",
        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
        body: JSON.stringify({
          cardId,
          name: `扫描_${cardId}_${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}`,
          dpi, paperSize, pageCount: pages.length,
        }),
      });
      if (!createRes.ok) throw new Error("创建远程会话失败");
      const { sessionId: remoteSessionId, uploadTokens } = await createRes.json() as { sessionId: string; uploadTokens: string[] };

      // Step 2: 逐页上传图片
      for (let i = 0; i < pages.length; i++) {
        const page = pages[i];
        const token = uploadTokens[i];
        setUploadMsg(`正在上传第 ${page.pageNum} 页 (${i + 1}/${pages.length})...`);

        // 获取本地图片并上传
        const imageRes = await authFetch(`/api/scanner/scan-image/${page.recordId}`);
        if (!imageRes.ok) continue;
        const blob = await imageRes.blob();

        const form = new FormData();
        form.append("image", blob, `page_${page.pageNum}.jpg`);
        form.append("token", token);
        form.append("pageNum", String(page.pageNum));
        form.append("side", page.side);

        const uploadRes = await authFetch(`/api/scanner/upload/sessions/${remoteSessionId}/pages`, {
          method: "POST",
          body: form,
        });
        if (!uploadRes.ok) {
          console.error(`Page ${page.pageNum} upload failed`);
        }
      }

      // Step 3: 标记完成
      await authFetch(`/api/scanner/upload/sessions/${remoteSessionId}/complete`, { method: "POST" });

~~~~markdown
### 5.4 扫描端远程模式

扫描端支持 **本地** 与 **远程** 双模，在扫描工作台上方切换：

| 模式 | 数据存储 | 扫描图片 | 答题卡来源 |
|------|---------|---------|-----------|
| **本地**（默认） | 本机 SQLite | 本机 | 本机数据库 |
| **远程** | 远端服务器 | 上传到远端 | 远端 API |

**远程模式配置**：在登录页展开 **「服务器连接（可选）」**，填入：

- **服务器地址**：如 `http://192.168.1.100:5174`
- **API Key**：管理员在账号设置 → API Key 中生成的扫描专用 Key

扫描完成后自动三步上传：创建会话 → 逐页上传图片 → 标记完成。
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The underlying consequence remains high because successful interception exposes reusable machine and user identities, answer images, and scanner-submission integrity. Likelihood is medium rather than high because the vector is local-network, user interaction is required, local mode is the default, and a reachable LAN proxy or listener must exist despite the repository server binding to 127.0.0.1. The separate deployment guide's HTTPS requirement is meaningful counterevidence but is not dispositive because the in-product guide and placeholder explicitly recommend plaintext remote operation and the client does not enforce TLS. This is not hard-suppressed: the attacker is an unauthenticated lower-privileged LAN party, the impact is not self-only, and following the documented workflow is a plausible product action rather than an attacker needing privileged write access. Applying the required matrix mechanically, impact=high and likelihood=medium produces medium final criticality. The result remains reportable.

路径
Scanner operator following the in-product remote-mode guide --enters the documented HTTP URL and scanner key, then logs in or scans--> Scanner client configured with plaintext HTTP base URL --transmits authentication material and educational records without TLS--> Unauthenticated on-path school-LAN attacker --observes or modifies plaintext requests and responses--> Captured password, API key, bearer token, upload token, and answer image --replays valid scanner or user credentials and can alter scanner submissions--> Project-X login and scanner upload APIs

Attacker story: a school scanner operator follows the guide bundled into the application, configures http://192.168.1.100:5174 with an administrator-issued scanner key, and logs in or uploads answer cards. The URL is accepted without scheme validation. The production request helper then sends the password body, X-Api-Key, bearer token, per-page upload token, and scanned images over ordinary HTTP. An unauthenticated on-path LAN attacker can capture reusable credentials and educational records or alter scan traffic. The supplied executable validation exercised the production URL and header helper and observed each claimed data class at an HTTP receiver. Repository controls include loopback server binding, local mode as the default, scoped/revocable API keys, authenticated upload routes, and a separate deployment guide requiring HTTPS. Those controls reduce likelihood but do not protect a user who follows the conflicting in-product HTTP instructions. The issue is reportable because it crosses a real LAN trust boundary and compromises identities and student data; it is not a self-only, developer-only, or attacker-privileged path.

发生可能性
Medium - Exploitation is technically simple and requires no Project-X account once a plaintext remote endpoint is used, and the exact unsafe URL is promoted in both the bundled guide and UI placeholder. However, the attacker must be on the victim's LAN path, the operator must select remote rather than default local mode, and the repository's server binds only to loopback, requiring additional LAN ingress configuration. The conflicting HTTPS deployment guidance further reduces deployment prevalence. These facts support medium rather than high likelihood.
影响程度
High - Plaintext transmission can disclose reusable account passwords, bearer sessions, scanner API keys, upload tokens, and identifiable student answer images. Reuse of a scanner key permits authenticated scanner submissions, while reuse of a user credential or token grants that user's broader permissions. Active interception can affect scan integrity as well as confidentiality. This is a high-impact identity and educational-data compromise, although the affected population and privilege level depend on which operator account and key traverse the connection.
假设条件
A school operator follows the in-product scanner guide or matching login-page placeholder and configures a reachable plaintext LAN endpoint.
An unauthenticated attacker can observe or modify traffic on the scanner workstation's local network path.
Because the Node server binds to loopback, a separate operator-configured LAN listener or reverse proxy is required for the documented 192.168.1.100 endpoint to be reachable.
A scanner operator configures remote mode with a non-loopback HTTP URL.
The HTTP endpoint is made reachable on the school LAN.
The victim enters account credentials or uploads scans through that endpoint.
The attacker is on-path or otherwise able to observe or modify LAN traffic.
控制措施
The Node service binds to 127.0.0.1 rather than directly opening port 5174 on the LAN.
Local scanner mode is documented as the default.
A separate deployment guide recommends an HTTPS Nginx reverse proxy and expressly requires HTTPS in production.
Scanner uploads enforce either a valid scanner/full API key or an authenticated user session.
API keys have scope and active-state checks and can be revoked.
These authentication and authorization controls do not provide transport confidentiality and accept credentials captured from plaintext traffic.
盲点
No repository manifest establishes a concrete LAN-facing listener, reverse proxy, load balancer, or deployment using the documented private address; the direct Node process is loopback-only.
The supplied PoC used an HTTP capture server rather than a separate physical school-LAN host or packet sniffer, although it exercised the production URL and request-header code.
The full TWAIN hardware workflow was not executed, but password and API-key exposure occurs independently of scanner hardware.
The exact number and privilege level of users likely to authenticate from scanner workstations cannot be determined statically.
The same HTTP example existed in readus/多端使用说明.md before this commit, so this commit newly distributes the guidance through the principal in-product guide rather than originating every instance of the behavior.

## 31. CWD-based database path can recreate the default admin

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/1f3af219e4748191ad0142001775b891?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
CWD-based database path can recreate the default admin
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
0642990
10:28 PM Jun 23, 2026

操作者：Toyama Kasumi

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced: the new process.cwd()-relative default is not backward-compatible with the previous module-relative path used by bundled standalone servers. Because the initialization path automatically creates a known bootstrap administrator when the newly selected database is empty, this is more than ordinary data-path breakage and can expose an administrator account on upgrade.

Before this commit, the default database path was derived from the database module's import.meta.url. In the esbuild single-file production bundle at dist/server/index.mjs, that resolved relative to dist/server. The commit instead resolves the database as process.cwd()/data/projectx.db. Therefore, a standalone bundled server that does not explicitly set PROJECTX_DB_PATH can select a different database after upgrading whenever its working directory is not the directory that produced the former module-relative path. There is no compatibility lookup or migration of the prior database. better-sqlite3 creates the newly selected file, initializeDatabase() installs an empty schema, and ensureDefaultAdmin() creates admin with the documented password admin123. If the server is automatically exposed through its existing reverse proxy, a remote unauthenticated attacker can log in before the operator recognizes the database switch and obtain full administrator control. The same path switch also makes the prior users, exams, and scores unavailable to the running service. Electron deployments are not affected because electron/main.cjs explicitly sets PROJECTX_DB_PATH, and source-mode launches from the repository root generally resolve to the same location; the vulnerable case is the supported standalone production bundle relying on the default path.

验证
 Established the exact pre/post-commit default SQLite paths for the production bundle and demonstrated divergence under a fixed install path and different cwd.
 Verified there is no compatibility lookup or migration and observed creation and initialization of a fresh SQLite database at the new default.
 Demonstrated seeding of admin/admin123 and confirmed there is no effective forced-password-change barrier before privileged access.
 Exercised the real HTTP login interface and used the issued token successfully against an administrator-only endpoint.
 Confirmed the required unset-environment/cwd preconditions, documented reverse-proxy exposure, repository-root source-mode limitation, Electron override, and the direct-bundle-execution caveat.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Opens the newly resolved path and implicitly creates a database file there when the former production database is not found at that location.
- Treats the newly created empty database as a fresh installation and creates its schema and seed data.
- Creates the predictable admin/admin123 administrator in the newly selected empty database.
- Introduces the process.cwd()-relative default database location, which can differ from the location used by an existing bundled installation.

### [src/server/db/index.ts:18](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/064299034b261ac0f738b2652f21178f749b45b5/src/server/db/index.ts#L18)

~~~~typescript
export function getDatabase(): Database.Database {
  if (!dbInstance) {
    const dbPath = resolveProjectDbPath();
    mkdirSync(path.dirname(dbPath), { recursive: true });
    dbInstance = new Database(dbPath);
    dbInstance.pragma("journal_mode = WAL");
    dbInstance.pragma("foreign_keys = ON");
    dbInstance.pragma("synchronous = NORMAL");
    dbInstance.pragma("busy_timeout = 5000");
    console.log(`[DB] Connected to: ${dbPath}`);
~~~~

### [src/server/db/index.ts:40](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/064299034b261ac0f738b2652f21178f749b45b5/src/server/db/index.ts#L40)

~~~~typescript
export function initializeDatabase(): void {
  const db = getDatabase();
  const hasTables = db.prepare("SELECT COUNT(*) as cnt FROM sqlite_master WHERE type = 'table'").get() as { cnt: number };

  if (hasTables.cnt === 0) {
    const schema = readFileSync(schemaPath(), "utf8");
    db.exec(schema);
    console.log("[DB] Schema created successfully");
  }

  runMigrations(db);
  seedDefaultData(db);
~~~~

### [src/server/db/index.ts:65](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/064299034b261ac0f738b2652f21178f749b45b5/src/server/db/index.ts#L65)

~~~~typescript
export async function ensureDefaultAdmin(): Promise<void> {
  const db = getDatabase();
  const existing = db.prepare("SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?").get("admin");
  if (existing) {
    return;
  }

  const passwordHash = await hashPassword("admin123");
  db.prepare(
    `INSERT INTO users (username, password_hash, name, role_id, is_active)
     VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`
  ).run("admin", passwordHash, "系统管理员", 1, 1);
  console.log("[DB] Default admin created: username=admin, password=admin123");
~~~~

### [src/server/db/paths.ts:3](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/064299034b261ac0f738b2652f21178f749b45b5/src/server/db/paths.ts#L3)

~~~~typescript
export function resolveProjectDbPath(): string {
  const envPath = process.env.PROJECTX_DB_PATH;
  if (envPath) return path.resolve(envPath);
  return path.join(process.cwd(), "data", "projectx.db");
}
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The policy matrix is applied after separating impact from likelihood. Impact is high because the executable validation verified an unauthenticated-to-wildcard-administrator boundary crossing and access to a protected administrator endpoint. Likelihood is medium rather than high because the attack is remote and easy only after a plausible but deployment-specific path switch: an upgrade, unset PROJECTX_DB_PATH, a divergent working directory, an affected launcher, and external proxying must all coincide. The strongest repository counterevidence—Electron's explicit path override, repository-root source launch instructions, and failure of direct bundle execution—does not make exploitation unachievable, but it prevents retaining the original critical rating. Under the required matrix, impact=high and likelihood=medium yields adjusted criticality=medium.

路径
Remote unauthenticated attacker --sends unauthenticated HTTPS request--> Public reverse proxy or Cloudflare tunnel --forwards request to 127.0.0.1:5174--> Loopback Express service on port 5174 --startup resolves database from process.cwd()--> CWD-relative data/projectx.db --better-sqlite3 creates missing file; initialization seeds administrator--> Fresh schema and admin/admin123 bootstrap account --known credential satisfies bcrypt authentication--> POST /api/auth/login --AuthService issues an administrator token--> Role-1 bearer token with wildcard permission --authMiddleware and requirePermission accept wildcard administrator authority--> Protected administrator APIs and school-service control

Attacker story: a school operator upgrades an externally proxied Project-X service while PROJECTX_DB_PATH is unset. If the upgraded process has a different working directory from the location implied by the parent revision, resolveProjectDbPath() selects cwd/data/projectx.db instead of the established database. better-sqlite3 creates that file, initializeDatabase() installs the schema, and ensureDefaultAdmin() creates admin/admin123 with role_id 1. A remote unauthenticated attacker can submit that known credential to POST /api/auth/login, obtain a token with administrator wildcard permission, and access protected routes such as GET /api/users. The supplied executable validation reproduced two distinct databases, HTTP 200 for the recreated login, wildcard permissions, and HTTP 200 from the administrator route. Existing authentication and authorization are real controls, but they accept the recreated administrator as legitimate; this commit has no forced-password-change gate before token issuance. The strongest counterevidence is deployment-related: startServer binds only to localhost; documented server launchers run source mode from the repository root; Electron explicitly supplies a stable database path; and direct execution of dist/server/index.mjs exits because cleanup.ts's main check is folded into the bundle. Localhost binding is not dispositive because the repository explicitly documents public Nginx and Cloudflare forwarding. The mainstream-launcher exclusions are more substantial and reduce likelihood: the demonstrated vulnerable server required importing the exported bundle through a separate launcher, and no non-Electron imported-bundle launcher is shipped in the repository. Impact remains high for an affected deployment because the boundary crossing to wildcard administrator is verified, but the old database is merely unavailable rather than directly exposed through the newly selected database.

发生可能性
High - Exploitation is trivial and remote once an affected server is running: the credential is fixed in source, login is public, and documented proxies can expose the service. Overall likelihood is medium because several deployment conditions must coincide. PROJECTX_DB_PATH must be unset, the working directory must diverge, and the deployment must use a non-Electron imported-bundle launcher or unusual source launcher. Repository-shipped Electron sets the path, documented source startup occurs from the repository root, and directly running the bundle currently exits instead of listening. The executable validation proves achievability but does not establish prevalence of such non-Electron bundle deployments. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The verified result is unauthenticated acquisition of a role-1 token with wildcard application permission. On an affected school service this permits broad administrative control over identities, configuration, grading, and records created in the newly active database. The path regression also causes availability loss for the prior users, exams, and scores. Impact is not rated critical because the old database is not directly exposed through the new database, and immediate impact may initially concern a freshly initialized service rather than all historical records.
假设条件
An existing SQLite installation is upgraded from the parent revision to this commit.
PROJECTX_DB_PATH is unset when the upgraded process starts.
The process working directory differs from the location that produced the parent revision's module-relative database path.
The affected server is started through an imported-bundle launcher or an equivalently affected source-mode launcher rather than Electron.
A documented Nginx reverse proxy or Cloudflare tunnel exposes the loopback HTTP service to the attacker.
The operator has not detected the new empty database and changed or disabled the recreated administrator account before the attacker logs in.
Upgrade an existing installation across the database-path change
Leave PROJECTX_DB_PATH unset
Launch from a working directory whose data/projectx.db differs from the old module-relative database location
Start the HTTP server using an affected non-Electron launcher
Expose the loopback service through a reverse proxy or tunnel
Reach the login endpoint before the recreated bootstrap credential is changed
控制措施
startServer binds to 127.0.0.1 rather than all interfaces
Deployment documentation recommends HTTPS reverse proxying
PROJECTX_DB_PATH permits an operator to pin a stable absolute database location
Electron sets PROJECTX_DB_PATH to its user-data directory before importing the server bundle
Passwords are checked with bcrypt
Successful login tokens use 32 random bytes
User-management routes require both authMiddleware and user:manage permission
Administrator wildcard permission is centrally enforced by RBAC
Documented source launchers change into the repository directory, normally keeping the old and new default paths aligned
No forced password-change gate prevents immediate use of the recreated administrator token in this commit
No login rate limiter is present on the affected login route in this commit
盲点
The repository contains no inventory or telemetry showing how many real installations use custom imported-bundle launchers or start source mode outside the repository working directory.
No systemd, container, Kubernetes, or other production-service manifest establishes the working directory and PROJECTX_DB_PATH behavior for a non-Electron bundle deployment.
The executable validation used a separate launcher that imported startServer(); that interface is exported and used by Electron, but a vulnerable non-Electron launcher is not shipped in the repository.
Actual public exposure depends on operator deployment. The repository documents Nginx and Cloudflare exposure but contains no deployed ingress state.
The validation proved loss of visibility for a legacy administrator sentinel but did not populate representative historical exams, scores, or student records.
Static repository evidence cannot determine whether an operator would notice startup logs or remediate the recreated administrator before an attacker attempts login.

## 32. Card deletion bypasses exam-write authorization

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/224e8a794eb48191b3c2c4f42aa970f2?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Card deletion bypasses exam-write authorization
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
243e8cc
9:49 PM Jun 17, 2026

操作者：Toyama Kasumi

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. Previously, deleting a referenced card did not delete the associated exams. This commit added the cascade and unlink behavior but reused the card route's existing grade-write gate instead of enforcing authorization for the newly affected exam resources.

Business-route authentication maps every non-GET request under `/api/cards` to `grade:write`, while `/api/exams` mutations normally require the distinct `exam:write` capability. The changed `DELETE /api/cards/:cardId` handler now accepts attacker-controlled `deleteReferencedExams` and `unlinkExams` flags. It globally enumerates exams referencing the supplied card and, without checking `exam:write` or access to each referenced exam, either nulls their card associations or calls `deleteExamRows()`. The latter permanently deletes question scores, student totals, scan batches, and the exams themselves. Thus an authenticated grading-only role with a known card ID can send `DELETE /api/cards/<id>` with `{"deleteReferencedExams":true}` and destroy every linked exam and its grading data. Sending no flag also discloses the names of linked exams in the 409 response. Default legacy teachers currently possess both capabilities, which masks the issue for that role, but the permission model explicitly supports fine-grained permission rows, so installations separating grading from exam administration are affected. The secondary exam objects must be authorized independently with `exam:write` and applicable exam/class/subject scope before either unlinking or deleting them.

验证
 Confirmed DELETE /api/cards/:cardId is reachable by an authenticated role with grade:write but without exam:write; direct exam deletion returned 403 while card deletion proceeded.
 Confirmed attacker-controlled deletion/unlink flags reach exam mutations with no secondary exam:write or per-exam authorization check; deletion was dynamic, and unlink was source-traced.
 Exercised the real authenticated HTTP interface and verified linked exams, question scores, student totals, scan batches, and scan records were removed from the database.
 Confirmed a no-flag request returns linked exam names to the underprivileged role in an HTTP 409 response.
 Established the preconditions: roles.permissions supports the narrowed capability set, RBAC enforcement was enabled, and card:read allowed the attacker to discover the card ID through GET /api/cards.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The newly added helper irreversibly deletes per-question scores, student scores, scan batches, and exam records.
- Non-GET card routes are guarded by grade:write, whereas exam routes use the separate exam:write permission.
- Attacker-controlled flags cause all exams referencing the card to be unlinked or deleted without any exam-write or per-exam scope authorization.

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:121](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/243e8cc491f56eedd541d4d27bef521b9d8d6d1b/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L121)

~~~~typescript
function deleteExamRows(db: ReturnType<typeof getDatabase>, examIds: number[]): void {
  for (const examId of examIds) {
    db.prepare("DELETE FROM question_scores WHERE exam_id = ?").run(examId);
    db.prepare("DELETE FROM student_scores WHERE exam_id = ?").run(examId);
    db.prepare("DELETE FROM scan_batches WHERE exam_id = ?").run(examId);
    db.prepare("DELETE FROM exams WHERE id = ?").run(examId);
  }
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:384](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/243e8cc491f56eedd541d4d27bef521b9d8d6d1b/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L384)

~~~~typescript
  // 业务路由 RBAC 网关
  const cardGate = makeGate(enforceAuth, PERMISSIONS.CARD_READ, PERMISSIONS.GRADE_WRITE);
  const examGate = makeGate(enforceAuth, PERMISSIONS.EXAM_READ, PERMISSIONS.EXAM_WRITE);
  const analysisGate = makeGate(enforceAuth, PERMISSIONS.GRADE_READ, PERMISSIONS.GRADE_READ);
  const scannerGate = makeGate(enforceAuth, PERMISSIONS.GRADE_WRITE, PERMISSIONS.GRADE_WRITE);
  app.use("/api/cards", cardGate);
  app.use("/api/exams", examGate);
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:863](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/243e8cc491f56eedd541d4d27bef521b9d8d6d1b/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L863)

~~~~typescript
  app.delete("/api/cards/:cardId", async (req, res, next) => {
    try {
      const cardId = safeId(paramValue(req.params.cardId));
      const card = cardRepo.findById(cardId);
      if (!card) {
        res.status(404).json({ message: "答题卡不存在" });
        return;
      }
      // 检查是否被考试引用
      const examRepo = new ExamRepository();
      const exams = examRepo.listExams();
      const referenced = exams.filter((e: any) => e.card_id === cardId);
      if (referenced.length > 0) {
        const body = (req.body ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
        const unlinkExams = requestFlag(body.unlinkExams);
        const deleteReferencedExams = requestFlag(body.deleteReferencedExams);
        if (!unlinkExams && !deleteReferencedExams) {
          res.status(409).json({
            message: `无法直接删除答题卡：已被 ${referenced.length} 个考试引用`,
            referencedExamCount: referenced.length,
            referencedExamNames: referenced.map((e: any) => e.name)
          });
          return;
        }
        const db = getDatabase();
        db.transaction(() => {
          if (deleteReferencedExams) {
            deleteExamRows(db, referenced.map((e: any) => Number(e.id)));
          } else {
            db.prepare("UPDATE exams SET card_id = NULL, updated_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP WHERE card_id = ?").run(cardId);
          }
          cardRepo.deleteCard(cardId);
        })();
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The original high rating correctly reflects the major technical impact and the verified authorization bypass. However, repository counterevidence materially lowers probability: default teachers already have exam:write, only three built-in roles are assignable through the user API, PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is disabled by default, and the service directly listens only on 127.0.0.1. These facts do not suppress the finding because roles.permissions is an actively enforced capability mechanism, a narrowed role was successfully exercised through the normal authenticated interface, and the supplied deployment threat model includes proxy/tunnel exposure. With impact=high and likelihood=medium, the required matrix mechanically yields medium. No hard-suppression condition applies because the effect is not self-only, the attacker is lower-privileged rather than an administrator or operator, and the preconditions are plausible rather than unachievable.

路径
Authenticated account with card:read and grade:write but no exam:write --card:read authorizes listing--> GET /api/cards reveals a usable card ID --attacker supplies discovered cardId--> DELETE /api/cards/:cardId passes cardGate using grade:write --grade:write gate permits request without exam:write--> Handler globally identifies exams whose card_id matches --no per-exam permission or scope check--> Attacker-controlled deletion or unlink flag selects an exam mutation --transaction executes DELETE statements or global UPDATE--> Exam and grading data are deleted, or exam-card associations are removed

Attacker story: a grading-only teacher or compromised grading account signs in to a remotely exposed school deployment, lists cards using card:read, and selects a card used by exams the account is not allowed to administer. The attacker sends DELETE /api/cards/<id> with deleteReferencedExams=true. Because cardGate maps every non-GET card request to grade:write, the request passes even though the same account is denied direct exam deletion for lacking exam:write. The handler globally lists exams, selects those referencing the card, and calls deleteExamRows() without secondary exam authorization. This permanently removes exam rows, question scores, student totals, and scan batches, with scan records cascading from deleted batches. The supplied executable PoC exercised the normal authenticated HTTP interface: direct DELETE /api/exams/<id> returned 403, while the indirect card deletion returned 200 and database checks confirmed deletion of two exams and their grading and scan records. A no-flag request also disclosed linked exam names. Counterevidence limits likelihood rather than validity: the default teacher permission set includes both grade:write and exam:write, the built-in user API does not expose a custom-role editor, authentication enforcement is disabled by default for compatibility, and the server directly binds only to localhost. Nevertheless, permissions are independently represented and loaded from roles.permissions, production is assumed to enable authentication, and the supplied threat model permits proxy/tunnel exposure. The result is a genuine cross-resource authorization vulnerability in installations that separate grading from exam administration.

发生可能性
High - Once the preconditions hold, exploitation is a low-complexity authenticated HTTP request with no user interaction, race, or secret extraction, and card IDs can be listed through the same API. The executable PoC confirmed reachability and effect. Likelihood is reduced to medium because the directly vulnerable privilege split is not present in the seeded teacher role, the user-management API has no custom-role editor, production must enable the optional RBAC switch, and direct service exposure is loopback-only without an operator proxy or tunnel. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - A successful request crosses from grading authority into exam administration and can destroy multiple linked exams, student totals, per-question scores, scan batches, and cascading scan records. This causes major integrity and availability damage to school examination records. The same handler can globally unlink exams from their answer card, while its conflict response exposes linked exam names. Impact is not self-only and can affect records belonging to other teachers, classes, or subjects because no per-exam scope check is performed.
假设条件
Production enables PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE as required by the supplied threat model.
The Node/Express service may be made remotely reachable through the reverse-proxy or tunnel deployment pattern described in the supplied threat model; the process itself binds only to 127.0.0.1.
An installation has narrowed the database-backed teacher role, or another role row, to include card:read and grade:write without exam:write.
The attacker has a valid account assigned to that role and at least one discoverable card is referenced by an exam.
A reachable Project-X Express API
PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE=1 or true
A valid authenticated account whose role grants card:read and grade:write but not exam:write
Knowledge of a card ID, obtainable through GET /api/cards with card:read
At least one exam referencing the selected card
控制措施
optionalAuth resolves bearer, cookie, or query tokens into req.user
With PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE enabled, makeGate returns 401 for unauthenticated callers and 403 when the selected route permission is absent
Direct /api/exams mutations are protected by exam:write
safeId validates the attacker-supplied card ID
The destructive operations execute in a database transaction
A 409 conflict is returned when linked exams exist and neither action flag is supplied
Default teachers possess both grade:write and exam:write, reducing affected default deployments
The Node server binds to 127.0.0.1 unless exposed through another local ingress component
盲点
No reverse-proxy, tunnel, Kubernetes, Terraform, or load-balancer manifest was found in the checkout, so actual Internet exposure depends on deployment configuration described by the supplied threat model.
The repository does not show how often operators directly customize roles.permissions or whether downstream deployments provide an external role-management interface.
The executable validation covered destructive deletion and exam-name disclosure; the unlinkExams branch was established by source tracing rather than a separate HTTP execution.
No route-local class, subject, or exam scope mechanism was found for this handler, but downstream forks or deployment-specific middleware cannot be assessed from this checkout.
The helper deletes scan metadata from the database; whether external backups or orphaned image files permit operational recovery is deployment-specific and does not remove the immediate integrity and availability impact.

## 33. Grading previews serve arbitrary uploads as same-origin HTML

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/198b4cb5d2d081919341f2c2cc435ad7?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Grading previews serve arbitrary uploads as same-origin HTML
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
43ae248
9:08 PM Jun 13, 2026

操作者：Toyama Kasumi

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. The parent revision already stored grading uploads without validating their type, but the recognition upload directory was not web-accessible. This commit adds preview URLs and a `sendFile` endpoint, converting the pre-existing validation weakness into a persistent same-origin XSS sink. Restrict uploads to verified image formats, generate a fixed safe extension, and serve previews with a fixed image Content-Type plus `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; alternatively force `Content-Disposition: attachment` or render transformed images from a separate origin. The preview route should also enforce authentication and the appropriate card/exam scope.

The grading upload's Multer configuration preserves the extension from the attacker-controlled original filename and applies only a size limit; it has no extension, MIME, or magic-byte validation. Both grading handlers now return a preview URL even when native recognition fails. The newly added preview route passes the resulting file directly to `res.sendFile()`, which derives the response Content-Type from that attacker-selected extension and does not force download or sandbox active content. At this revision, neither the grading upload nor preview route applies authentication or authorization. An unauthenticated attacker can obtain a card ID from the card API, upload a payload named `payload.html` in the `files` multipart field, receive the generated preview URL despite recognition failure, and induce a teacher or administrator to follow that legitimate same-origin URL. The payload then executes as HTML in the Project-X origin and can issue same-origin requests, read accessible application data, alter browser-visible state, or present credential-stealing UI. The client intentionally opens these preview URLs in a new tab; `rel="noreferrer"` prevents opener access but does not prevent the preview document itself from executing scripts.

验证
 Attacker-controlled multipart filename/content reaches recognition storage with an active-content extension and without file-type or authentication controls.
 Failed recognition still returns a stable preview URL for the persisted payload.
 The preview route is unauthenticated and serves the uploaded HTML inline as text/html without attachment disposition, CSP, or nosniff protection.
 A realistic HTTP upload-to-preview reproduction confirms persistence across a server restart and exact payload recovery.
 Direct victim navigation is a realistic execution path, and the client exposes a non-sandboxed preview link; browser automation was attempted but blocked by a missing system library.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The new UI navigates directly to the preview resource; opening in a new tab with noreferrer does not sandbox or disable scripts in the served document.
- The upload configuration preserves the untrusted original extension and has no file type or content validation.
- The objective grading handler returns a preview URL even when recognition throws, so non-image HTML uploads remain retrievable.
- The combined grading handler likewise exposes previews for both successful and failed recognition attempts.
- The introduced route serves the stored file inline using extension-derived content handling, allowing an uploaded .html file to execute under the application origin.

攻击路径分析

No hard suppression applies: the impact is not self-only, the attacker is unauthenticated rather than privileged, the upload path is a normal product grading workflow, and the required victim navigation is plausible rather than unachievable. Repository code and executable HTTP evidence establish attacker-controlled input, persistent storage, inline text/html delivery, and absent route authorization. The strongest contrary fact is the 127.0.0.1 bind at index.ts:697-704, but the required threat model states that production deployments expose this loopback service through a reverse proxy or tunnel; the missing ingress artifact lowers confidence without mechanically changing the remote vector to localhost. Impact is medium because same-origin script execution can affect confidentiality, integrity, and user trust but no session-token theft, administrator takeover, or broad score corruption was proven. Likelihood is high for a reachable deployment because exploitation is unauthenticated, deterministic, and demonstrated, notwithstanding required user interaction. Applying the required matrix mechanically, medium impact multiplied by high likelihood yields medium adjusted criticality.

路径
Unauthenticated remote attacker --Uploads HTML bytes with an attacker-selected .html filename--> Public Multer grading upload --Stores bytes under a generated filename retaining .html--> Persistent recognition/uploads/<cardId> storage --Recognition result exposes a preview URL even when recognition fails--> Public res.sendFile preview route --Serves the stored bytes inline as text/html without CSP, nosniff, or attachment disposition--> Teacher or administrator browser --Attacker script executes with the Project-X origin after victim navigation--> Project-X origin APIs, data, and trusted UI

The Express answer-card service is a production grading component backed by SQLite and persistent upload storage. Its recognitionUpload configuration takes path.extname(file.originalname), so an unauthenticated request can cause attacker-controlled bytes to be stored with a .html extension. The objective and combined grading handlers expose preview URLs in both success and exception branches. The preview route then calls res.sendFile() without authentication, a fixed image media type, download disposition, CSP, or nosniff protection. The supplied executable HTTP reproduction confirmed unauthenticated card creation/listing, HTML upload, persistence across server restart, and a byte-identical preview response with Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8. The application also renders preview URLs as target=_blank links; rel=noreferrer prevents opener access but does not sandbox the loaded document. Strongest counterevidence includes the explicit 127.0.0.1 bind, the need for victim navigation, React's normal escaping, path.basename/safeId traversal controls, the existence of an unused bearer authentication middleware, and the absence of directly observed browser execution. None prevents this sink: the production threat model permits proxy exposure, React does not render the response, traversal controls do not constrain its media type, no auth middleware is attached to these routes, and standard browser behavior for the demonstrated inline HTML response is deterministic. However, the victim-interaction requirement, missing ingress artifact, and lack of proof of bearer-token theft constrain impact to medium.

发生可能性
High - For a proxy-exposed deployment, exploitation is low-complexity and requires no account: card IDs can be listed or created publicly, the upload accepts arbitrary bytes and extensions, and the server returns a usable preview URL even after recognition failure. The executable HTTP reproduction exercised the complete storage and delivery path. Victim interaction and uncertainty about actual proxy deployment are limiting factors, but a same-origin grading-preview link is credible social-engineering material and the attack does not require a race, secret, or privileged write path. | Remote network vector
影响程度
Medium - The payload executes under the trusted Project-X origin in a victim browser and can access origin-readable application responses, invoke origin-accessible operations, alter the displayed interface, and present credential-phishing content. This crosses an unauthenticated-attacker-to-teacher/admin-browser trust boundary. Impact is not high because exploitation requires victim navigation, the revision uses explicit bearer authentication rather than proven ambient privileged cookies, no bearer-token theft or administrator takeover was demonstrated, and several relevant APIs were already unauthenticated.
假设条件
The Node service is exposed to intended school users through the reverse-proxy or Cloudflare-tunnel deployment described by the supplied threat model; the repository itself contains no ingress manifest.
The frontend and grading preview are served from the same browser origin, as indicated by the relative preview URL and application route composition.
A teacher or administrator can be induced to open the legitimate Project-X preview URL, either through social engineering or while handling grading-related content.
Normal browser handling of an inline text/html response executes its script because the response has no blocking Content-Security-Policy or forced-download disposition.
A Project-X deployment reachable through its configured reverse proxy or tunnel
A valid card ID, obtainable from the unauthenticated card-list endpoint or by creating a card
A multipart upload no larger than 20 MiB whose original filename has an active extension such as .html
A teacher or administrator opening the returned or attacker-supplied preview URL
控制措施
startServer binds the Node service to 127.0.0.1, limiting direct exposure when no proxy or tunnel is configured.
Multer limits each recognition upload to 20 MiB but does not limit the content to images.
safeId and path.basename constrain directory traversal but do not prevent active-content upload or inline HTML delivery.
The client uses target=_blank with rel=noreferrer, which limits opener/referrer behavior but does not disable scripts in the preview document.
React auto-escapes JSX content, but the vulnerable document bypasses React and is served directly by Express.
Bearer authentication middleware exists elsewhere in the repository but is not attached to the affected card, grading, or preview routes.
Recognition attempts to process the file, but both success and failure branches expose the stored upload, so recognizer rejection is not a mitigation.
盲点
No reverse-proxy, Cloudflare-tunnel, Kubernetes, or load-balancer manifest exists in the checkout, so public exposure relies on the supplied production threat model rather than a concrete repository deployment artifact.
Actual JavaScript execution was not observed in a browser because the bundled Electron binary could not start without libatk-1.0.so.0; the server-side prerequisite was nevertheless demonstrated as an unsandboxed inline text/html response.
The validation environment lacked the declared bcrypt native package and used an inert seeded administrator row solely to start the application; this did not modify the affected routing or upload behavior.
No evidence proves that a privileged bearer token is stored in browser-readable storage at this revision or automatically sent by the preview document, so account takeover and authenticated administrator actions are not claimed.
Static evidence does not establish how operators distribute preview links, how often teachers open externally supplied links, or whether an external proxy adds defensive CSP, nosniff, or Content-Disposition headers.

<a id="结果-34"></a>

## 34. Forgeable red marks are accepted as subjective grades

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/2f4e1b22368c8191a8d310833c002da3?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Forgeable red marks are accepted as subjective grades
严重级别
Medium

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
2b1a383
9:12 PM Jun 11, 2026

操作者：chuh

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This commit introduces automatic recognition and aggregation of subjective score-grid marks. It validates the visual shape of a red mark but not its provenance, then treats visually valid marks as confirmed scores rather than untrusted recognition suggestions.

A student controls the physical answer card before it is graded, while uploaded or scanned answer-card images are also untrusted. The new recognizer identifies a score-cell selection exclusively by its HSV red color, coverage, component count, extent, and straightness. None of these properties establish that the mark was made by an authorized grader rather than by the student or through image manipulation. A student can therefore draw a straight red line through the maximum-value cell before submitting the card. A clean forged line is assigned status "ok" by build_subjective_questions. The TypeScript grading layer then sets needsReview to false solely because that status is "ok", adds the recognized value to the subjective and total scores, and returns it through the grading endpoint for display and CSV export. There is no mandatory grader confirmation or other provenance control before this value is treated as a grade. This crosses the student-to-grader integrity boundary and can silently inflate one or more students' subjective scores. Subjective OCR values should remain pending review unless produced in a controlled post-grading workflow, or the score marks should be captured through a grader-authenticated channel that students cannot pre-populate.

验证
 Confirm that an attacker-controllable physical/scanned card exposes selectable subjective score cells, including the maximum-value cell.
 Confirm that the closest recognizer control validates only reproducible visual properties and contains no grader-provenance mechanism.
 Confirm that a clean single red line satisfying the documented thresholds becomes an ok subjective score in native output.
 Confirm through the real grading interface that an ok subjective result is added to the total with no review requirement.
 Confirm that the resulting value is operationally displayed/exported and that no mandatory downstream confirmation control intervenes.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- Score marks are selected solely by broad HSV red-color thresholds and a minimum red-pixel ratio.
- Any red mark meeting simple component, extent, and straightness tests is declared valid; there is no indication of grader provenance.
- A visually valid selected cell is converted directly into an "ok" subjective score and returned as authoritative recognition output.
- The resulting subjective and total scores are included in the downloadable grade CSV, making the unconfirmed recognition operationally consumable.
- The new combined grading endpoint processes uploaded card images and returns totals containing automatically accepted subjective marks.
- An "ok" recognition is accepted without review and its score is directly added to the subjective and overall totals.

### [native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp:195](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/2b1a38303fbf98ad6c0f9f48da6d4c32251a3612/native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp#L195)

~~~~cpp
    cv::Mat hsv;
    cv::cvtColor(roi, hsv, cv::COLOR_BGR2HSV);
    cv::Mat red_low;
    cv::Mat red_high;
    cv::inRange(hsv, cv::Scalar(0, 55, 55), cv::Scalar(12, 255, 255), red_low);
    cv::inRange(hsv, cv::Scalar(165, 55, 55), cv::Scalar(180, 255, 255), red_high);
    cv::Mat red_mask = red_low | red_high;

    cv::Mat kernel = cv::getStructuringElement(cv::MORPH_ELLIPSE, cv::Size(3, 3));
    cv::morphologyEx(red_mask, red_mask, cv::MORPH_OPEN, kernel);
    cv::morphologyEx(red_mask, red_mask, cv::MORPH_CLOSE, kernel);

    const int red_pixels = cv::countNonZero(red_mask);
    const double red_ratio = static_cast<double>(red_pixels) / static_cast<double>(red_mask.total());
    sample.has_red = red_ratio >= MIN_RED_RATIO;
~~~~

### [native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp:278](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/2b1a38303fbf98ad6c0f9f48da6d4c32251a3612/native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp#L278)

    if (!sample.has_red) {
        sample.reason = "no_red_line";
        return sample;
    }
    if (component_count != 1) {
        sample.reason = component_count == 0 ? "red_noise_only" : "multiple_red_components";
        return sample;
    }
    if (extent_ratio < MIN_LINE_EXTENT_RATIO) {
        sample.reason = "red_mark_does_not_cross_cell";
        return sample;
    }
    if (mean_error_ratio > MAX_LINE_MEAN_ERROR_RATIO || max_error_ratio > MAX_LINE_MAX_ERROR_RATIO) {
        sample.reason = "red_mark_is_not_single_straight_line";
        return sample;
    }

    sample.valid = true;
    sample.confidence = round_to(line_quality, 4);
    return sample;

### [native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp:488](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/2b1a38303fbf98ad6c0f9f48da6d4c32251a3612/native/AnswerCardRecognizer/answer-card-recognizer/answer_recognition.cpp#L488)

~~~~cpp
        if (valid_samples.empty()) {
            status = "invalid";
            message = "no_valid_subjective_score";
        } else {
            std::vector<double> half_scores;
            std::vector<double> base_scores;
            std::vector<double> tens_scores;
            std::vector<double> ones_scores;

            for (const auto& sample : valid_samples) {
                const double value = sample.cell.score;
                if (is_half_score(value)) {
                    half_scores.push_back(value);
                } else if (max_score > 16.0) {
                    if (is_tens_score(value)) {
                        tens_scores.push_back(value);
                    } else if (value >= 0.0 && value <= 9.0 && is_whole_score(value)) {
                        ones_scores.push_back(value);
                    } else {
                        base_scores.push_back(value);
                    }
                } else {
                    base_scores.push_back(value);
                }
            }

            if (max_score > 16.0) {
                if (tens_scores.size() > 1 || ones_scores.size() > 1 || half_scores.size() > 1 || !base_scores.empty()) {
                    status = "invalid";
                    message = "invalid_subjective_score_combination";
                } else {
                    score =
                        (tens_scores.empty() ? 0.0 : tens_scores.front()) +
                        (ones_scores.empty() ? 0.0 : ones_scores.front()) +
                        (half_scores.empty() ? 0.0 : half_scores.front());
                }
            } else {
                if (base_scores.size() > 1 || half_scores.size() > 1) {
                    status = "invalid";
                    message = "invalid_subjective_score_combination";
                } else {
                    score =
                        (base_scores.empty() ? 0.0 : base_scores.front()) +
                        (half_scores.empty() ? 0.0 : half_scores.front());
                }
            }

            if (status == "ok" && score > max_score + 0.001) {
                status = "invalid";
                message = "subjective_score_exceeds_max";
            }
        }

        double confidence = 0.0;
        if (!valid_samples.empty()) {
            confidence = std::accumulate(valid_samples.begin(), valid_samples.end(), 0.0, [](double sum, const auto& sample) {
                return sum + sample.confidence;
            }) / static_cast<double>(valid_samples.size());
        }

        json question = {
            {"blockId", first.block_id},
            {"questionId", first.question_id},
            {"questionNumber", first.question_number},
            {"score", status == "ok" ? json(round_to(score, 3)) : json(0)},
            {"maxScore", round_to(max_score, 3)},
            {"status", status},
            {"validCells", valid_cells},
            {"invalidCells", invalid_cells},
            {"confidence", round_to(confidence, 4)},
        };
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx:89](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/2b1a38303fbf98ad6c0f9f48da6d4c32251a3612/src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx#L89)

~~~~tsx
function downloadCsv(rows: CombinedGradingRow[], cardId: string) {
  const header = ["文件名", "学号", "识别状态", "总分", "满分", "客观题得分", "主观题得分", "待复核题数", "异常数", "备注"];
  const lines = [
    header,
    ...rows.map((row) => [
      row.fileName,
      row.studentId ?? "未识别",
      row.recognitionStatus,
      String(row.totalScore),
      String(row.totalMaxScore),
      `${row.objectiveScore}/${row.objectiveMaxScore}`,
      `${row.subjectiveScore}/${row.subjectiveMaxScore}`,
      String(row.needsReviewCount),
      String(row.issueCount),
      row.message ?? ""
    ])
  ];
  const csv = lines.map((line) => line.map((cell) => `"${cell.replace(/"/g, '""')}"`).join(",")).join("\n");
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:261](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/2b1a38303fbf98ad6c0f9f48da6d4c32251a3612/src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts#L261)

~~~~typescript
  app.post("/api/cards/:cardId/grading", recognitionUpload.array("files", 200), async (req, res, next) => {
    try {
      const cardId = safeId(paramValue(req.params.cardId));
      const card = await readCard(cardId);
      if (!card) {
        res.status(404).json({ message: "答题卡不存在" });
        return;
      }

      const files = Array.isArray(req.files) ? req.files : [];
      if (files.length === 0) {
        res.status(400).json({ message: "没有收到答题卡图片" });
        return;
      }

      await readLayout(cardId);
      const pageNumber = Number(fieldValue(req.body.page || req.query.page) || "1");
      const dpi = Number(fieldValue(req.body.dpi || req.query.dpi) || "300");
      const safePageNumber = Number.isFinite(pageNumber) && pageNumber > 0 ? pageNumber : 1;
      const safeDpi = Number.isFinite(dpi) && dpi > 0 ? dpi : 300;

      const rows = [];
      for (const file of files) {
        try {
          const recognition = (await recognizeAnswerCard({
            imagePath: file.path,
            layoutPath: layoutPath(cardId),
            pageNumber: safePageNumber,
            dpi: safeDpi
          })) as CombinedRecognitionResult;
          recognition.subjectiveQuestions = recognition.subjectiveQuestions ?? [];
          rows.push(gradeCombinedRecognition(card, file.originalname || path.basename(file.path), recognition));
        } catch (error) {
          const recognition: CombinedRecognitionResult = {
            status: "failed",
            imagePath: file.path,
            pageNumber: safePageNumber,
            message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
            questions: [],
            subjectiveQuestions: []
          };
          rows.push(gradeCombinedRecognition(card, file.originalname || path.basename(file.path), recognition));
        }
      }

      const result: CombinedGradingBatchResult = {
        batchId: `batch_${Date.now()}_${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8)}`,
        cardId,
        rows
      };
      res.json(result);
~~~~

    status: isOk ? "ok" : "invalid",
    needsReview: !isOk,
    confidence: Number.isFinite(recognized.confidence) ? recognized.confidence : 0,
    validCells: recognized.validCells ?? [],
    invalidCells: recognized.invalidCells ?? [],
    message: recognized.message ?? (isOk ? undefined : "未识别到有效评分")
  };
}

export function gradeCombinedRecognition(
  card: AnswerCard,
  fileName: string,
  recognition: CombinedRecognitionResult
): CombinedGradingRow {
  const objective = gradeObjectiveRecognition(card, fileName, recognition);
  const recognizedSubjective = new Map((recognition.subjectiveQuestions ?? []).map((question) => [question.questionId, question]));
  const subjectiveQuestions = subjectiveScoreQuestions(card).map((question) => gradeSubjectiveQuestion(question, recognizedSubjective.get(question.questionId)));
  const subjectiveScore = roundScore(subjectiveQuestions.reduce((sum, item) => sum + item.score, 0));
  const subjectiveMaxScore = roundScore(subjectiveQuestions.reduce((sum, item) => sum + item.maxScore, 0));
  const objectiveScore = objective.score;
  const objectiveMaxScore = objective.maxScore;
  const totalScore = roundScore(objectiveScore + subjectiveScore);
  const totalMaxScore = roundScore(objectiveMaxScore + subjectiveMaxScore);
  const subjectiveIssueCount = subjectiveQuestions.filter((question) => question.needsReview).length;
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The policy-adjustment pass does not trigger hard suppression. The impact is not self-only because a forged grade changes an institutional record and can affect educators and comparative student outcomes. The attacker does not require privileged physical access, operator privileges, or a protected write path; ordinary custody of one's answer card is the intended workflow, while staff perform only the expected scan/import action. Although the service is localhost-bound, direct network reachability is unnecessary because the malicious document is carried into the service by normal operation. Preconditions are therefore plausible rather than unrealistic. Impact is high for integrity of the affected student's subjective and total grade, while likelihood is medium due to the optional manual-score-grid configuration, staff interaction, possible visual detection, and missing end-to-end native execution. Applying the required matrix mechanically, impact=high and likelihood=medium yields adjusted criticality=medium.

路径
Student controlling submitted answer card --places a forged red line in the desired score cell--> Printed manual score grid with selectable values --staff scans or imports the submitted card through the normal workflow--> POST /api/cards/:cardId/grading --passes the uploaded image and generated layout to the native process--> C++ HSV and line-shape recognizer --returns the visually valid cell value with status ok--> TypeScript subjective-grade aggregation --adds the value to subjectiveScore and totalScore with no review flag--> Displayed and CSV-exported total

A student can exploit the normal answer-card workflow by drawing a clean red line through a high-value subjective score cell before submission. The generated card exposes all selectable values, including the configured maximum. The native recognizer checks broad HSV red ranges, minimum coverage, one connected component, line extent, and line-fit error, but has no signal establishing that an authorized grader made the mark. A passing mark is emitted with status "ok". The TypeScript layer then copies the score, sets needsReview to false, adds it to the subjective and total scores, and makes it available to the grading UI and CSV export. The strongest counterevidence is that the application is loopback-only by default, the score grid is intended for a human grader, the recognizer rejects malformed marks, and a diligent grader could visually notice a pre-marked cell. These facts reduce likelihood and breadth but are not dispositive: the attacker input enters through routine staff scanning rather than direct network access, the validation rules establish shape rather than provenance, and no repository-enforced confirmation is required for a clean mark. The executable validation confirmed the endpoint and aggregation effect—15/15, needsReviewCount 0, issueCount 0—while mocking only the unavailable native executable. Native acceptance remains established statically from the explicit thresholds and successful branch.

发生可能性
Medium - The required mark is simple, the score values are printed on the card, students normally control their own cards, and no account or protected system access is required. The recognizer's checks are deterministic visual-quality checks rather than provenance controls. Likelihood is reduced to medium because exploitation requires a manual-score-grid card, routine staff scanning, a sufficiently clean mark, and failure of a human grader to notice or independently verify the pre-existing selection. The native detector was not dynamically executed in the available Linux environment.
影响程度
High - Successful exploitation can replace the integrity of an affected student's subjective result with an attacker-selected value up to the configured maximum, and that value is incorporated into the displayed and exported total without a warning. This can influence official grading decisions and comparative outcomes, so it is not merely self-only. The affected code does not demonstrate automatic persistence to the central score database, and a single student ordinarily controls only their own submission, which bounds breadth and prevents critical classification.
假设条件
Students ordinarily possess and write on their own printed answer cards, so marking a score cell does not require privileged physical access to a protected system.
The configured card contains at least one subjective question using the manual_score_grid style.
Staff import or scan the submitted card through the normal grading workflow and may rely on questions reported with status "ok" and needsReview=false.
No external procedural control, such as mandatory visual comparison by a grader or strict control of red marking instruments, is assumed because none is enforced by repository code.
The supplied executable validation exercised the real HTTP route, layout generation, native-process adapter, and TypeScript aggregation, but mocked the unavailable Windows/OpenCV recognizer boundary.
A printed or digitally manipulated answer card containing a manual_score_grid subjective question
Student custody of the card before scanning or control over an image later imported for grading
A clean red line through the desired score cell that satisfies the recognizer's coverage, component, extent, and straightness thresholds
Routine staff scanning or import of the card
Operational reliance on the automatically accepted result without an independent provenance check
控制措施
The native recognizer requires at least 1.2 percent red coverage.
The recognizer requires exactly one significant red component.
The red mark must cross enough of the cell and satisfy mean and maximum straight-line error thresholds.
Multiple or malformed score selections are marked invalid.
Recognized scores above the configured maximum are rejected.
Missing or invalid subjective recognitions are assigned zero and marked needsReview=true.
The Express service binds to loopback by default, and Electron uses an ephemeral loopback port.
The grading endpoint limits each uploaded file to 20 MiB and accepts at most 200 files.
The native recognizer process has a 30-second timeout.
None of these controls verifies who made a visually valid mark or requires approval of a successful recognition.
盲点
The Windows OpenCV recognizer binary and required OpenCV build environment were unavailable, so the detector itself was not executed against a forged image.
The executable validation mocked only the native-process output using metrics consistent with the source thresholds; it conclusively verifies downstream endpoint and aggregation behavior but not image-level acceptance.
Repository artifacts do not establish whether a deployment mandates an external human comparison, controls red pens, or uses other procedural anti-forgery measures.
No ingress or load-balancer manifest was found, so deployments may expose the otherwise loopback-only service differently.
The analyzed grading component displays and exports scores but does not itself show persistence into the broader central score database; downstream use of the CSV is operational rather than code-enforced.
The frequency with which schools enable manual_score_grid questions is not established by repository artifacts.

## 35. Cancel fallback can kill a reused Windows PID

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/667d3d6cc8d881918e602edbaa4f3e17?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=2)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Cancel fallback can kill a reused Windows PID
严重级别
Low

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
f9ecba6
6:38 PM Aug 9, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This range introduces the cancellation endpoint and its taskkill fallback. The initial liveness check correctly considers both exitCode and signalCode, but the delayed check omits signalCode and retains no cancellation handle, introducing a PID-reuse time-of-check/time-of-use flaw.

cancelScan() first terminates the scanner child with child.kill(), then unconditionally retains a two-second watchdog associated only with its numeric PID. The watchdog tests child.exitCode === null but does not test child.signalCode. In Node.js, a child terminated by a signal normally retains a null exitCode while signalCode is set, including after the close event. Consequently, the watchdog can execute taskkill /F /T even though the original scanner process has exited. Because the close handler does not cancel this watchdog, Windows may have reused the PID for another process by the time taskkill runs, causing an unrelated process tree running under the server's account to be terminated. When native scanning is enabled, a scanner API key or a user with GRADE_WRITE can reach the newly added cancel endpoint; repeated start/cancel requests can increase process churn and the likelihood of collateral termination. The fallback should be canceled on child close and should at minimum require both exitCode and signalCode to remain null before invoking taskkill, preferably with additional process-identity validation.

验证
 Establish that attacker-controlled cancellation of a live scan reaches cancelScan under the documented native-scanner and authorization conditions.
 Demonstrate that the scanner child can close from the cancellation signal while exitCode remains null before the two-second watchdog fires.
 Demonstrate that the uncancelled production watchdog still invokes taskkill after the original child has closed.
 Verify that the forced-kill sink uses only the captured numeric PID, with no signalCode, active-child, or process-identity validation.
 Reproduce an actual Windows PID-reuse collision and collateral termination of an unrelated process tree; this was not feasible on the Linux validation host.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The introduced authenticated HTTP endpoint invokes cancelScan for nonterminal scan sessions, exposing the flawed termination path.
- The new cancellation logic sends a termination signal and later runs taskkill based only on exitCode; signal-terminated children can have exitCode null after exit, leaving a stale-PID kill.
- The child error/close handlers remove the registry entry but do not cancel the two-second taskkill watchdog.

        const id = safeId(req.params.sessionId);
        const session = await getSession(id);
        if (!session) {
          res.status(404).json({ message: "扫描会话不存在" });
          return;
        }

          res.json({ message: "扫描已结束，无需取消", status: session.status });
          return;
        }

        const terminated = cancelScan(id);
        await updateSessionStatus(id, "cancelled", "用户取消扫描");
        emitProgress(id, { sessionId: id, type: "cancelled", message: "扫描已取消" });

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/twain-bridge.ts:17](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/f9ecba6caf39f98a651b1bfd765214c6deb876e3/src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/twain-bridge.ts#L17)

~~~~typescript
export function cancelScan(sessionId: string): boolean {
  cancelRequested.add(sessionId);

  const child = activeScans.get(sessionId);
  if (!child || child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) {
    return false; // 子进程尚未注册或已退出，交由 runBridge 的取消检查拦截
  }

  child.kill(); // Windows 下 SIGTERM → TerminateProcess

  const pid = child.pid;
  setTimeout(() => {
    if (child.exitCode === null && pid) {
      execFile("taskkill", ["/F", "/T", "/PID", String(pid)], { windowsHide: true }, () => {
        // 强杀结果不阻塞调用方；失败时进程也会被 10 分钟超时兜底
      });
    }
  }, 2000).unref();
  return true;
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/twain-bridge.ts:102](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/f9ecba6caf39f98a651b1bfd765214c6deb876e3/src/apps/answer-card/server/scanner/twain-bridge.ts#L102)

~~~~typescript
    child.on("error", (error) => {
      clearTimeout(timeout);
      if (sessionId) {
        activeScans.delete(sessionId);
      }
      reject(error);
    });

    child.on("close", (code) => {
      clearTimeout(timeout);
      if (sessionId) {
        activeScans.delete(sessionId);
      }
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The calibrated impact is low because the supported effect is bounded same-host availability loss with no demonstrated data, identity, or grading-integrity compromise. Likelihood is low because the stale watchdog is proven but collateral harm depends on a short Windows PID-reuse race plus scanner enablement and authorized access. Under the required matrix, impact=low with any non-ignore likelihood yields low. Hard suppression does not apply: this is not self-only, operator-only, developer-only, or purely local correctness behavior. A scanner or grading principal is authorized to cancel the scanner child but is not thereby authorized to terminate unrelated host processes, so the issue represents a limited privilege-boundary delta in a supported production scanner workflow. The strong counterevidence—loopback binding, default-disabled web TWAIN surface, default authentication, session-state checks, and the unproven final PID collision—keeps the finding at low rather than making it non-reportable.

路径
Scanner/full API-key client or GRADE_WRITE user --uses authorized scanner endpoint when TWAIN is enabled--> POST /api/scanner/scan creates an active native scan session --supplies the returned nonterminal session ID--> POST /api/scanner/scan/:sessionId/cancel --route invokes cancelScan--> cancelScan calls child.kill() and schedules a two-second watchdog --termination signal ends the original child--> Scanner child closes with exitCode null and signalCode set --watchdog remains pending after close and holds only the numeric PID--> Windows potentially reuses the released numeric PID --watchdog checks only exitCode === null--> taskkill /F /T /PID targets the current holder of that PID --forced recursive termination under server-account authority--> Unrelated same-host process tree may be terminated

Attacker story: a holder of a scanner/full API key or a user with GRADE_WRITE starts a native scan and cancels its returned session while scanner-bridge.exe is active. The cancellation route calls cancelScan, which sends child.kill() and schedules taskkill for two seconds later. A signal-terminated Node child can have exitCode === null while signalCode is set even after close, but the watchdog checks only exitCode. The close/error handlers remove the child from activeScans without cancelling the watchdog. The supplied executable harness exercised these production functions and observed the original child close approximately 105 ms after cancellation, followed by taskkill dispatch for the now-absent PID at two seconds. If Windows reuses that PID, taskkill /F /T can terminate the replacement process tree under the server account. The strongest counterevidence is that native TWAIN is disabled by default for web deployments, Electron enables it on a loopback-bound server, default authentication requires a scanner/full key or GRADE_WRITE, and actual harm additionally depends on an unproven Windows PID-reuse collision in a short interval. Existing verification tests cover cancellation before child registration rather than this active-child watchdog, so their success does not negate the stale-PID behavior. No secret data flows through this flaw; the security effect is limited to host availability.

发生可能性
Low - The stale-PID taskkill dispatch is directly reproducible, and authorized scanner clients or GRADE_WRITE users can invoke the normal start/cancel workflow. Nevertheless, the final impact requires Windows, explicitly enabled native scanning, an active bridge, suitable authorization, signal-driven exit, and PID reuse in an approximately two-second window. The service also binds to loopback and the web deployment disables TWAIN by default. Repeated churn may increase opportunity but does not make the collision deterministic.
影响程度
Low - Successful exploitation can terminate one unrelated same-host process tree that the Project-X service account is allowed to kill. This crosses the intended boundary of cancelling only scanner-bridge.exe, but the demonstrated consequence is availability loss rather than confidentiality, grade integrity, identity compromise, or fleet-wide impact. The attacker cannot deterministically choose the replacement process, and no actual collateral termination was demonstrated.
假设条件
The affected deployment runs on Windows, where the fallback invokes taskkill.
Native TWAIN scanning is enabled through PROJECTX_ENABLE_SCANNER=1/true or PROJECTX_VARIANT=teacher-scanner.
The attacker possesses a scanner/full API key or an authenticated account with GRADE_WRITE; alternatively, the operator has explicitly disabled authentication.
A scanner session reaches the point where scanner-bridge.exe is registered in activeScans and remains live when cancellation is requested.
The original scanner child exits after child.kill(), but its numeric PID is reassigned to another process before the two-second watchdog runs.
Remote reachability requires a reverse proxy, tunnel, or comparable ingress to the loopback-bound Express service; the checkout does not establish such ingress for the scanner-enabled deployment.
Windows host
Native TWAIN scanner feature enabled
Valid scanner/full API key or authenticated GRADE_WRITE account under the default enforced-auth configuration
Active scanner-bridge child associated with a nonterminal session
Signal-driven child exit before the two-second watchdog
Numeric PID reuse during the remaining watchdog interval
An unrelated process that the Project-X OS account is permitted to terminate receives the reused PID
控制措施
Native TWAIN routes are feature-gated and disabled by default in the web package.
The server binds to 127.0.0.1; nonlocal access requires separate ingress.
Authentication is enabled by default.
Scanner routes require a scanner/full API key or GRADE_WRITE permission when authentication is enforced.
The cancellation route verifies that the session exists and is not already completed, errored, or cancelled.
cancelScan initially rejects missing or already-terminated children by checking both exitCode and signalCode.
The forced fallback waits two seconds, narrowing the PID-reuse window.
taskkill executes without a shell and receives the PID as a fixed argument, preventing command injection.
No general rate limit constrains repeated scan/start cancellation churn.
The close/error handlers remove activeScans state but do not cancel or revalidate the forced-kill watchdog.
盲点
The validation host was Linux; a real Windows taskkill execution and actual PID-reuse collision were not observed.
Repository artifacts do not establish whether a scanner-enabled deployment is exposed through a reverse proxy, tunnel, or LAN ingress.
Windows PID allocation and reuse frequency under realistic scanner workload are not established by repository evidence.
The identity and privilege level of the production Windows service account are deployment-specific and unknown.
The executable validation harness instrumented the taskkill sink and proved stale dispatch after child close, but did not prove termination of a replacement process.
No manifests in the examined checkout establish OS-level process isolation, service recovery, or endpoint rate limiting outside the application.
