### [src/server/services/ReviewService.ts:233](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/df48a8ea0fc4286336d22928450397ac60fbce77/src/server/services/ReviewService.ts#L233)

~~~~typescript
    scoreBreakdown.push({ round: finalReviewRound, reviewerId: params.userId, score: totalScore, reviewedAt: now, questionScores });

    // 仅在所有轮次完成且无争议后才写正式分数，避免最后一评的分数提前影响排名。
    if (scoreBreakdown.length >= reviewMode) {
      const allScores = scoreBreakdown.map((b) => b.score);
      const disputeResult = computeMultiReviewResult(allScores, config.disputeThreshold, config.rounding);
      disputed = disputeResult.disputed;
      disputeReason = disputeResult.reason;
      if (!disputed) {
        finalScore = disputeResult.finalScore;
        const resolvedQuestionScores: Array<{ item: typeof submittedScores[number]; score: number }> = [];
        for (const item of submittedScores) {
          const values = scoreBreakdown.map((round) => round.questionScores?.[String(item.questionNumber)]).filter((v): v is number => typeof v === "number");
          if (values.length !== scoreBreakdown.length) throw new Error("历史评审缺少逐题分数，无法安全合并，请转仲裁处理");
          const resolved = computeMultiReviewResult(values, config.disputeThreshold, config.rounding);
          if (resolved.disputed || resolved.finalScore == null) {
            disputed = true;
            disputeReason = `第${item.questionNumber}题${resolved.reason}`;
            finalScore = null;
            break;
          }
          resolvedQuestionScores.push({ item, score: resolved.finalScore });
        }
        // Do not persist a partial set if a later question is disputed.
        if (!disputed) {
          for (const resolved of resolvedQuestionScores) {
            await tx.run(upsertSQL, params.examId, crop.student_id, resolved.item.questionNumber, null, crop.block_id, resolved.score, resolved.item.maxScore, resolved.item.scoreType, 1, params.userId, now);
          }
        }
        if (!disputed) totalScore = await recomputeStudentTotals(tx, params.examId, crop.student_id!, params.userId, now);
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Technical calibration is medium impact multiplied by low likelihood, which mechanically yields low severity. The integrity effect is real once the prerequisite exists, and the executable validation proves that conditional behavior. The final policy pass nevertheless suppresses the finding because the PoC seeded the disputed row through a protected database write, while normal current-schema crop creation defaults review_round to 1 and ReviewService caps it as though it began at 0. This appears to prevent an ordinary teacher from producing the required multi-review dispute at this commit, and no alternate production writer was identified. Thus repository evidence does not establish a realistic lower-privileged attacker path to the prerequisite. Suppression is based on the protected or highly unrealistic prerequisite, not on the service's loopback binding or authenticated nature.

路径
Authenticated teacher previously involved in the review --Sends authenticated remote request--> Documented public Cloudflare tunnel to loopback Node service --Forwards HTTPS traffic to 127.0.0.1:5174--> POST /api/review/exams/:examId/block-crops/:cropId/submit --Requires exam access and grading permissions--> grade:read, grade:write, and exam-visibility checks --No assignment or expected-reviewer authorization follows--> Duplicate guard disabled for disputed status --Disputed status permits an existing reviewer to submit again--> Repeated score appended to score_breakdown --All entries are counted as independent observations--> 3P+ median-adjacent majority resolver --Resolved result is persisted and totals/rankings are recomputed--> question_scores, student_scores, and rankings

Attacker story: a teacher who previously gave one of the scores on a disputed answer crop repeats their preferred score through the ordinary review API. Although the route authenticates the teacher, checks grade permissions, and checks exam visibility, the service deliberately disables its duplicate-review guard whenever status is disputed. It then appends the repeated score and supplies every history entry to a resolver that does not distinguish unique reviewers. Two identical entries from one teacher can therefore become the median-adjacent majority and overwrite the student's authoritative score. The supplied executable validation confirmed the conditional effect through the authenticated Express endpoint, including changes to question_scores and student_scores. The operation remains visible in score_breakdown and is limited to one crop per request, so the impact is medium rather than high. The decisive reportability limitation is reachability of the prerequisite at this exact commit: repository defaults initialize review_round to 1, and the service increments and caps it as if it began at 0. This appears to prevent the second review needed to create a normal disputed crop, and no alternate production writer of disputed status was found. The validation therefore required a seeded database row. The vulnerable branch should still be fixed, but the absence of a realistic lower-privileged path to its prerequisite triggers final policy suppression.

发生可能性
Low - If a qualifying disputed row exists, exploitation is simple and the supplied executable PoC confirms it through the normal authenticated HTTP endpoint. Public tunnel deployment can make the endpoint remotely reachable, and ordinary teachers hold the required grade permissions. However, the current schema initializes review_round to 1 while the service treats it as zero-based; this appears to reject the review that would normally create the disputed state. No alternate production writer of disputed status was found. Existing restored or manually altered databases could still contain such rows, but repository evidence does not establish that as a realistic lower-privileged path.
影响程度
Medium - A successful request changes another student's authoritative per-question score, total score, and potentially ranking, crossing the independent-review and arbitration integrity boundary. The effect is bounded to a valid score range and one crop per request, requires a teacher account, and remains visible in review history. The supplied threat model classifies recoverable one-student score changes with audit evidence as medium impact.
假设条件
Authentication remains enabled, as it is by default unless PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is explicitly set to 0 or false.
The attacker is an ordinary authenticated teacher whose role has grade:read and grade:write and whose teacher scope includes the target exam.
Remote exposure follows the documented production deployment using Cloudflare Tunnel to publish the loopback-bound service.
The current-schema review_round default and submission logic are representative of normal crop creation at this commit.
An authenticated teacher account with grade:read and grade:write
Visibility of the target exam through requireExamAccess
Knowledge of a crop ID belonging to that exam
A multi-review crop already in status disputed
The attacker must already appear in that crop's score_breakdown
The disputed row must exist despite the current review_round initialization defect that appears to block ordinary creation of such a row
控制措施
Authentication is enabled by default and accepts an opaque bearer token or authentication cookie.
The /api/review mount applies a grade:read gate, and the submit route separately requires grade:write.
requireExamAccess restricts scoped teachers to visible exams and blocks students from this route.
The crop query requires the crop ID to belong to the supplied exam.
Submitted scores are clamped to zero and the configured question maximum.
Existing reviewers are rejected while the crop is not disputed.
The configured arbitrator is prohibited from participating through the initial-review submission path.
Disputed re-review is capped at reviewMode plus two history entries.
Transactional updates and a review_round compare-and-swap protect against concurrent overwrites.
Review history stores reviewer IDs, scores, and timestamps, making manipulation auditable.
The dedicated arbitration endpoint requires the configured arbitrator.
No general request rate limit was found for repeated grading submissions.
No assignment or expected-reviewer authorization protects the affected disputed-crop submission.
盲点
Static repository analysis cannot determine whether a deployed or restored production database already contains disputed rows created by an older version, manual correction, or external tooling.
The supplied executable PoC proved the vulnerable branch but seeded the already-disputed database state rather than generating it through sequential production HTTP requests.
No deployment state was inspected, so the documented Cloudflare ingress establishes supported public exposure rather than proving that every installation is publicly reachable.
Repository search did not find another production writer of disputed status, but out-of-repository database administration or import processes could create the prerequisite.
The interaction of historical databases with earlier application versions was not dynamically reconstructed.

概要

Introduced: the commit adds importable seed and backup workflows that install active accounts with publicly documented fixed passwords. In particular, the new teacher is left without a fine-grained teacher role, which activates the application's backward-compatibility path granting visibility to all exams and combines with the teacher role's write permissions.

The new seed command defaults to the application's current database and unconditionally creates `demo-teacher` with the hardcoded password `teacher123`. The documentation publishes this credential and recommends both seeding the current database and restoring the bundled database. The account is created as a teacher without a `teacher_role`; the existing authorization logic treats such legacy teachers as having access to every exam, while the default teacher role includes card, exam, and grading write permissions. Consequently, after an operator imports the dataset into an Internet-reachable installation, an unauthenticated attacker who knows the repository can log in with the published credential, read school-wide exam data, and modify or delete cards, exams, and grades. The full-restore path additionally packages a database initialized with the documented `admin/admin123` bootstrap account. Exploitation is conditional on an operator explicitly importing this test package, but neither import mode restricts itself to an isolated development database, disables the accounts, generates one-time credentials, or warns that the resulting installation must not be exposed. The demo teacher should be disabled by default or use a randomly generated, forced-rotation credential and a scoped `subject_teacher` role limited to the demo classes; production/nonempty databases should require an explicit unsafe override.

验证
 The documented seed/restore workflows are operator-reachable, target the current/default application database, and have no production/nonempty-DB safety gate.
 Import creates active accounts with repository-public fixed credentials; both teacher and bundled admin hashes were verified.
 The seeded teacher has NULL teacher_role, no class assignments, and authenticates through the real login endpoint without forced rotation.
 The account receives unrestricted exam visibility and effective card/exam/grade read/write capabilities at the actual authorization controls.
 A focused isolated reproduction under enforced authentication demonstrated cross-subject data access, exam mutation/deletion, and grade modification.
验证输出结果

- Directly supporting impact: the default teacher role has card, exam, and grading read/write permissions.
- Publishes the demo teacher's username and password.
- Recommends restoring the bundled full database or writing the seed directly into the current database without an exposure warning or credential-rotation step.
- With no explicit path, the seed targets the normal application database rather than requiring an isolated test database.
- Creates the active teacher with the fixed password `teacher123` and omits `teacher_role`, leaving it null.

~~~~typescript
 * - 普通 teacher（无 teacher_role）→ 全部可见（向后兼容）
~~~~

攻击路径分析

Technical impact remains high: testdata/demo-exams/scripts/seed.ts:238-244 creates the fixed-password teacher without teacher_role; src/server/auth/permissions.ts:68-79 grants teacher read/write powers; and src/apps/answer-card/server/index.ts:356-400 makes NULL-role teachers globally scoped. The supplied executable validation confirmed sensitive reads and destructive writes. Nevertheless, the strongest repository counterevidence is dispositive under the requested policy: testdata/README.md:1-18 labels this as an explicitly imported test fixture, import-all.sh:12-29 requires a shell-capable operator or administrator, and backup.ts:17-24 protects restore with USER_MANAGE. The service also needs documented proxy exposure because index.ts:2071-2085 binds loopback. Therefore the prerequisite is privileged, operator-initiated incorporation of test data into a reachable deployment. Applying hard suppression first yields likelihood=ignore; the provided impact/likelihood matrix then mechanically yields adjusted_criticality=ignore, without disputing the high conditional impact.

路径
Privileged operator imports test/demo package --seed or restore creates the account--> Active fixed-password demo teacher is installed in runtime database --active bcrypt-backed account authenticates--> Server issues teacher bearer token --session identity contains teacher_role = NULL--> NULL teacher_role activates unrestricted legacy exam visibility --object-scope filter returns unrestricted visibility--> Teacher read/write permissions authorize exam and grade routes --exam and grade APIs perform authorized reads and writes--> Student records disclosed and academic data modified or deleted

The demo seed selects data/projectx.db when no alternate path is supplied, initializes it, and creates an active teacher with a fixed repository-documented password while omitting teacher_role. UserRepository persists the omitted role as NULL. Authentication accepts the credential and issues a token carrying the default teacher role's broad card, exam, and grading permissions. The exam visibility function deliberately returns an unrestricted scope for NULL-role legacy teachers, so the imported account crosses subject and class boundaries. The supplied executable validation enabled authentication, logged in through the real API, read exams and identifiable scores outside the account's nominal Mathematics scope, changed a question score, renamed an exam, and deleted an exam. The strongest contrary evidence is scoping rather than exploitability: the artifact is explicitly test data, import is never automatic, seed mode requires privileged shell access, restore requires USER_MANAGE, and the server itself binds to loopback. Public reachability is nevertheless supported by the documented Nginx deployment. Thus the post-import security impact is real and high, but triggering the vulnerable state requires a privileged operator to install a test fixture into a reachable runtime. The supplied hard-suppression policy therefore makes it non-reportable despite the demonstrated conditional impact.

发生可能性
High - The network exploit is straightforward after import, uses a repository-public credential, and was reproduced through the real API. However, creation of the vulnerable state requires a privileged operator to deliberately run a test seed against the runtime database or use an administrator-protected restore operation, then leave the imported account unchanged and expose the service. The package is explicitly separated test data rather than an automatic production migration. Under the supplied hard-suppression rule for operator-only or protected-write-path preconditions, likelihood is classified as ignore for final reportability. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - Once the test package is imported into a populated runtime database, the account's unrestricted legacy scope and teacher write permissions permit broad disclosure of identifiable exam results and integrity or availability attacks against exams and grades. The executable validation demonstrated cross-subject reads, score modification, exam renaming, and exam deletion. This is high conditional impact because academic records are sensitive and grade integrity is a primary protected asset.
假设条件
A shell-capable operator runs the demo seed against the application's current database, or an authenticated administrator restores the bundled demo backup.
The imported demo account remains active and its repository-public fixed password is not changed before the service becomes reachable.
The API is exposed through the documented HTTPS reverse-proxy deployment despite the Node process binding to loopback.
Authentication enforcement is enabled; otherwise the same data routes are already accessible without relying on the imported credential.
High-impact school-wide disclosure or modification through seed mode requires the current database to contain real exams or student records in addition to the demo data.
A privileged operator deliberately imports the repository's test/demo package into the runtime database.
The imported fixed-password account remains active and unchanged.
The resulting service remains reachable through a reverse proxy or equivalent ingress.
For impact beyond demo records, seed mode is run against a populated school database.
控制措施
Passwords are bcrypt-hashed rather than stored in plaintext in the runtime users table.
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, the business-route gate rejects unauthenticated requests and checks role permissions.
The restore endpoint independently requires authentication and USER_MANAGE permission.
The server binds to 127.0.0.1 and requires a proxy or tunnel for remote access.
Bearer tokens are randomly generated after successful password verification.
Object-level exam authorization exists, but its intentional NULL teacher_role compatibility branch fails open for this account.
The imported teacher is not assigned an explicit scoped teacher role, disabled state, random credential, or demonstrated forced-rotation requirement.
盲点
Repository evidence cannot establish whether any real deployment imported this package, retained the fixed credentials, or exposed the resulting service.
No deployment-specific ingress, firewall, or cloud configuration was inspected; remote scope is based on the supplied threat model and documented Nginx deployment.
The bundled backup's active credential state relies partly on the supplied validation's direct archive inspection; this analysis did not expose or reproduce credential hash material.
The validation server required a temporary startup-only dependency stub, although none of the authenticated exam or score paths exercised that dependency.
The executable validation used seeded demo records rather than an actual populated school database; broader real-record impact follows from the source-confirmed unrestricted visibility behavior.

概要

Introduced. Replace both new `indeterminate` literals with the existing `indefinite` ObjectiveMode value. In particular, the deletion condition should preserve scoring rules when `objective.mode` is either `multiple` or `indefinite`.

The commit introduces the string `indeterminate` in two places even though `ObjectiveMode` defines the value as `indefinite`. The comparison in the question-type change handler therefore produces TypeScript error TS2367, preventing the repository's normal `npm run build` workflow because it runs `tsc --noEmit`. If type checking is bypassed, selecting the valid `indefinite` mode makes both comparisons at line 2382 true and deletes every question's `scoringRule`. The grading implementation relies on that rule to award partial credit, so subsequent grading can silently award zero or fallback scores across all affected questions and students. The same typo also causes automatically generated titles for indefinite-choice blocks to use the generic objective-question label. This is an integrity and release-quality bug, but not a security-boundary violation: triggering it requires an authorized card editor changing grading configuration, an actor who already has permission to alter those rules.

验证
 Confirmed the canonical ObjectiveMode is indefinite and both claimed indeterminate literals exist at this commit and were introduced by it.
 Confirmed the normal npm run build invokes type checking and reproduced TS2367 at App.tsx:2382.
 Confirmed the valid indefinite mode is selectable and dynamically reproduced deletion of every normalized question's scoring rule.
 Traced persistence and production grading consumption, and dynamically demonstrated a configured partial score changing from 1 to 0 with a corrected-literal control remaining at 1.
 Assessed reachability and permissions: this is an authorized-editor correctness/integrity defect rather than a security-boundary crossing under the stated production assumptions.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The new title mapping uses the nonexistent `indeterminate` mode, so valid `indefinite` blocks receive the generic fallback title.
- The invalid mode literal causes TS2367 and makes the runtime condition delete scoring rules for every valid `indefinite` question.
- The canonical ObjectiveMode union confirms that the supported value is `indefinite`, not `indeterminate`.

攻击路径分析

Before policy suppression, the established medium data-integrity impact combined mechanically with low likelihood yields low severity. The final policy pass then applies the protected-write-path and privileged-only suppression rule: under the production threat model, triggering persistence requires grade:write, and the actor already possesses the authority to directly change or remove the affected grading rules. There is no verified privilege escalation, authorization bypass, or cross-boundary capability gain. The default authentication-disabled compatibility mode does not rescue reportability because it already permits arbitrary card writes and is outside the assumed hardened production posture. The issue should therefore be fixed as a release-blocking grading-correctness defect but ignored as a security vulnerability.

路径
Authorized teacher/editor selects indefinite mode --normal designer dropdown event--> App.tsx typo treats indefinite as an unsupported mode --incorrect indeterminate comparison--> Per-question scoringRule values are deleted --1.2-second autosave serializes mutated card--> Autosave PUT /api/cards/:cardId passes grade:write gate --authorized persistence--> CardRepository stores scoring_rule_json as NULL --production grading consumes missing rule--> Grading or recomputation uses zero or legacy fallback

The canonical ObjectiveMode is single, multiple, or indefinite, but App.tsx uses indeterminate in both the auto-title map and the block-mode change handler. In a transpile-only or otherwise non-typechecked execution, an authorized editor selecting indefinite causes the handler to delete each question's scoringRule. Autosave then submits the card to the card API, and CardRepository persists each absent rule as NULL. Production grading permits partial credit for multiple and indefinite modes but falls back to the wrong/extra score, normally zero, when no rule or legacy fallback exists. The supplied executable reproduction demonstrated a partial score changing from 1 to 0. The strongest counterevidence against runtime impact is that npm run build executes tsc first and rejects the comparison with TS2367, so a normal release cannot be built from this commit. The strongest counterevidence against security reportability is the grade:write gate: the realistic trigger is an actor already authorized to modify these grading rules, and no lower-privileged attack or privilege delta is established. This is therefore a confirmed product correctness and grading-integrity defect, but it is suppressed as a security finding.

发生可能性
Low - The UI path is straightforward for an authorized editor and the supplied executable reproduction confirmed the mutation and score change. Nevertheless, runtime exploitation requires an actor with the protected grading-write capability, a card with relevant partial-credit rules, persistence followed by grading, and a runtime path that bypasses the normal typechecked build. The ordinary build rejects this commit before release, and no realistic lower-privileged attacker path is established. These conditions make security exploitation likelihood low.
影响程度
Medium - If the defective frontend runs and its change is saved, deletion of partial-credit rules can alter objective scores and recomputed totals for multiple students, making the correctness impact material. The effect is not universal because a legacy multipleScoring rule can supply fallback behavior, and the typo also causes only a cosmetic title defect. From a security perspective, the affected actor is already authorized to alter the same grading configuration, so the impact is data-integrity correctness rather than an authorization or trust-boundary violation.
假设条件
Production follows the supplied threat model and enables PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE, even though repository compatibility behavior leaves it disabled by default.
The loopback-bound Node service may be exposed through an operator-managed reverse proxy or Cloudflare Tunnel as described in the supplied threat model; no checked-in ingress manifest establishes a specific deployment.
The affected card already contains per-question partial-credit scoring rules and does not have a legacy block-level multipleScoring rule that would mask the deletion.
Runtime impact requires a transpile-only development workflow, a prebuilt artifact containing the defect, or another path that bypasses the normal typechecked build.
An authenticated teacher/editor has legitimate authority to modify the affected card's grading configuration.
An actor with grade:write privileges, or an explicitly authentication-disabled compatibility deployment
An objective block with per-question partial-credit scoring rules
Selection of the valid indefinite block mode in the answer-card designer
Successful autosave or manual save of the mutated card
Execution through a transpile-only, development, or prebuilt path because the normal build rejects the typo
Later grading or score recomputation using the affected card
Absence of a legacy block-level multipleScoring rule that fully masks the deleted per-question rule
控制措施
The normal npm run build invokes tsc --noEmit first and fails on TS2367 at the invalid comparison, limiting release exposure.
With PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE enabled, non-GET /api/cards requests require authentication and grade:write.
Students do not receive card or grading write privileges in the default permission mapping.
The design UI is gated by the design variant and CARD_READ permission.
The server normalizes objective questions before persistence, but it does not reconstruct a deleted per-question scoring rule.
A legacy block-level multipleScoring rule may mitigate the score effect for some older cards.
The service binds directly to 127.0.0.1, although the supplied production model permits external exposure through a proxy or tunnel.
No secret access, code execution, identity takeover, or executable sink is involved.
盲点
The executable reproduction duplicated the small inline React mutation rather than driving an actual browser and live HTTP/database integration.
No checked-in reverse-proxy, Cloudflare Tunnel, or load-balancer manifest establishes the exact deployed network exposure.
Static repository evidence does not establish whether any production or distributed prebuilt bundle contains this commit despite the failing normal build.
The number of cards, questions, exams, or students actually affected cannot be determined from repository artifacts.
A deployment that intentionally leaves PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE disabled would make card writes unauthenticated, but that broader configuration weakness is distinct from this typo and provides no typo-specific capability gain.

## 46. Autosave race can desynchronize printed cards and grading layout

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/4823f95f748c8191b21e657152e10cf2?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

014cef0
11:25 PM Jun 19, 2026

操作者：Toyama Kasumi

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This commit introduces the race by adding asynchronous autosave and replacing the direct PDF link with a save-then-export sequence that does not wait for edits made during the save. The local revision check protects the React state from being replaced by an old response, but it does not stop exportPdfForCurrentCard from consuming that old response or ensure the subsequently opened PDF corresponds to the final revision.

PDF export captures a card snapshot and revision, sends it to the server, and unconditionally returns that saved snapshot. If the teacher edits the card while this request is in flight, persistCardSnapshot detects the newer local revision but only schedules another autosave; it still returns the older server response. exportPdfForCurrentCard immediately validates that older response and opens its PDF URL, while the newer revision is saved approximately 1.2 seconds later. The editor inputs are not locked while the PDF save is running, so this race is reachable during ordinary use, particularly through a remote reverse proxy. The result can be a printed answer card generated from the older question numbering, score fields, or geometry while the server subsequently retains the newer card and uses its rebuilt layout for scanning and grading. This can silently misrecognize or misgrade an entire batch. The export path should either prevent edits until export completes or continue saving until savedRevisionRef equals editRevisionRef, then generate a PDF bound to that exact immutable revision.

验证
 Establish that a card edit can occur while the PDF-initiated save is in flight; editor inputs are not disabled by isBusy.
 Establish that the revision check detects but does not await a newer revision, and that the stale save response is returned to export.
 Establish that the PDF URL is not bound to an immutable revision and the server renders mutable stored card state.
 Reproduce through the real HTTP save/PDF interfaces and verify the opened PDF contains revision A while deferred autosave leaves revision B on the server.
 Reproduce through a full browser UI and then scan/grade the mismatched physical card; Electron startup was blocked by a missing host library, and physical grading was out of scope for the bounded test.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The save detects a newer edit and schedules another autosave, but still returns the older saved snapshot to its caller.
- The flush operation captures only the current snapshot and revision; it does not loop until all edits made during the request have been persisted.
- PDF export immediately validates and opens the snapshot returned by the first save, even when persistCardSnapshot observed that it was already stale.
- Edits remain possible during the PDF save, increment the revision, and defer persistence through a new 1.2-second autosave timer.

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx:511](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/014cef038f8716e26b85754ab4a128aacd38d709/src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx#L511)

~~~~tsx
  async function persistCardSnapshot(
    snapshot: AnswerCard,
    revision: number,
    source: "auto" | "manual" | "switch" | "pdf" = "manual"
  ): Promise<AnswerCard> {
    setAutoSaveState("saving");
    if (source !== "auto") {
      setIsBusy(true);
      setStatus(source === "pdf" ? "正在保存并检查分值..." : "正在保存答题卡...");
    }
    try {
      const saved = await fetchJson<AnswerCard>(`/api/cards/${snapshot.id}`, {
        method: "PUT",
        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
        body: JSON.stringify(snapshot)
      });

      const stillCurrent = latestCardRef.current?.id === snapshot.id;
      const noNewerEdits = editRevisionRef.current === revision;
      if (stillCurrent && noNewerEdits) {
        latestCardRef.current = saved;
        savedRevisionRef.current = revision;
        setCard(saved);
        setAutoSaveState("saved");
      } else if (stillCurrent) {
        scheduleAutoSave();
      }

      try {
        await refreshCards();
      } catch {
        // Saving succeeded; a stale sidebar timestamp is less important than preserving edits.
      }

      if (source === "auto") setStatus("已自动保存");
      else if (source === "pdf") setStatus("已保存，正在检查分值");
      else if (source === "switch") setStatus("已保存当前答题卡");
      else setStatus("已保存，并生成坐标布局数据");
      return saved;
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx:560](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/014cef038f8716e26b85754ab4a128aacd38d709/src/apps/answer-card/client/App.tsx#L560)

~~~~tsx
  async function flushPendingCardSave(
    source: "auto" | "manual" | "switch" | "pdf" = "manual",
    force = false
  ): Promise<AnswerCard | null> {
    clearAutoSaveTimer();
    const snapshot = latestCardRef.current;
    if (!snapshot) return null;
    const revision = editRevisionRef.current;
    if (!force && revision === savedRevisionRef.current) return snapshot;
    return persistCardSnapshot(cloneCard(snapshot), revision, source);
~~~~

~~~~tsx
  async function exportPdfForCurrentCard() {
    let savedCard: AnswerCard | null = null;
    try {
      savedCard = await flushPendingCardSave("pdf");
    } catch {
      return;
    }
    if (!savedCard) return;
    const validation = validateCardScores(savedCard);
    const pdfUrl = urlWithToken(`/api/cards/${savedCard.id}/pdf?v=${encodeURIComponent(savedCard.updatedAt)}`);
    if (validation.issues.length > 0) {
      setPdfWarning({ validation, pdfUrl });
      setStatus(`分值检查发现 ${validation.issues.length} 个提示，请确认后导出`);
      return;
    }
    openPdf(pdfUrl);
    setStatus("正在打开 PDF...");
~~~~

~~~~tsx
  function updateCard(mutator: (draft: AnswerCard) => void) {
    if (!card) return;
    const draft = cloneCard(card);
    mutator(draft);
    editRevisionRef.current += 1;
    latestCardRef.current = draft;
    setCard(draft);
    setAutoSaveState("dirty");
    scheduleAutoSave();
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Technical calibration is high integrity impact multiplied by medium likelihood, which mechanically yields medium under the supplied matrix. The separate policy-adjustment pass then applies hard suppression: the demonstrated path requires a teacher or administrator already authorized to modify the same shared card through a protected write workflow, and the race grants no additional authorization, identity, data access, or mutation capability. A malicious authorized teacher could directly save or print either layout without exploiting the race. No student, unauthenticated user, scanner key, or less-privileged teacher is shown to trigger the victim's browser race or cross a trust boundary. Accordingly, despite the executable proof of a real operational defect and potential batch-level grading consequences, the privileged-only/no-privilege-delta rule changes final reportability from medium to ignore.

路径
Authorized teacher in card-design UI --clicks PDF with pending edits--> PDF export captures revision A and issues PUT /api/cards/:cardId --network response remains pending--> Teacher creates revision B while PUT response is in flight --edit revision changes before response--> Revision check schedules B autosave but returns stale saved object A --export consumes stale return value--> GET /api/cards/:cardId/pdf renders current mutable record A --1.2-second autosave later persists B--> Deferred autosave stores B and replaces server layout --printed and grading revisions diverge--> Printed A may be scanned or graded using layout B

The executable validation demonstrates a real race in a production answer-card workflow: revision A was rendered as a 9,508-byte single-sided PDF with five student-number columns, while the deferred autosave left revision B on the server as double-sided with nine columns. The client notices that A is stale but merely schedules another autosave and returns A to export. The PDF endpoint has no immutable revision lookup and renders the current database row. Public reverse-proxy deployment is documented, so remote latency can enlarge the race window even though Express binds to loopback. Existing controls preserve revision B in memory, eventually persist it, validate scores, and disable the PDF/Save buttons, but ordinary editor inputs remain active. Strongest counterevidence is that the only demonstrated actor is an already authorized teacher editing a card they are allowed to change; no student, unauthenticated user, or less-privileged teacher gains access or integrity authority. The same teacher can intentionally create either card state without exploiting the race. The issue is therefore a confirmed high-consequence correctness and operational-integrity defect, but not a reportable security vulnerability under the required protected-write-path suppression policy.

发生可能性
High - The code path is reachable in ordinary card editing, remote proxy latency creates a practical response window, editor controls remain active, and the supplied executable reproduction produced distinct stale and final PDFs using real endpoints and rendering. Occurrence still requires precise edit timing plus subsequent printing and grading without detection, and there is no realistic lower-privileged attacker path or security-control bypass. | Remote network vector
影响程度
High - The proven stale PDF changes title, sidedness, and student-number geometry, and the newer server record rebuilds a different grading layout. If the stale document is printed for a batch and later scanned against the new layout, integrity impact could span many students and require rescanning or regrading. However, physical scanning and actual misgrading were not demonstrated, the newer edit is not lost, and the authorized operator can inspect or regenerate the PDF.
假设条件
Production follows the supplied threat model by enabling PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE and placing the loopback Express service behind an HTTPS reverse proxy.
The actor is an authenticated teacher or administrator permitted to design and persist answer cards.
The stale PDF is printed or distributed before the later autosave updates the server-side card and grading layout.
Scanning or grading later uses the newest mutable card definition rather than an immutable revision matching the printed PDF.
Access to an authenticated card-design session with permission to edit the affected card
A dirty card followed by invocation of the PDF export action
A second card edit while the export-triggered PUT request is in flight
The first PUT response and PDF GET completing before the 1.2-second deferred autosave persists the second edit
Use of the stale PDF for printing or distribution without detecting the discrepancy
Subsequent recognition or grading against the newer server-side card layout
控制措施
Production threat-model assumption that PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled
Card route RBAC rejects unauthenticated or insufficiently privileged callers when enforcement is enabled
Design UI is permission-gated
Revision comparison prevents an old response from overwriting newer React state
Deferred autosave eventually persists the newer edit
PDF and manual Save buttons are disabled while the export-triggered save is busy
Score validation may require confirmation before opening warning-bearing PDFs
PDF responses use no-store and no-cache headers
No executable-code sink or secret flow is involved
盲点
The supplied reproduction did not run an end-to-end physical scan or grading batch, so actual recognition and score corruption remain inferred from the proven geometry mismatch.
The race outcome varies with refreshCards timing and network scheduling; a later write can win before the mutable PDF endpoint reads the card.
Actual deployment configuration is unavailable, including whether PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled and whether the documented public reverse proxy is used.
The deployment guide suggests setting HOST=0.0.0.0, but the inspected server implementation hard-codes 127.0.0.1; public exposure therefore depends on a separate reverse proxy.
Direct Electron browser automation was unavailable in validation because a host GUI library was missing, although the real server, PUT/PDF endpoints, validator, and PDF renderer were exercised.
No repository evidence establishes a way for a lower-privileged actor to force the required concurrent edit inside another user's card-design session.

## 47. Vertical option layout is discarded when cards are persisted

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/b9b5623efbfc8191b92d847962604789?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Vertical option layout is discarded when cards are persisted
严重级别
Informational

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
c3d9434
10:59 AM Jun 19, 2026

操作者：xiaoxu66609

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced. The commit exposes and consumes a new option-layout field without implementing its end-to-end normalization and database persistence.

The commit adds `optionLayout` at both block and question level and uses it to select the new `vertical-grid` layout. However, `CardRepository` neither writes this property to `objective_blocks` or `objective_questions` nor reconstructs it in `findById()`. Consequently, a normal user can select vertical layout and receive a successful PUT response with a vertical layout, but the next GET loses the setting and `objectiveQuestionDefinitions()` defaults it to `horizontal`. Server-side PDF generation, layout regeneration, and recognition routes load cards through `findById()`, so they also use the reverted horizontal arrangement. Per-question settings are lost even earlier because `normalizeObjectiveQuestions()` omits `question.optionLayout` from its returned configuration. This is not an authorization-boundary vulnerability: only users already permitted to design cards can trigger it, and the documented PDF workflow generally produces a consistently horizontal card. It is nevertheless a reliability defect that can create inconsistent exported or cached layouts and could produce incorrect recognition if a vertical preview or stale vertical layout is used as the physical-card source. Persist the field through a database migration and repository read/write paths, include it in `normalizeObjectiveQuestions()`, and add a save/reload/PDF round-trip test.

验证
 Confirm that the normal card-design input and PUT endpoint accept block-level optionLayout: vertical and report success.
 Demonstrate a save/reload round trip loses the block-level property and verify that neither objective database table has a persistence column for it.
 Demonstrate that normalizeObjectiveQuestions() discards a submitted per-question optionLayout before persistence.
 Show that loss of the property materially changes production layout coordinates from vertical-grid to horizontal after reload.
 Directly print/parse a PDF and scan a physical vertical card through the native recognizer; these downstream paths were source-traced, but the conditional recognition error was not executed.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The new user-facing selector stores vertical layout in `ObjectiveBlock.optionLayout`.
- The database write path stores neither block-level nor question-level `optionLayout`.
- The database read path reconstructs objective blocks and questions without either layout property, causing the setting to disappear after reload.
- Question normalization reconstructs every question configuration but omits the newly added `optionLayout`, discarding question-level values.
- Layout selection depends on the lost property; once reloaded without it, normalized questions default to horizontal rather than `vertical-grid`.

~~~~typescript
  private insertObjectiveBlock(block: any, cardId: string): void {
    const questions = normalizeObjectiveQuestions(block);
    const firstQuestion = questions[0];
    const stmt = this.db.prepare(`
      INSERT INTO objective_blocks (id, card_id, sort_order, title, question_start, question_count, option_count, mode, score_per_question, density, wrong_or_extra_score)
      VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
    `);
    stmt.run(
      block.id,
      cardId,
      0,
      block.title ?? "",
      firstQuestion?.questionNumber ?? block.questionStart ?? 1,
      questions.length || block.questionCount || 0,
      firstQuestion?.optionCount ?? block.optionCount ?? 4,
      firstQuestion?.mode ?? block.mode ?? "single",
      firstQuestion?.score ?? block.scorePerQuestion ?? 0,
      block.density ?? "compact",
      firstQuestion?.scoringRule?.wrongOrExtraScore ?? block.multipleScoring?.wrongOrExtraScore ?? 0
    );

    const keyStmt = this.db.prepare(
      "INSERT OR REPLACE INTO objective_answer_keys (block_id, question_number, correct_options) VALUES (?, ?, ?)"
    );
    const questionStmt = this.db.prepare(
      `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO objective_questions (block_id, question_number, sort_order, mode, option_count, score, scoring_rule_json)
       VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`
    );
    questions.forEach((question, index) => {
      questionStmt.run(
        block.id,
        question.questionNumber,
        index,
        question.mode,
        question.optionCount,
        question.score,
        question.scoringRule ? JSON.stringify(question.scoringRule) : null
~~~~

### [src/shared/grading.ts:116](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c3d94344dd22c0125d49053e16ec70cf7790dd2d/src/shared/grading.ts#L116)

~~~~typescript
export function normalizeObjectiveQuestions(block: ObjectiveBlock): ObjectiveQuestionConfig[] {
  const answerKey = normalizeObjectiveAnswerKey(block);
  return objectiveQuestionDefinitions(block).map((question) => {
    const options = normalizeOptions(answerKey[question.questionNumber], question.optionCount);
    return {
      questionNumber: question.questionNumber,
      mode: question.mode,
      optionCount: question.optionCount,
      score: question.score,
      answerKey: question.mode === "single" && options.length > 1 ? [options[0]] : options,
      scoringRule: question.scoringRule
    };
~~~~

### [src/shared/layout.ts:155](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c3d94344dd22c0125d49053e16ec70cf7790dd2d/src/shared/layout.ts#L155)

~~~~typescript
function objectiveArrangementMode(questions: ObjectiveQuestionDefinition[]): ObjectiveArrangementMode {
  if (questions.some(isVerticalQuestion)) {
    return "vertical-grid";
  }
  return questions.length >= 15 ? "grid" : "rows";
}

function isWideObjectiveQuestion(question: ObjectiveQuestionDefinition): boolean {
  return question.optionCount > OBJECTIVE_WIDE_OPTION_THRESHOLD;
}

function isVerticalQuestion(question: ObjectiveQuestionDefinition): boolean {
  return question.optionLayout === "vertical";
}

function isSoloRowQuestion(question: ObjectiveQuestionDefinition): boolean {
  return isWideObjectiveQuestion(question);
}

function objectiveGridCellQuestions(mode: ObjectiveArrangementMode): number {
  return mode === "vertical-grid" ? OBJECTIVE_VERTICAL_GROUP_QUESTIONS : OBJECTIVE_GRID_CELL_QUESTIONS;
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The pre-policy severity remains low because the proven effect is a data-consistency and reliability failure, while the claimed academic-integrity effect requires an additional stale physical-card mismatch. The final policy pass applies hard suppression: the realistic trigger is an authorized or local card-write operation, and the actor gains no privilege or cross-boundary capability beyond their existing ability to rewrite the card. The strongest contrary fact is that PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE defaults off, but that is not dispositive for this finding because the checked-in service binds to localhost and, even behind an external proxy, an unauthenticated caller able to PUT an entire card can already corrupt it directly without relying on optionLayout loss. With impact=low and likelihood=ignore, the required matrix yields adjusted_criticality=ignore.

路径
Authorized teacher or local answer-card designer --selects the supported vertical arrangement--> React vertical-layout selector or crafted optionLayout JSON --client submits the complete card JSON--> PUT /api/cards/:cardId on Express answer-card service --server normalizes the submitted card--> normalizeCard() and normalizeObjectiveQuestions() --question-level value is omitted and repository has no persistence column--> CardRepository and objective_blocks/objective_questions tables --reload reconstructs no block-level or question-level layout property--> CardRepository.findById() returns card without optionLayout --missing property defaults to horizontal--> buildLayout(), PDF, and recognition regenerate horizontal coordinates --produces proven layout inconsistency and a conditional recognition-integrity risk--> Lost preference or conditional physical-card/recognition mismatch

Attacker story: a teacher or other user already allowed to design cards selects the supported vertical layout and saves the card. The PUT path computes and returns a vertical layout, but normalization omits question-level optionLayout and the repository schema/read-write paths omit both block-level and question-level values. A subsequent reload defaults the card to horizontal, and layout, PDF, and recognition paths that load through findById() use the reverted arrangement. The executable validation confirmed the vertical-to-horizontal coordinate change. The strongest counterevidence against security reportability is that this is a protected card-authoring operation when RBAC is enabled, the actor already has authority to alter the entire card and answer key, and no privilege, identity, exam, or confidentiality boundary is crossed. Although authentication enforcement is disabled by default, the service is loopback-bound and this omission adds no capability beyond direct card modification. Normal server-side PDF generation reloads the persisted card and therefore generally produces a consistently horizontal card; actual grading harm requires an additional stale or externally produced vertical physical card. These facts are dispositive for reportability, though not for the existence of the correctness defect.

发生可能性
Ignore - The functional defect is easy to trigger through normal use, but a meaningful security attack requires a card-authoring write path. Under enforced RBAC that path is available to teachers already able to modify the whole card and grading configuration; under the default configuration it is loopback-only absent unproven external ingress. The omission creates no security capability delta for a lower-privileged actor, so the privileged/protected-write-path suppression applies.
影响程度
Low - The executable PoC proves loss of a supported setting and inconsistent vertical-versus-horizontal coordinates after reload. No confidentiality disclosure, code execution, identity compromise, privilege escalation, or authorization bypass occurs. Recognition error is only a conditional integrity consequence requiring a stale or physical vertical card to be used after horizontal regeneration and was not demonstrated end to end.
假设条件
The evaluated checkout is exactly commit c3d94344dd22c0125d49053e16ec70cf7790dd2d.
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, card writes require an authenticated role satisfying the card gateway's write permission, which is grade:write in this commit; default teacher permissions include that permission.
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is disabled, the card routes do not require authentication, but the repository server binds to 127.0.0.1 by default and contains no deployment manifest proving external ingress.
Incorrect recognition requires a physical or cached vertical card to survive while recognition later regenerates a horizontal layout; that downstream mismatch was not demonstrated end to end.
The validation HTTP run used a temporary expr-eval loading stub unrelated to the card persistence, layout, PDF, or recognition code paths.
Ability to edit and save an answer-card definition through the designer or PUT /api/cards/:cardId
Selection or submission of optionLayout=vertical
A subsequent reload, PDF request, layout regeneration, or recognition operation that reads the card through CardRepository.findById()
For grading harm, use of a physical or stale vertical representation after the persisted card has reverted to horizontal
控制措施
optionalAuth parses bearer or cookie identity for all /api routes.
When PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is enabled, the /api/cards gateway returns 401 to unauthenticated callers and 403 when the required permission is absent.
Default student permissions do not include card or grade write access.
The HTTP listener binds to 127.0.0.1:5174 by default.
safeId normalizes card identifiers before repository and filesystem use.
PDF responses disable caching, although this does not repair semantic persistence.
Normal PDF and recognition workflows reload through CardRepository, which tends to produce a consistently horizontal artifact rather than preserving an unsupported transient vertical state.
盲点
No checked-in ingress, reverse-proxy, or load-balancer artifact establishes whether a particular deployment exposes port 5174 remotely.
PROJECTX_AUTH_ENFORCE is deployment-controlled, so the effective authentication boundary cannot be determined from the checkout alone.
Native OCR behavior against a physical vertical card was not executed.
PDF bytes were not visually parsed to confirm every rendered coordinate.
The validation server required a temporary expr-eval module-loading stub, although that dependency is outside the exercised card-layout path.
A browser printing or exporting a transient vertical preview as the physical-card source was not demonstrated from repository evidence.

## 48. Backup restore permits ZIP-bomb denial of service

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/2b5e8a2504048191a433c2900e05df6b?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

Backup restore permits ZIP-bomb denial of service
严重级别
Informational

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
c2a1745
10:53 PM Jun 17, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This range introduces the vulnerable full-backup restore feature. Its compressed upload limit does not constrain decompressed output, and the new extraction helper performs unbounded synchronous decompression before validating the backup structure.

Although the route requires the administrator-only user:manage permission, backup files remain untrusted inputs. The request parser limits only the compressed request body to 512 MiB. extractZipFromBuffer then synchronously enumerates every entry, calls entry.getData() to materialize each entry's complete uncompressed contents in memory, and writes it synchronously to disk. There is no limit on individual or cumulative uncompressed size, compression ratio, or entry count. Extraction also occurs before metadata.json and projectx.db are validated, so the malicious archive need not be a valid Project-X backup. An attacker who convinces an administrator to import a crafted ZIP bomb can block the Node event loop, exhaust heap or native buffer memory, fill temporary storage, and potentially terminate the server. If the process is killed, normal cleanup will not execute and the expanded temporary data can remain on disk. Restoration should enforce conservative entry-count, per-entry, and aggregate uncompressed-size limits while streaming extraction, reject suspicious compression ratios and unsupported entry types, and avoid synchronous whole-entry allocation.

验证
 Establish attacker-controlled ZIP reachability and required privilege: confirmed via the real account-menu upload flow, mounted HTTP route, successful administrator login, and user:manage middleware.
 Evaluate the nearest resource controls: confirmed that the 512 MiB parser limit applies only to compressed input and that the ZIP magic/path checks impose no decompressed-size, ratio, or entry-count limits.
 Trace validation order and sink behavior: confirmed extraction precedes backup-structure validation and uses synchronous whole-entry getData() plus writeFileSync.
 Reproduce amplification and availability impact through the production interface: a 261,030-byte archive expanded to 268,435,456 bytes and delayed a concurrent request for approximately 2.08 seconds.
 Verify cleanup behavior and characterize preconditions: the invalid-backup early return left all expanded data in /tmp; exploitation nevertheless requires administrator interaction or equivalent user:manage authorization.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The route is administrator-restricted, but the only resource limit applies to the compressed request body and permits up to 512 MiB.
- The complete request is accepted in memory and the archive is fully extracted before metadata or required database files are validated.
- Every ZIP entry is synchronously decompressed with entry.getData() and written without entry-count, decompressed-size, compression-ratio, or aggregate-output limits.

### [src/server/routes/backup.ts:164](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c2a1745e7fc4593baaa746eb50d7e2a3d0646851/src/server/routes/backup.ts#L164)

~~~~typescript
router.post("/restore", rawBodyParser, async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  const zipBuffer = req.body as Buffer;
  if (!zipBuffer || !Buffer.isBuffer(zipBuffer) || zipBuffer.length === 0) {
    res.status(400).json({ message: "请上传 .zip 备份文件（需以 application/zip Content-Type 发送）" });
    return;
  }

  // 快速校验 ZIP 魔数
  if (zipBuffer[0] !== 0x50 || zipBuffer[1] !== 0x4b) {
    res.status(400).json({ message: "上传的文件不是有效的 ZIP 格式（缺少 PK 文件头）" });
    return;
  }

  const tmpDir = path.join(os.tmpdir(), `projectx-restore-${crypto.randomUUID()}`);
  try {
    await mkdir(tmpDir, { recursive: true });

    // 使用 adm-zip 解压
    extractZipFromBuffer(zipBuffer, tmpDir);

    // 验证 metadata
    const metadataPath = path.join(tmpDir, "metadata.json");
    if (!existsSync(metadataPath)) {
      res.status(400).json({ message: "备份文件格式不正确，缺少 metadata.json" });
      return;
    }

    // 验证必须有 projectx.db
    const projectxBak = path.join(tmpDir, "projectx.db");
    if (!existsSync(projectxBak)) {
      res.status(400).json({ message: "备份文件中未找到 projectx.db" });
      return;
~~~~

### [src/server/routes/backup.ts:294](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c2a1745e7fc4593baaa746eb50d7e2a3d0646851/src/server/routes/backup.ts#L294)

~~~~typescript
function extractZipFromBuffer(zipBuffer: Buffer, destDir: string): void {
  const zip = new AdmZip(zipBuffer);
  const entries = zip.getEntries();
  for (const entry of entries) {
    // 安全检查：防止路径穿越攻击
    const relativePath = path.normalize(entry.entryName).replace(/^[\\/]+/, "");
    const safePath = path.join(destDir, relativePath);
    if (!safePath.startsWith(path.resolve(destDir))) {
      continue;
    }
    if (entry.isDirectory) {
      mkdirSync(safePath, { recursive: true });
    } else {
      mkdirSync(path.dirname(safePath), { recursive: true });
      writeFileSync(safePath, entry.getData());
    }
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The original medium rating reflects a genuine ZIP-bomb condition and potentially high single-service availability impact. Under the required matrix, high impact combined with low likelihood calibrates to low severity. The separate hard policy pass then suppresses the finding to ignore because reaching the vulnerable sink requires an administrator or equivalent user:manage principal to perform a protected restore operation, and the defect does not provide a privilege-escalation delta. Repository evidence shows enforced authentication and authorization at src/server/routes/backup.ts:19-24, administrator-only UI exposure at AccountMenu.tsx:168-192, default loopback binding at index.ts:1366-1380, and no realistic lower-privileged path. Supported proxy deployments could broaden network reach but would not remove the administrator authorization and user-interaction precondition.

路径
Crafted high-expansion ZIP --privileged user is induced to select the archive--> Administrator import UI --browser sends the file unchanged with the user's bearer token--> POST /api/db/restore with authenticated user:manage session --authMiddleware and user:manage authorization succeed--> 512 MiB compressed-body limit and PK-prefix check --compressed-size and magic-byte checks do not constrain expansion--> AdmZip entry.getData() plus writeFileSync extraction --whole entries are synchronously decompressed and written--> Shared Node event loop, process memory, and temporary filesystem --event-loop blocking and memory or disk consumption affect the service--> Project-X availability degradation or outage

Attacker story: a malicious party prepares a highly compressible ZIP and persuades a Project-X administrator to import it as a backup. The normal UI sends the raw archive to POST /api/db/restore with the administrator's token. The route is a production workflow mounted at /api/db, but authMiddleware and requirePermission(user:manage) prevent unauthenticated, student, and default teacher access. Once authorized, only the compressed request is limited to 512 MiB and only the PK prefix is checked. Extraction occurs before backup metadata validation, and every entry is synchronously expanded into a complete Buffer and written synchronously without decompressed-resource limits. Supplied executable validation expanded a 261,030-byte archive to 268,435,456 bytes, delayed a concurrent request by approximately 2.075 seconds, and demonstrated that an early missing-metadata response left the expanded directory on disk. This confirms a real availability defect, while OOM, process termination, and full-disk impact remain extrapolated rather than safely reproduced. The strongest counterevidence is dispositive for reportability: the endpoint is protected by strong administrator authorization, the UI is administrator-only, the service directly binds to loopback, and there is no repository-established lower-privileged path or authorization bypass. The issue is therefore technically valid and in a real product workflow but suppressed under the required privileged-only policy.

发生可能性
Low - The archive format is easy to construct and the vulnerable extraction is deterministic, but normal access requires a valid user:manage session and an explicit privileged restore action. The server binds to localhost by default, no repository ingress artifact establishes public exposure, and no lower-privileged attacker can directly invoke the route under the inspected authorization controls. An external attacker additionally depends on social engineering or prior administrator compromise.
影响程度
High - Successful resource amplification can block the shared Node event loop, consume native Buffer memory and temporary storage, terminate the process, or make the single Project-X service unavailable. Validation directly demonstrated synchronous cross-request blocking and persistent 256 MiB temporary output; process termination and complete disk exhaustion were not attempted. There is no demonstrated confidentiality, integrity, code-execution, or fleet-wide impact.
假设条件
The inspected commit represents the production backup-restore implementation.
The Node process shares its event loop and temporary filesystem with requests from other Project-X users.
No uninspected reverse proxy or host-level resource quota prevents archive expansion or automatically removes abandoned restore directories.
A non-administrator attacker would need to deliver the crafted archive to an administrator or first compromise an account granted user:manage; the repository does not establish such a delivery or compromise path.
A valid authenticated session whose role grants user:manage
A privileged administrator or equivalent permission holder must submit or select the crafted ZIP for database restoration
For an external attacker story, the attacker must persuade that privileged user to import the archive or already control the privileged account
The host must have enough memory or temporary storage for the chosen expansion before resource exhaustion occurs
控制措施
Mandatory token authentication on the complete backup router
Server-side user:manage permission check independent of the optional global API identity parser
Administrator-only import control in the React UI
512 MiB compressed request-body limit
Nonempty Buffer and PK-prefix checks
Path normalization and destination-prefix containment check
Temporary-directory cleanup after successful restore and caught exceptions
Default listen address restricted to 127.0.0.1:5174
No restore-specific rate limit, extraction quota, compression-ratio limit, or worker isolation
盲点
No deployment-specific reverse-proxy, tunnel, load-balancer, container, or host resource-limit manifest was found, so actual remote exposure and filesystem quotas remain unknown.
The supplied validation deliberately did not cause OOM, terminate the process, or fill the filesystem; those maximum impacts remain inferred from unbounded extraction.
Peak heap and native Buffer memory were not instrumented.
The repository cannot establish how likely administrators are to import backups obtained from untrusted third parties.
Custom role-permission assignments may grant user:manage to non-default roles, but the route still requires that privileged permission.
Host-level cleanup jobs, disk quotas, process supervisors, or reverse-proxy request limits outside the checkout could reduce persistence or outage duration.

## 49. Windows application executable signing is disabled

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/fe293302ee748191ba5c34e9f2a16830?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Windows application executable signing is disabled
严重级别
Informational

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
c4cdb02
6:03 PM Jun 14, 2026

操作者：火箭

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

This commit introduced the signing regression by changing the default-enabled electron-builder behavior to `signAndEditExecutable: false`. The icon path change uses a repository-controlled PNG and does not expose attacker input. Adding `--use-system-ca` affects only the operator-run MSI build process and is not independently exploitable under the stated trusted-build-host assumptions.

The new global Windows build option sets `signAndEditExecutable` to `false`. Electron-builder normally uses this stage to edit and Authenticode-sign the packaged application executable when signing credentials are supplied. Consequently, even a release environment configured with signing credentials will produce an unsigned inner application executable for both the portable and MSI packaging flows. This does not necessarily prevent the outer MSI or portable container from being signed independently, but it removes executable-level publisher authentication and prevents signature-enforcement policies from validating the installed or extracted application. An attacker who can modify a shared deployment copy, extracted portable application, or installed executable could substitute code without invalidating an application-executable signature. Exploitation still requires write access to a distribution or application location and subsequent user execution, so this is a low-priority supply-chain/local integrity weakness rather than a remotely reachable application vulnerability. Remove the option or set it to `true`, provide signing credentials in the release environment, and verify signatures on produced artifacts.

验证
 Confirm the commit adds signAndEditExecutable=false in the effective Windows electron-builder configuration.
 Confirm the configured electron-builder version/default semantics make this option disable executable editing/signing even when credentials are present.
 Confirm the option applies to real portable and MSI release flows and is not overridden by scripts/configuration.
 Reproduce the closest feasible production signing behavior through electron-builder's configuration loader and actual WinPackager.signApp implementation; a complete Windows artifact remained unavailable for the documented platform reasons.
 Establish the claimed integrity impact and required attacker/operator preconditions under the supplied threat model.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The global Windows packaging configuration explicitly disables electron-builder's executable signing and editing stage.

### [package.json:75](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c4cdb02cdb22b3abc94edddccf2bcf5cd513cfff/package.json#L75)

~~~~json
    "win": {
      "icon": "resources/icon.png",
      "executableName": "答题卡设计系统",
      "signAndEditExecutable": false,
      "target": [
        {
          "target": "portable",
          "arch": [
            "x64"
          ]
        }
      ],
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The calibrated gross impact is high because a successfully substituted executable can run arbitrary code and access locally stored examination data, but likelihood is low, yielding low severity under the required high-impact/low-likelihood matrix. The separate policy-adjustment pass then applies hard suppression: every supported attack path requires pre-existing privileged or protected-path write access to a build output, deployment copy, extracted portable directory, or installation directory. The disabled signature does not provide that access or a privilege-escalation delta, and there is no evidenced remote or lower-privileged product entry point. The final reportability decision is therefore ignore despite the verified build-control regression.

路径
Project-X Windows release build --signAndEditExecutable=false skips the inner executable signing stage--> Portable EXE or MSI containing an inner executable without Project-X publisher signing --no Project-X inner-executable signature exists to invalidate--> Substituted application executable --replacement is presented for user execution--> Victim user launches application --payload executes with the logged-in user's privileges--> Arbitrary code execution as victim and access to local Project-X data

This is a genuine executable-integrity regression in a documented Windows product workflow. package.json globally sets build.win.signAndEditExecutable=false, and the supplied executable validation against pinned app-builder-lib 26.15.2 demonstrated that WinPackager.signApp returns before editing or signing the application executable even when signing would otherwise occur. The setting applies to both documented portable and MSI build commands. The strongest counterevidence is that the repository contains no signing credentials, forceCodeSigning configuration, release automation, updater, or signed artifact proving that Project-X currently operates a signed release pipeline. In addition, outer MSI or portable containers can still be signed independently, and the MSI is configured per-user. Those points do not disprove the configuration defect: valid external credentials would still be bypassed for the inner executable. They are nevertheless dispositive for reportability because exploitation requires pre-existing write authority over a protected distribution or application path, bypass or expiration of outer-container protection, and subsequent victim execution. The repository provides no remote, student, teacher, or other lower-privileged path to gain that write access. The issue therefore weakens defense-in-depth publisher authentication but does not itself cross a privilege boundary.

发生可能性
Low - There is no network-reachable signing sink, update mechanism, or repository-supported lower-privileged route to replace the executable. An attacker must already control a release share or writable application location, avoid or outlive outer-artifact verification, and induce user execution. No committed signing credentials or signed release pipeline establish that executable signing would otherwise occur. These conditions make exploitation unlikely.
影响程度
High - Successful substitution and execution provides arbitrary code execution with the victim application's user privileges. The packaged application points to a local database and answer-card data under Electron's userData directory, while project documentation states that scan images and scores are stored locally. Confidentiality, integrity, and availability consequences could therefore be high after exploitation, although the signing setting does not itself grant the necessary write access.
假设条件
Windows releases are built through the repository's electron:dist or electron:msi scripts and consume the global build.win configuration.
The pinned electron-builder/app-builder-lib 26.15.2 behavior demonstrated by the supplied validation remains representative of release builds.
A meaningful attack requires a victim to obtain or run an executable from a location the attacker can modify.
Signing credentials may be supplied outside the repository; no credentials or signed artifacts are committed.
A Windows portable or MSI release is produced using the affected electron-builder configuration.
The release environment would otherwise have valid Project-X signing credentials.
The attacker obtains write access to a shared deployment copy, extracted portable directory, or installed application executable.
Any outer-container signature check is bypassed, omitted, or no longer applicable because substitution occurs after extraction or installation.
A victim executes the substituted application.
控制措施
ASAR packaging is enabled for application resources, but it does not authenticate or protect a replaced outer application executable.
The outer MSI or portable container can be signed independently; that can detect pre-extraction tampering when signature verification is enforced.
MSI perMachine=false configures a per-user installation rather than a privileged machine-wide installation.
Electron uses contextIsolation=true, nodeIntegration=false, and sandbox=true, but those renderer controls do not mitigate whole-executable replacement.
No automatic-update mechanism was found in the repository, eliminating an evidenced remote update-delivery path for the replacement.
Filesystem and release-channel ACLs are the primary prerequisite control, but their deployment configuration is outside the repository.
盲点
No complete Windows artifact was produced in the Linux validation environment, so the actual signatures of released PE files were not directly inspected.
The repository contains no signing credentials, release CI configuration, or signed artifacts, so current operational signing practices are unknown.
External GitHub organization controls, release-asset protections, school deployment shares, Windows ACLs, AppLocker or WDAC policy, and endpoint protection are outside the repository.
The repository does not establish how many systems consume a given artifact, so fleet reach remains unknown.
An upstream Electron vendor signature might remain on some input PE files, but it would not authenticate Project-X as the publisher.

概要

Introduced. Grade-Analysis-System-database.html is newly added in this range, and its inline script fails syntax validation because await is used inside a non-async map callback.

In fetchClassStats(), the callback passed to classes.map() is not declared async, but it uses await when calling fetchExams(). This is a JavaScript syntax error, so the browser rejects the entire inline script during parsing. Consequently, initialization and all event-handler functions—including API health checking, login, demo-data loading, and report rendering—are never defined. The failure occurs before any live or demo workflow can run. The file is a standalone root-level page rather than part of the production Vite build, which limits its operational impact.

验证
