 Confirm the error is in the page's single main application script and prevents initialization and event-handler declarations.
 Exercise the real served page through a bounded local interface and apply the smallest parser harness when browser execution is unavailable.
 Establish deployment reachability and verify that the standalone page is excluded from the primary production build.
 Verify introduction at the supplied commit and document counterevidence, preconditions, and the additional parse error affecting patch sufficiency.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- classes.map receives a synchronous callback, but line 313 uses await inside it, causing the complete inline script to fail parsing.

### [Grade-Analysis-System-database.html:310](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/9bd9f593fe99059f588940b675c53a3ee816ffa3/Grade-Analysis-System-database.html#L310)

~~~~text
  const classes = [...new Set(students.map(s => s.className))];
  return classes.map(cls => {
    const clsStudents = students.filter(s => s.className === cls);
    const subjects = (await fetchExams()).find(e => e.id === examId).subjects.map(s => s.name);
    const subjectAvgs = {};
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The underlying correctness defect has low technical impact because it makes only the standalone page unusable. Likelihood is set to ignore after the policy-adjustment pass: no attacker-controlled input reaches the fault, no identity or authorization boundary is crossed, and repository evidence places the file outside the normal production build and static-serving directory. Reaching it requires loopback source-root development hosting or a separate operator deployment plus an explicit URL. Applying the required matrix mechanically, impact=low with likelihood=ignore yields adjusted criticality=ignore.

路径
Explicit request to standalone root-level HTML page --browser loads and parses the page--> Classic inline JavaScript parser --parsing reaches line 313--> Illegal await in non-async map callback --SyntaxError prevents script instantiation--> Whole inline script rejected --health check, login, demo loading, rendering, and initialization are undefined--> Standalone page workflows unavailable

The reported syntax failure is confirmed at Grade-Analysis-System-database.html:313: fetchClassStats is async, but the callback passed to classes.map at line 311 is not, making its await expression illegal. Standards-compliant parsing rejects the entire classic script, so later health-check, login, demo-data, and initialization declarations never become available. The supplied executable parser evidence confirms this behavior. The strongest scoping counterevidence is that Vite can serve repository-root files during development and this page contains API-facing grade-analysis logic. Nevertheless, the repository's normal development command binds Vite to 127.0.0.1, its production build uses index.html and dist/client, and the production Express server serves only dist/client. No attacker input triggers the fault and no security boundary is crossed. The result is a standalone-page availability/correctness bug, not a reportable security vulnerability.

发生可能性
Ignore - There is no realistic lower-privileged attacker action: the fault is unconditional, attacker input is absent, and requesting the page only demonstrates an already-existing failure to the requester. Repository-supported reachability is limited to an explicit path on the loopback development server; production static serving excludes the file. Under the mandatory policy, the absence of an attacker path and the developer/operator deployment precondition make security exploitation non-reportable.
影响程度
Low - If separately served, the defect completely disables one standalone analysis page for every user who opens it. It does not affect the primary production React build and does not disclose or modify credentials, student records, scores, or other sensitive assets. The technical impact is therefore low and limited to availability/functionality of the isolated artifact.
假设条件
The repository's documented Vite and Express commands represent the normal development and production workflows.
No deployment system outside the repository separately publishes Grade-Analysis-System-database.html.
A source-root Vite development server can serve the file when a user explicitly requests its filename, as established by the supplied validation evidence.
An operator must run source-root development/static hosting or separately deploy the standalone HTML file.
A user must explicitly navigate to Grade-Analysis-System-database.html because the normal application entry point is index.html.
The browser must parse the page's classic inline script; no attacker-controlled input is required.
控制措施
The Vite development command explicitly uses --host 127.0.0.1.
The normal production client build emits to dist/client and does not include the standalone HTML file.
The production Express static handler serves dist/client rather than the repository root.
The normal index.html entry loads the React client, not Grade-Analysis-System-database.html.
Browser parsing fails before credentials, bearer tokens, API responses, or grade data can be processed by this page.
盲点
A deployment process outside the repository could copy repository-root HTML files into a public document root.
Static analysis cannot establish whether operators distribute this standalone file manually.
A full browser reproduction was unavailable because the validation environment lacked a required Electron/Chromium system library, although Node parsing and vm.Script independently confirmed the syntax error.
No repository ingress or load-balancer artifact establishes external exposure of the Vite development server.

## 51. Untrusted DLL search can hijack Scanner Bridge

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/1869f3c8b46c81918c59372b8c21d212?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

使用 Codex 修复
Untrusted DLL search can hijack Scanner Bridge
严重级别
Informational

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
c8b993a
6:40 PM Jun 12, 2026

操作者：chuh

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced: the range adds the custom LoadLibraryA-based DSM resolver and its unsafe candidate ordering. The baseline called DSM_Entry directly and did not contain this explicit developer-path and bare-name runtime loading logic.

The commit replaces the previously linked DSM_Entry call with a custom loader. On its first TWAIN operation, it passes several unverified candidates to LoadLibraryA, including a fixed D:\ developer path followed by the bare names TWAINDSM.dll and twain_32.dll. The fixed path is checked before the packaged DLL name, while bare-name loading can include the application directory and other process search locations. LoadLibraryA executes a DLL's initialization code before GetProcAddress checks whether DSM_Entry exists, so merely planting a DLL in an applicable location is sufficient. The build script normally copies TWAINDSM.dll beside scanner-bridge.exe, which limits the bare-name case, but packaging continues with only a warning when that copy is absent and the runtime loader still prefers the hard-coded D:\ path. A lower-privileged local user who can create or replace a DLL at that path, or in an effective search directory on an installation without the bundled DLL, can obtain code execution as the account running Project-X when scanner source enumeration or scanning starts. Internet-only attackers cannot create such a local file, and managed scanner workstations are an explicit assumption, so the project-specific criticality is low. The bridge should instead resolve an operator-configured or packaged DLL to a canonical absolute path, restrict loading with LoadLibraryEx search flags, and preferably validate its publisher or digest.

验证
 Confirm the affected commit introduced the custom resolver, exact candidate order, and LoadLibraryA-before-GetProcAddress behavior.
 Establish the attacker-controlled file, closest controls, vulnerable loading sink, and the filesystem/privilege preconditions.
 Confirm normal scanner source enumeration and scanning reach the loader through real CLI and application interfaces.
 Confirm packaging can continue without the adjacent DSM and that the packaged DLL does not supersede the earlier hard-coded D: candidate.
 Dynamically load a proof DLL through scanner-bridge on Windows; blocked because the Linux environment has no Windows SDK/toolchain, Wine, or built bridge binary.

### 证据注释（原文）

- Introduces the unverified environment, hard-coded developer-path, and bare-name DLL candidates and loads each with LoadLibraryA before checking its export.
- Uses the same developer-specific default DLL path and allows packaging to continue with only a warning when the intended adjacent TWAINDSM.dll cannot be copied, leaving runtime fallback searches possible.

### [native/ScannerBridge/scanner-bridge/twain_controller.cpp:40](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c8b993a799842d8e07207e291e74c388cee05663/native/ScannerBridge/scanner-bridge/twain_controller.cpp#L40)

~~~~cpp
    if (!dsmEntry) {
        char envPath[MAX_PATH] = {};
        DWORD envLen = GetEnvironmentVariableA("TWAIN_DSM_DLL", envPath, static_cast<DWORD>(sizeof(envPath)));
        const char* envCandidate = (envLen > 0 && envLen < sizeof(envPath)) ? envPath : nullptr;
        const char* candidates[] = {
            envCandidate,
            "D:\\twain-dsm-2.5.1\\twain-dsm-2.5.1\\Releases\\dsm_020403\\windows\\64\\TWAINDSM.dll",
            "TWAINDSM.dll",
            "twain_32.dll"
        };

        for (const char* candidate : candidates) {
            if (!candidate || !candidate[0]) continue;
            dsmModule = LoadLibraryA(candidate);
            if (!dsmModule) continue;

            dsmEntry = reinterpret_cast<DsmEntryProc>(GetProcAddress(dsmModule, "DSM_Entry"));
            if (dsmEntry) break;

            FreeLibrary(dsmModule);
            dsmModule = nullptr;
~~~~

### [scripts/build-scanner-bridge.bat:145](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/c8b993a799842d8e07207e291e74c388cee05663/scripts/build-scanner-bridge.bat#L145)

~~~~text
set "TWAIN_DSM_DLL=%TWAIN_DSM_DLL%"
if not defined TWAIN_DSM_DLL set "TWAIN_DSM_DLL=D:\twain-dsm-2.5.1\twain-dsm-2.5.1\Releases\dsm_020403\windows\64\TWAINDSM.dll"

if exist "%OUTPUT%" (
    echo Output: %OUTPUT%
    echo.

    if not exist "%DEST%" mkdir "%DEST%"
    copy /Y "%OUTPUT%" "%DEST%\scanner-bridge.exe" >nul
    if !ERRORLEVEL! EQU 0 (
        echo Copied to: %DEST%\scanner-bridge.exe
        if exist "%TWAIN_DSM_DLL%" (
            copy /Y "%TWAIN_DSM_DLL%" "%DEST%\TWAINDSM.dll" >nul
            if !ERRORLEVEL! EQU 0 (
                echo Copied to: %DEST%\TWAINDSM.dll
            ) else (
                echo [WARNING] Failed to copy TWAINDSM.dll from: %TWAIN_DSM_DLL%
            )
        ) else (
            echo [WARNING] TWAINDSM.dll not found: %TWAIN_DSM_DLL%
        )
~~~~


攻击路径分析

Before policy suppression, conditional native code execution has high impact but low likelihood, which mechanically maps to low severity. The separate final policy pass suppresses the finding to ignore because repository evidence does not establish a realistic lower-privileged in-scope attacker who can write the hard-coded or effective search path while gaining access beyond their existing account. The documented product deployment is a loopback-only Electron scanner application running with the launching user's token, and no service elevation or cross-user ACL condition is shown. The managed-workstation assumption further weakens the local attacker story. The packaged adjacent DSM is not dispositive against the code defect because the hard-coded D: path is attempted first and packaging tolerates a missing DSM, but those facts alone do not establish the privilege-escalation delta required to make this local file-planting issue reportable under the supplied policy.

路径
Lower-privileged local Windows user --requires filesystem write access--> Attacker-controlled DLL at selected candidate path --selected before or through bare-name fallback--> LoadLibraryA candidate loop --LoadLibraryA runs DLL initialization before GetProcAddress--> scanner-bridge.exe process --inherits the launching process account token--> Native code execution with Scanner Bridge account access

This is a genuine conditional DLL-hijacking vulnerability in a production scanner workflow. native/ScannerBridge/scanner-bridge/twain_controller.cpp:40-60 loads an environment-selected path, a fixed D: developer path, TWAINDSM.dll, and twain_32.dll with LoadLibraryA. The fixed D: path is attempted before the packaged DLL name, and the two final candidates use unrestricted bare-name resolution. Because LoadLibraryA initializes the module before GetProcAddress checks for DSM_Entry, a planted DLL can execute even without exporting the expected function. Normal source enumeration reaches the sink through main.cpp:31-41, listSources at twain_controller.cpp:180-184, and openDSM at lines 400-405. The loopback GET /api/scanner/sources route also invokes scanner-bridge with the list argument, while scanning reaches the same loader. The strongest counterevidence is operational: Electron binds the API to 127.0.0.1, the build usually places a legitimate TWAINDSM.dll next to the bridge, managed scanner workstations are assumed, and the repository proves neither attacker-writable ACLs on D: nor an elevated or separate Scanner Bridge account. The adjacent DLL does not neutralize the earlier hard-coded candidate, and a failed DSM copy is only a warning, but no realistic cross-account privilege boundary is established. No executable Windows proof was run because the supplied environment lacks the required Windows runtime and toolchain.

发生可能性
Low - Exploitation is local and conditional. It requires a Windows deployment, write access to a candidate DLL path, no earlier valid DSM candidate, and a later list or scan operation. The API is loopback-only, scanner workstations are assumed managed, and the build usually packages an adjacent DSM. The hard-coded D: candidate is still tried first and packaging can continue without a DSM, but repository artifacts do not establish production ACLs, the existence or writability of D:, or an elevated Scanner Bridge identity. These conditions support low technical likelihood rather than a realistic remote or local-network attack path.
影响程度
High - If a lower-privileged attacker controls the selected DLL path and the bridge runs under a more privileged or data-rich account, DLL initialization provides arbitrary native code execution with that account's access, affecting confidentiality, integrity, and availability of local scanner operations and accessible application data. Impact is therefore high on successful cross-account exploitation, but the repository does not prove that such a privilege difference exists.
假设条件
The affected deployment is Windows and includes scanner-bridge.exe.
A lower-privileged local attacker can create or replace a DLL at the hard-coded D:\twain-dsm-2.5.1\twain-dsm-2.5.1\Releases\dsm_020403\windows\64\TWAINDSM.dll path, or at an effective Windows bare-name DLL search location.
No valid TWAIN_DSM_DLL environment candidate is selected before the attacker-controlled candidate.
A user or local request subsequently invokes scanner source enumeration or scanning.
A meaningful privilege or data-access difference exists between the file-planting attacker and the account running Project-X; the repository does not establish this difference.
Windows scanner workstation
Write access to the hard-coded D: candidate or another effective DLL search directory
Absence of an earlier valid environment-selected DSM
Invocation of scanner source enumeration or scanning
A privilege or data-access difference between attacker and Scanner Bridge account for security impact beyond self-execution
控制措施
The application server binds to 127.0.0.1 rather than a public interface.
Electron starts the local server on an ephemeral port.
The build normally copies TWAINDSM.dll beside scanner-bridge.exe, reducing the practical bare-name TWAINDSM.dll case.
The bridge executable is spawned with an argument array rather than through a command shell.
The scanner bridge runs with the launching account's token; no intentional elevation is evidenced.
Scanner workstations are assumed physically managed by the supplied threat model.
No SetDefaultDllDirectories, AddDllDirectory, SetDllDirectory, restricted LoadLibraryEx flags, signature validation, or digest validation was found for this loader.
The scanner routes themselves do not provide authentication or authorization, although their server is loopback-bound.
盲点
The checkout contains no built scanner-bridge.exe or concrete release package, so actual packaged DLL placement and signatures could not be inspected.
Repository artifacts do not define production Windows ACLs for the D: path, application directory, current directory, or other DLL search locations.
The repository does not establish whether Project-X is ever launched elevated, as a Windows service, or under a dedicated account with access unavailable to local attackers.
No Windows runtime, Wine environment, Windows SDK, or cross-toolchain was available to execute a proof DLL.
The effective Windows DLL search order can vary with OS configuration and process-wide settings outside the repository.
External reverse-proxy or tunnel configuration is not present in the checkout; however, remote triggering alone would not provide the required local DLL write.

<a id="结果-52"></a>

## 52. Data-directory relocation hides existing answer cards

[原始报告](https://chatgpt.com/codex/cloud/security/findings/f65bd4f7e1d481918e982f16f3846238?repo=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2FDalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C%2FProject-X&sev=&page=3)

关闭
使用 Codex 修复
Data-directory relocation hides existing answer cards
严重级别
Informational

调整 以提高未来扫描的准确性

提交
81afe4d
9:49 PM Jun 8, 2026

操作者：chuh

代码仓库
Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X
举报
聊天
概要

Introduced: installations upgraded from the preceding packaged v0.1.0 release stop seeing all previously saved answer cards unless an operator manually moves `<old-data>/cards`, `<old-data>/layouts`, and `<old-data>/assets` into the new `answer-card` directory. Standalone deployments relying on the default data path are affected similarly. A startup migration or legacy-path fallback is needed.

Before this commit, the Electron application stored cards, layouts, and assets under `<userData>/data`, while the standalone server defaulted to `<cwd>/data`. The commit unconditionally changes those locations to an `answer-card` subdirectory. On the first launch after upgrading, `ensureDataDirs()` creates an empty directory tree at the new location, so the application lists no existing cards and cannot resolve their associated assets. There is no migration, copy, rename, or legacy-path fallback. The original files remain recoverable at the old location, so this is a recoverable availability/data-discovery regression rather than destructive data loss.

验证
 Confirm the immediate predecessor and packaged v0.1.0 resolved Electron and standalone storage roots to <userData>/data and <cwd>/data.
 Confirm commit 81afe4de changes both production roots to an answer-card subdirectory.
 Confirm startup initializes the new tree without migration or a legacy-path fallback for cards, layouts, or assets.
 Reproduce through the real storage/server interface that legacy data is hidden after upgrade while remaining present and that empty new directories are created.
 Establish the realistic upgrade/default-path preconditions and classify the impact as recoverable availability/data discovery rather than attacker-triggered destructive loss.
验证输出结果

### 证据注释（原文）

- The Electron startup path now points to `<userData>/data/answer-card` instead of the location used by existing installations, with no migration before passing it to the server.
- The standalone default is also relocated, and initialization simply creates empty cards, assets, and layouts directories at the new location rather than detecting or migrating the legacy tree.

### [electron/main.cjs:12](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/81afe4de2515f6fe3b01ea72f711df6c0a3b94f6/electron/main.cjs#L12)

~~~~text
async function startLocalServer() {
  const appRoot = getAppRoot();
  const serverBundle = path.join(appRoot, "dist", "server", "index.mjs");
  const clientDist = path.join(appRoot, "dist", "client");
  const dataDir = path.join(app.getPath("userData"), "data", "answer-card");

  process.env.ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
~~~~

### [src/apps/answer-card/server/storage.ts:9](https://github.com/Dalian-No-5-Middle-School-I-T-C/Project-X/blob/81afe4de2515f6fe3b01ea72f711df6c0a3b94f6/src/apps/answer-card/server/storage.ts#L9)

~~~~typescript
export const rootDir = process.cwd();
export const dataDir = process.env.ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR
  ? path.resolve(process.env.ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR)
  : path.join(rootDir, "data", "answer-card");
export const cardsDir = path.join(dataDir, "cards");
export const assetsDir = path.join(dataDir, "assets");
export const layoutsDir = path.join(dataDir, "layouts");

export async function ensureDataDirs(): Promise<void> {
  await mkdir(cardsDir, { recursive: true });
  await mkdir(assetsDir, { recursive: true });
  await mkdir(layoutsDir, { recursive: true });
~~~~


攻击路径分析

The unadjusted operational severity remains low because the regression causes recoverable single-service availability and data-discovery loss without deleting files. The final policy pass applies hard suppression because the trigger is exclusively a trusted operator upgrade/startup condition, attacker-controlled input is absent, and no trust or authorization boundary is crossed. Mechanically applying the required matrix to `impact=low` and `likelihood=ignore` yields `adjusted_criticality=ignore`. Loopback binding, possible external forwarding, and unauthenticated card routes do not alter that result because none provides control over the startup data path.

路径
Trusted operator upgrades or restarts an installation containing legacy data --normal product startup after upgrade--> Electron or standalone startup selects the new `answer-card` data root --initialization creates missing directories--> `ensureDataDirs()` creates an empty directory tree at the new root --storage exports point exclusively at the new root--> Card and asset consumers query only the new tree --list/read/static requests do not inspect the legacy root--> Legacy answer cards and assets are hidden but remain recoverable

Commit 81afe4de changes the packaged Electron root from `<userData>/data` to `<userData>/data/answer-card` and the standalone default from `<cwd>/data` to `<cwd>/data/answer-card`. Startup creates the new directories, and card enumeration, individual lookup, and static asset serving subsequently inspect only that new tree. The supplied executable reproduction confirmed empty card results and 404 responses after startup while hashes showed that all legacy files remained intact; explicitly selecting the old root restored HTTP 200 responses. This is a real product correctness regression, but not a realistic attacker story. The required trigger is a trusted upgrade or startup action, and neither network requests nor lower-privileged identities control the storage-root decision. Strongest counterevidence against impact is that `ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR` can select the old root, legacy files are preserved, and layouts can be regenerated from migrated cards. Those controls make recovery practical but do not prevent the default-path regression. Strongest counterevidence against the localhost exposure classification is the threat-model possibility of a reverse proxy or tunnel; even if externally forwarded, however, remote callers cannot trigger this specific path relocation or gain a security advantage from it.

发生可能性
Ignore - The operational regression is plausibly encountered during a normal upgrade with legacy data, but no realistic in-scope attacker can trigger or exploit it. The storage-root choice is made by trusted startup code and operator-controlled environment state, not by a network request or lower-privileged user. Under the required policy, an operator-only path with no privilege-escalation delta has likelihood `ignore` for security reportability.
影响程度
Low - All cards and associated assets stored under the previous default can disappear from the application's view after an upgrade, affecting availability of the answer-card designer workflow. The effect is limited to the affected installation and storage service, the source files remain intact, an explicit environment override restores access, and cards/assets can be moved manually. No confidentiality, integrity, identity, or privilege impact is demonstrated.
假设条件
The affected installation previously stored answer-card data under the old default `<userData>/data` or `<cwd>/data` root.
The upgraded Electron application uses the same OS user-data location, or the standalone server starts from the same working directory.
The operator has not explicitly set `ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR` to the legacy root and has not manually migrated the files.
No deployment-specific wrapper outside the repository performs a migration before application startup.
A trusted operator installs or starts the version containing commit 81afe4de2515f6fe3b01ea72f711df6c0a3b94f6.
Legacy cards or assets already exist under the former default data root.
The application retains the same Electron user-data location or standalone working directory.
No explicit legacy `ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR` override, migration, or manual relocation is applied.
控制措施
The standalone service accepts an operator-controlled `ANSWER_CARD_DATA_DIR` override that can point to the legacy root.
The server binds directly to `127.0.0.1`; Electron also uses a dynamic loopback port.
Initialization uses recursive directory creation and does not delete, overwrite, or rename the legacy files.
Card identifiers and asset basenames are normalized, but those checks do not address backward-compatible root selection.
No migration, legacy-path fallback, or startup compatibility warning is present.
The relevant card API routes do not implement authentication, but remote request input does not influence this storage-root regression.
盲点
The packaged Electron GUI and MSI upgrade process were not dynamically exercised in the headless environment, although the packaged entry point explicitly sets the changed path before starting the same server.
Deployment-specific wrappers or external reverse proxies are not established by the inspected artifacts; external forwarding would change API exposure but not the non-attacker-controlled relocation trigger.
Filesystem ACLs and installation-specific user-data path behavior are not represented in the repository.
The supplied executable reproduction exercised the standalone HTTP interface rather than an actual package-manager upgrade.
